# @graview/skills

## 0.1.63

### Patch Changes

- c91af18: A record's page draws a blank fact an act the seat may run writes as an empty slot, Roadmap request:record-pages-draw-writable-but-blank-facts-as-empty-editable-slots. A placement made without its allocation or its role had nowhere on its own page to be given them: a blank fact was not drawn, and the act that writes it (`edit-placement`) surfaces only as the dashed editor on a fact, never as a button — so a new record's empty field could be set only from a form somewhere else. Now each field with no value that an act the seat may run writes — taking the value and asking nothing else — stands where the kind's page orders it (`display.page`, `set-page-fields`) as its label and a dim "add", a button named "Add Allocation" that the keyboard reaches; pressing it opens the same in-place editor a value opens, a choice opening on no choice, and saving fills the fact. A blank no act writes (a `fixed` field) stays unsaid, and a seat the policy keeps from the act — a reader — is offered no slot; a file field is still filled where its files are put (FR-163), and another app's address or a record in one is still picked where it is drawn. Underneath, `readableFields` takes `blanks` — the fields to say as empty slots, never on a glance — and a `ReadableField` read that way carries `blank: true`; `recordFacts` passes `blanks` through, the derived record page asks `blankWritable(store, id, principal)` for them where values are changed in place, `RecordField` draws the slot, and `EditableValue` takes `blank`. Pages' `a-blank-fact-an-act-writes-is-an-empty-slot.test.tsx` and core's `a-blank-a-page-may-fill-is-said-where-it-stands.test.ts` hold it, and a new harness, `verify-blank-facts`, opens a staffing firm's placement in three engines and finds the slot by Tab, fills it by Enter, and finds no slot for the fixed field or for a reader (aBlankFieldAnActWritesIsAnEmptySlot, aBlankFieldNoActWritesStaysHidden, aReaderSeesNoEmptySlot). And a filled value is drawn as a control only where an act the seat may run writes it, Roadmap request:in-place-editors-ignore-grants: the in-place editors offered every act that wrote a field whatever the seat's grants, so a reader saw dashed values the store would refuse; `editableFields` now takes `{ principal }` and offers only the acts `store.permittedMutations` gives it, `useEditableFields` passes the seat's, and a reader's values are read-only on every face — as are everyone's under a provider handed no seat over a policy, which the store would refuse anyway (the same check the blank slots make; aReaderSeesNoEditableValue in `verify-blank-facts`). The graview-pages skill says it in one sentence.
  
  A hosted page's first load stays within its budget. The pages face alone carries 1_329 / 463 more, a visitor's form alone 225 / 83, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 225 / 88, every face 529 gzipped and the embed with the studio handed in 520 gzipped, each under a raise of its own (`scripts/budget-raises/a-blank-fact-an-act-writes-is-an-empty-slot-*.json`); Cloud's worker, which had no room, 600 bytes.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The look: a record's page draws a blank writable fact as `[data-graview-blank-fact="<field>"]` with a `button[data-graview-blank]` named "Add <label>". What Graview Cloud does: nothing; a reader's values stop looking editable, a placement's allocation is set from its own page, and a host that drew its own "add" for a blank field can take it away.
- c85bcc0: A Notion connection makes the properties it needs, and one database holds several kinds, Roadmap request:syncing-with-notion-needs-to-allow-for-graview-to-autocreate-the-necessary-properties (Nick, from the site). A Notion connection kept one data source as one kind, and a property it mapped that the data source lacked was only said ("no property in the database for Status, so it is not kept in step") — a person set up every column by hand before anything synced, and a database whose data sources were a project's tasks and its rooms took one connection per source, each with its own credentials and passes. Now a connection may name `sources: [{ "source", "kind", "properties"? }]` in place of its own `source`, `kind` and `properties`: one engine of several resources (`pages:<kind>`), each data source read with a cursor of its own and each page written to the source its kind is kept in; a relation between two of them is a Notion relation property, kept as an edge as one between two connections is. And on its first pass, and whenever one goes missing, a connection that writes gives a data source each property a mapped field lacks (PATCH `/data_sources/{id}`), of the type the field is held in — words a text, a choice a select with its options as people read them ("In progress"), several choices a multi-select, a date, a checkbox, a number, a URL, an email, a phone, and a relation one to the source keeping the kind it reaches — named as the declaration names it, or, matched by name, as the field reads ("Due date"); the name field is the title Notion always has. It never renames or deletes a property somebody made, says one of another type and leaves it, makes no status and no people property, and says what it made in the report and the pass's line: "Made 3 properties in Notion: Status, Due and Done" (`SyncReport.said`, new — what the transport did there or found, as sentences, the first time). `create: false` turns it off; a connection that only reads makes none. A record's page says the source that keeps it, "Kept in step with Notion · Tasks", with Open in Notion to its own page where it came from there and to the database otherwise (`keptInStepWith` on `@graview/core/words`; a calendar's says Google Calendar). `graview check` refuses a source of a kind the document lacks (`connection-kind` at `connections.<name>.sources.<n>.kind`), a property mapped to a field the kind lacks (`connection-field` at the source's `properties.<field>`), and, as the new error `connection-sources`, two sources for one kind, one data source named twice, or `sources` beside the connection's own `kind`, `source` or `properties`; a connection with neither is `connection-kind`. `scriptedNotion()` changes a data source's properties as Notion does, and says a source's properties (`properties(id)`). Core's `notion-makes-the-properties-it-needs-and-keeps-several-sources.test.ts`, pages' `a-record-says-the-notion-source-it-is-kept-in-step-with.test.tsx` and ship's `declared-connections-run-beside-the-store.test.ts` hold it against the scripted Notion; the graview-sync skill says the two forms and what is made. A hosted page carries 0.2 KB more up front (200 bytes minified, 66 gzipped) — the compile reading each source's fields — under a raise naming this changeset; a page handed a compiled app carries nothing more; the pages face alone 1_179 / 464 more (the record page's line), every face 3_295 / 1_192 and the embed with the studio handed in 3_295 / 1_189, and Cloud's worker 11_148 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_078_654 B (1054 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — a connection of one source keeps its links under `pages` and its cursor as before; one of `sources` keeps each source's links under `pages:<kind>` and its cursor as `{ "by": { "<resource>": … } }`, which a host keeps without reading. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — a Notion connection gains `sources` and `create`, its `kind` is optional when `sources` names each kind, and the checker gains the error `connection-sources` and says `connection-kind` and `connection-field` under `sources.<n>`; a declaration that passed before still passes, and a connection that writes now adds to its data source the properties it mapped and did not find — `create: false` keeps a data source exactly as it is. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: its connections form offers a Notion connection's data sources as a list — each a data source of the database picked by name and the kind it keeps, with its field-to-property mapping — writing `sources` when there are several, and a "Make the properties it needs" switch (on by default, `create: false` off); it shows `SyncReport.said` in the connection's activity, and draws nothing of its own for the record's line, which the pages face draws.
- 511184d: A person proposes changes and a reviewer accepts them, Roadmap f-proposals-for-review. Only the app's own actor could leave a change waiting for somebody: a helper who could see a task and was not trusted to close it met a struck-through act and a sentence, and an agent acting for them could do nothing at all.
  
  A grant may now say what its roles may propose and not run: `{ roles: ["helper"], mutations: [], propose: ["close-task"] }` (acts by name or `"*"`, on the grant's `kinds`; a grant that only proposes says `mutations: []`). Reviewing takes no grant of its own: whoever may run the calls decides. `proposes(policy, principal, act, kind)` and `store.mayPropose(call, principal)` say whether a seat may propose a call it may not run; `proposedMutations` is on `@graview/core/tidying`.
  
  `propose(store, principal, calls, intent)` on `@graview/core/tidying` keeps the calls as the same suggestion op the app's own actor makes, with the person as its author and a lineage naming them (`proposedBy`), its basis their words, else "Proposed by Ada". It travels as a call of the store's own `propose-changes` act (`PROPOSE_CHANGES`, `{ calls, intent? }`), which every store answers to and no list offers, and the store makes its op itself: refused, in a sentence, where the seat may neither run nor propose one of the acts, and each call rehearsed as the store would judge it for the reviewer — the act's own guard, the rules, what the seat may see. The same change already waiting is the one that waits. Whoever may run the calls accepts it (`acceptSuggestion`), made as themselves, the lineage naming the proposal and its proposer (`suggestion`, `proposedBy`); a decline answers it in words ("Declined Ada's proposal: …"); its proposer withdraws it (`withdrawSuggestion`). Accepted, it lands in the working state, and reaches readers on the next publish where the app publishes. `isProposal(suggestion)` tells a person's proposal from the app's own suggestion.
  
  An act's form whose seat may propose it and not run it reads "Propose" and keeps the change as the seat's proposal ("Proposed: it waits in Suggested for somebody who may make it."); a record's page offers such an act beside the rest rather than striking it through. The Suggested tray lists a person's proposals apart and first, under "Proposed", each "Proposed by Ada:", with Withdraw in place of a vote for its proposer. An agent acting for somebody who may propose is offered `propose_changes` (`{ calls: { act, args }[], intent? }`), and `list_suggestions` says `proposedBy`. The studio keeps a grant's `propose`. The permissions and tidying skills say how.
  
  The store's own "Propose changes", the proposal it makes, a grant's `propose` and the form's "Propose" cost a hosted page 2.7 KB more up front (2_813 bytes minified, 834 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 2.4 KB (2_492 / 737), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 4_120 / 1_191 more, a visitor's form alone 3_909 / 1_127, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 2_968 / 940, every face 7_378 / 2_234, the embed with the studio handed in 6_430 / 1_993 and Cloud's worker 10_754 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_096_224 B (1071 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — a suggestion's op may now have a person as its author, made by the built-in `propose-changes` act, its lineage naming them as `proposedBy` (1–120 characters, which `lineageOf` holds it to); no primitive is new, and accepting, declining and withdrawing are the ops they were. Stored formats — the one lineage key, and nothing moves: a build before this one reads a proposal as any suggestion and keeps `proposedBy` as a key it does not know. The wire — unchanged in shape: a proposal travels as a `call` of `propose-changes`, which a server that predates it refuses as an act it does not have. The declaration and check finding codes — a grant may say `propose`, `grant-act` reads it too (at `policy.grants.<n>.propose`), `add-grant` and `remove-grant` take it, and a grant may name no act to run where it names acts to propose; nothing that compiled before is refused. Derived tool names and input schemas — `propose_changes` is new, listed only for a seat whose policy lets it propose an act it may not run, so a surface whose policy names no `propose` keeps its tools and its hash; `list_suggestions` carries `proposedBy` on a proposal. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build, lets an owner say who may propose in its permissions editor, and draws its review queue from `suggestions()` with `isProposal`, accepting as the reviewer and declining or withdrawing with the functions above.
- 52aed08: A shared or signed-out seat cannot vote on a suggestion, Roadmap f-votes-shared-seat (from Cloud's asks after 0.1.62: the shared anonymous seat's votes, advisory votes, the tray's "Sign in to vote" and the away line's tally). A host that serves every signed-out visitor as one principal — Cloud's `anonymous` — got one shared vote on 0.1.62: a stranger's `POST /graview/ops` was kept as that seat's vote, the next stranger flipped it, and the tray showed one stranger another's. Now a principal says it is nobody in particular with `shared: true` (`Principal.shared`), and the store refuses a vote on a suggestion from such a seat, from a `visitor`, or from an agent acting for either, in the words "Sign in to vote on this suggestion." — through `permits`, so Accept and Decline, `voteSuggestion`, a raw call, the wire, a socket and an RPC all meet it, in an app with a policy or without one. A host that wants strangers' sentiment says `votes: { advisory: "visitors" }` on the store (`StoreOptions.votes`, the handler's and `serve`'s `votes`, `openRemote`'s `storeOptions`) and names each visitor behind the shared seat with `visitorKey`: such a vote is kept with `advisory: true` and `voter` (that key) on its op, one standing vote per visitor, counted apart in `tally.advisory: { for, against }`, never toward `autoAcceptAt`, a visitor's own in `tally.mine`, and only that visitor's to undo — a shared seat naming no visitor is still refused. `signedOutSeat`, `advisoryVoterOf` and `SIGN_IN_TO_VOTE` are on `@graview/core/tidying`, `VotesOptions` on the main entry; `votesIn(ops, { advisory: true })` reads the advisory votes alone. The Suggested tray asks the store before a press: a seat whose vote would be refused sees the host's `signIn` as one native button, "Sign in to vote", where Accept and Decline were, and no vote at all where the host gave no way in; whoever may decide reads visitors apart, "1 for, 0 against · 2 visitors agree". `AwaySummary` takes `agreed`, and `whileAway` counts it — the most people whose vote stands for one waiting suggestion — said "3 readers agree with one that waits". `a-shared-or-signed-out-seat-votes-only-where-the-host-keeps-its-vote-advisory.test.ts` (core), `a-strangers-vote-over-the-wire-is-refused-unless-the-host-keeps-it-advisory.test.ts` (ship), `a-visitor-is-offered-sign-in-rather-than-a-vote.test.tsx` (primitives) and `while-away-says-how-many-agree-with-one-that-waits.test.ts` (tools) hold it, and the chrome harness opens the tray on both faces as the host's shared signed-out seat and finds Sign in to vote in place of a vote, pressing it asking the host and keeping nothing (aVisitorIsOfferedSignInRatherThanAVote). The graview-tidying skill says it.
  
  A hosted page carries 0.7 KB more up front (717 bytes minified, 220 gzipped; handed a compiled app, 0.7 KB: 711 and 236): who is anybody in particular and an advisory vote's place in the tally — the raises in `scripts/budget-raises/a-shared-seat-cannot-vote-*.json` say each entry's bytes; the pages face alone 717 / 215 more, a visitor's form alone 717 / 225, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 723 / 240, every face 1_796 / 656, the embed with the studio handed in 1_033 / 356 and Cloud's worker 2_166 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_078_694 B (1054 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — a vote op from a shared or signed-out seat (`shared: true`, a `visitor`, or an agent acting for one) is now refused where it was kept, unless the host keeps it advisory; an advisory vote's op carries two new optional keys on `vote`, `advisory: true` and `voter`, which a build before it keeps and reads as a vote of the shared seat's; no primitive is added. Stored formats — nothing moves; a log of 0.1.62's shared-seat votes folds and counts as before. The wire — unchanged in shape; such a vote is refused as any act is, with its sentence. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged; `votes` is the host's, on the store, not the document's. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; `list_suggestions`' `tally` may carry `advisory`. What Graview Cloud does: mark its signed-out principal `shared: true` in `seatOf`, and drop its own refusal or keep it; to gather strangers' sentiment, hand `votes: { advisory: "visitors" }` to the store and the remote's `storeOptions` and a per-visitor `visitorKey` (its visitor cookie) on the seat, and count `tally.advisory` in its tidying notice apart; hand the page `signIn` for a signed-out reader so the tray says Sign in to vote; pass `agreed` from `whileAway` in `AwaySummary`.
- 013977e: An app declares what it does on its own, and when: workflows (Roadmap f-jobs-declared, f-job-step-types, f-job-idempotency, f-job-pause-dry-run). The app's own actor ran only as dreaming, on a host's schedule, and anything else an app did by itself — fill out next week's events every Sunday, keep events in step with plan items — was a script outside the declaration that nobody could check, pause or read. A document now says it at the top, as `workflows`, by name (`defineApp({ workflows })` the same): a `title` a person reads; `when`, one or several of a clock in words (`{ "every": "sunday 18:00" }`, "day 03:00", "weekday 09:00", "month 1 09:00", "hour") or five-field `cron`, both on the brand's time zone, a record `made` or `changed` (a rule-language `where`, the fields that matter), a connection `synced`, or `byHand`; `status` active or paused; `approve`, what its changes become — "apply", "suggest" (made by how sure it is, as dreaming's bands route them) or "ask" — "ask" unsaid when a step writes to another system through a connection, else "apply"; and its `steps`, each one of a closed set told apart by its one key (`STEP_KINDS`): run an act once or over the records a rule picks (`{ "act", "over", "args", "key" }`), or let a model propose acts from a list over the kinds it reads, under a budget (`{ "agent", "reads", "acts", "model", "budget" }`). The word is `workflows`; the purpose's jobs are the questions an app answers and keep their name.
  
  `@graview/core/workflows`, an entry on no page, reads them: `workflowsOf(app)` resolves each with its defaults, its words, the kinds it reads and writes, and lists dreaming as the built-in workflow every app has (`dreaming`, `builtIn: true`, every night at three) unless every kind says the actor never tidies it; `workflowWords` says one ("Every Sunday at 6 PM, and when a grocery list is made: fill out next week's events"); `nextRunAt(workflow, after, zone)` says when it next runs on the wall clock, DST and all — 6 PM on the Sunday the clocks spring forward, a time the clock skips run as it jumps past; and `triggeredBy(workflow, commit, store)` says which `made`, `changed` and `synced` triggers a commit or a pass fires, read by the subscriptions' own matcher as the workflow's author sees the app, so a workflow never starts itself. `graview check` holds each to the declaration in the person's words, each finding with the edit that fixes it where one can be written: `workflow-steps-empty`, `workflow-schedule-invalid` (naming what it reads), `workflow-connection-unknown`, `workflow-kind-unknown`, `workflow-field-unknown`, `workflow-where-invalid`, `workflow-act-unknown` (naming the near act), `workflow-key-field-unknown`, `workflow-agent-acts-empty`, `workflow-follows-unknown` and `workflow-shape`, errors; `workflow-sends-out-unasked`, a warning — a step that writes to another system with `approve: "apply"` said is allowed, and said. The edit vocabulary gains `add-workflow`, `set-workflow`, `remove-workflow`, `pause-workflow` and `resume-workflow`, the diff says each, `describeApp` lists every workflow with its steps one to a line, the studio carries them, and Activity says a workflow's run by its title ("Fill out next week's events: 6 things today").
  
  `@graview/tools/workflows` runs one: `runWorkflow({ app, store, workflow, now?, trigger?, dryRun?, log, ai?, principal? })` answers a `WorkflowRun` — its outcome (`ok`, `nothing-new`, `partial`, `held`, `failed`), what it made, changed, set aside and suggested, what it held back and why, how far it read the log (`through`) and the hash of the workflow as it ran. It runs as an automated system author, `workflow:<name>` named by its title, every op under one `lineage.run`, so Activity folds a run into one row; the runs are the host's to keep (`WorkflowLog`, `memoryWorkflowLog()`), never written into the graph. The first run after a workflow is added or changed is a dry run — "First run after a change: nothing was kept" — and a run by hand of a paused one too; a paused workflow's clock or trigger starts nothing and says so. A keyed act step makes each record under an id worked out from the workflow and the key (`keyedId`, `<prefix>:wf.<workflow>.<key>`), so a rerun over the same input is `nothing-new`, a changed source changes what it made through the kind's edit, a record a person removed is not made again, and a retry after a step failed half way makes no second record. A model's step is shown the records it reads within its token budget and proposes only the acts it is given; with no model lent it is skipped, and the run is `partial`, never failed.
  
  A hosted page carries none of it: a compiled app's workflows are read off the document it remembers (`declaredWorkflows`), never copied onto what a page draws, and only Activity's words for a workflow's run are up front, within the page's budget. The studio's checked compile judging them and its five edits add 21_592 / 7_038 bytes to every face and 21_592 / 7_052 to the embed with the studio handed in, and `graview check` and `describeApp` judging and saying them 37_912 bytes to Cloud's worker (from 2_382_984 to 2_420_897), each under a raise of its own; when a workflow next runs and running one are on entries the worker does not import.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged; a run's ops are ordinary ops by an automated `system` author, their lineage carrying `run` and `key` (a key a lineage already kept as JSON). Stored formats — unchanged; the declaration document gains the top-level `workflows`, optional within `graview-document@1`, which a build before refuses by path. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — the document gains `workflows` and the edit vocabulary its five ops; the `workflow-*` codes above are new, and no code that existed changes meaning. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: keeps a `WorkflowLog` per app, runs each active workflow at `nextRunAt` in the app's time zone and on what `triggeredBy` says a commit or a connection's pass fires, with `runWorkflow` and the app's model lent as `ai` where the plan allows it, and offers Run now (a dry run while paused, or the first time after a change) and Pause, Resume through `pause-workflow` and `resume-workflow`.
- 57575e1: The 0.1.63 batch holds together: the sync skill, which two branches each added to, says the same in fewer words so it keeps to its length, and a hosted page's scene face, measured with every branch merged, keeps to 1056 KB before it draws.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged.
- affa912: The right-click menu says what is under the cursor, Roadmap request:add-copy-link-under-open-in-a-new-tab and request:right-click-context-menu-options-to-be-more-relevant-to-the-item-under-my-cursor. On the routed face a record, a list or a place offered "Open in a new tab" and nothing to paste elsewhere; now "Copy link" stands directly under it, puts the whole address on the clipboard and says "Link copied" quietly at the foot of the window. "Inspect" stands at the foot of every menu, after a rule: a page cannot open the browser's developer tools, so it opens a small sheet of what Graview knows of the thing under the cursor — its kind in words and by its key, the record's id, the field, the place and the picture that drew it (a status board, a view, a lens, by its name), the column it stands in and the link to its page, and, for whoever may change the app (a host's `keptViews` that says so, or an app with no policy), how its kind is declared; keys and ids are written as code. The sheet is fetched the first time somebody asks. Text a person selected is no longer swallowed: a right-click on the selected text keeps the browser's own menu, as before, and a right-click elsewhere while it stays selected opens the menu with "Copy" first, which copies the selection; a finger resting on text no longer opens the menu over it — the phone's own selection handles win — and opens it only on a card, a row, a link, a value, a column or another handle. Safari, and every WebKit on a Mac, selects the word under a right-click before the menu event, so the menu used to find "text somebody selected" under every right-click on words and leave the browser's own; a selection the right-click itself made now counts for nothing — it is put back and the menu opens, while text selected before the press still keeps the browser's menu. And a column under the cursor answers: a status board's column now says `data-column="<field>:<value>"` (with `data-column-kind` and `data-column-label`), a worker view may mark any region the same way, and a right-click on its ground offers "Add a task in Doing" — the kind's add act begun with the column's value given, its form asking only what is left (`PointerMenu.begin`, which the Inspector's menu opens on) — and "Show only Doing", the place or list narrowed by its address (`?filter=status:doing`, as `narrowedPath` writes it). A menu target says the column as `MenuTarget.column` (`MenuColumn`: kind, field, value, label), so a host's section can read it too. The graview-view-links skill says the attribute. `the-menu-says-what-is-under-the-cursor.test.tsx` holds it, and the menu harness proves it at a desk and a phone's width: theMenuOffersCopyLinkUnderOpenInANewTab, inspectSaysWhatIsUnderTheCursor, aSelectionIsCopiedNotSwallowed, and aColumnOffersAddInItAndShowOnlyIt on the tasks' status board and on a worker view that marks its own columns; its long-press claim now rests on a value rather than on a heading's text.
  
  The rows, the column's reading and the sheet's door cost the pages face alone 4.8 KB more (4_939 bytes minified, 2_070 gzipped), every face 10.1 KB (10_307 / 4_261) and the embed with the studio handed in 4.9 KB (5_001 / 1_974), each under a raise naming this change in `scripts/budget-raises/the-menu-says-what-is-under-the-cursor-*.json`; a hosted page stays within its budget.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. `@graview/react` gains the optional `MenuTarget.column`, `PointerMenu.column` and `PointerMenu.begin` and the type `MenuColumn`, all additive. What Graview Cloud does: nothing is required; a view that draws columns marks each `data-column="<field>:<value>"` to have the menu offer adding in it and showing only it, and a host that keeps the declaration says `may.lenses` or `may.views` through `keptViews` for Inspect to show the declaration.
- d6edd89: The starter reads a template as a person would and composes from its whole reading, and the app's own tidying has an edit, Roadmap request:starting-document-drops-rules-and-views, request:starter-readme-excerpt-without-markdown, request:read-starter-keeps-fit-precision, request:still-unmet-takes-traits and request:edit-op-for-app-tidying. Five things Graview Cloud's starter and chats had been doing for themselves.
  
  A kinda turn asks about a template's kinds, rules and views alike, and `readStarter` reads back the ones to drop as `StarterPart`s, but `startingDocument`'s `drop` took only kinds and traits by name, so Cloud removed the unticked rules and views itself. `drop` now takes a `DropPart` (`{ part: "kind" | "rule" | "lens" | "trait", name }`, on `@graview/core/compose`) beside a bare name, so a `KindaReading`'s `drop` goes in as it comes: a rule by its name, a view by its title, each said in `left`, kept out of `took` and one of the `edits` that make the same document from the base (`remove-rule`, `remove-lens`); a rule or view the base lacks is said and changes nothing. `composeReshape` takes the same. `a-starting-document-drops-the-rules-and-views-a-person-unticked.test.ts` holds it.
  
  A template's README went to the model as its first 280 characters raw — `# Title`, badges and markdown. `about` now starts at the first plain sentence, with the markdown taken out (a link said as its words, emphasis and code as their text, a list item as its line, a table, an image and a code block left out) and is cut at a word (`readmeExcerpt` on `@graview/tools/starter`). A fit's `level` was the score rounded to two decimals and then scaled, which put levels on 0.04 steps (3.1 read as 3.12); it is now the expected level itself, rounded once (`expectedLevelOf` beside `scoreOf`), and ranking reads it first. `stillUnmet` takes the traits themselves (`readonly TraitName[]`) as well as a reading. `the-starter-reads-a-template-as-a-person-would.test.ts` holds each.
  
  `set-tidying` covers a kind or a relation, so a chat set `tidying.autoAcceptAt` at the top of a document with a JSON Patch. The edit vocabulary gains `set-app-tidying` (`{ autoAcceptAt: n | null }`, 1 to 10,000; null takes it away and leaves no empty `tidying`), said in a sentence ("The app makes a suggestion on its own once 4 people vote for it.") that `diffDocuments` says of the same change. `the-app-says-when-a-suggestion-is-made-by-an-edit.test.ts` holds it, and the tidying skill names the edit.
  
  The new edit and its sentences cost Cloud's worker 2.0 KB (2_018 bytes), every face 0.2 KB gzipped (250 bytes, 177 measured) and the embed with the studio handed in 0.3 KB gzipped (300 bytes, 219 measured), each under a raise in `scripts/budget-raises/the-starter-and-app-tidying-*.json`; a hosted page's first load is unmoved.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — no shape or code changes; the edit vocabulary gains `set-app-tidying`, which a build before this one refuses as an edit it does not know. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; the starter's state says a template's `about` as plain prose and a ranked template's `level` to the hundredth of a level. What Graview Cloud does: hands a kinda reading's `drop` to `startingDocument` and stops removing unticked rules and views itself; drops its own README cleanup; and has a chat set `autoAcceptAt` with `set-app-tidying` rather than a JSON Patch.
- 45a60d6: What LifeLogics Estimates found, Roadmap f-lifelogics-findings: five requests from one Cursor session on 2026-10-28 (bug-a-timeline-lens-is-accepted-but-never-becomes-a-place, bug-money-shows-00-cents-on-some-amounts-and-none-on-others, bug-option-ids-are-made-into-labels-badly-and-describe-lines-and-badges-show-raw-values, more-than-20-computed-fields-per-kind-and-glances-that-can-show-computed-fields, bug-link-changes-made-through-act-batch-are-missing-from-history).
  
  A timeline is accepted only where it draws. `add-lens` with lens `timeline` over an initiative's start and end dates applied cleanly, `get_problems` said nothing, and the lens was never a place: a timeline lays out spans by number — minutes of a day, 0 to 1440 — across columns, and dates with no column gave it neither. `add-lens` now refuses a timeline whose `start` or `end` is not a number field, or that has no columns, as `lens-cannot-draw` at `edits.<i>.bindings.<kind>.<role>` or `edits.<i>.options.columns`, and its fix names the bindings a timeline takes and the calendar that draws spans of dates (`"lens": "calendar", "bindings": { "initiative": { "start": "start", "end": "end" } }`); the checker says the same of a declaration written by hand, a warning at the binding's path. A timeline over numbers with columns is accepted and draws as before.
  
  Money never reads ".00". A list read "$11,424.00" beside "$7,840" and "$22,758.40": a sum worked out as 11423.999… was not whole, so it took two places that rounded to ".00". A sum is now rounded to the cent before it is judged whole, so it shows cents only where it has them. A money field — or a computed field said as money — may say `"places": 0`, and a template `{total | money: 0}`: the sum in whole units, rounded ("$22,758"); `set-format` sets it (`"places": 0`) and clears it (`null`), and the document refuses it on a field that is not money. A field's describe line and label say it so too.
  
  An option is said by its label, and keeps its author's capitals. A kind's `describe` line, its label, a rule's sentence and a card's badge said a choice named bare by its id ("done by Dec 28"), where every other face said its label; now each says the option's label, and where none is given, its id spoken — as a chip is when the field is the whole template, in lower case among other words ("Rainy day (idea)", "supply chain", never "supply-chain"). An option with no label written with capitals keeps them: "LifeLogics", "OpenSet" and "Open Set" stay as written where they read "Life logics" and "Open set" (`humanizeValue` on `@graview/core`); one in lower case or camelCase is spoken as a key is. A field's key is spoken as it was.
  
  A kind works out 60 computed fields, where it could 20 (`MOST_COMPUTED` on `@graview/core/document`). The cap bounds what one record costs to read: each value is worked out for every record a card, a row or a page draws, from a budget of steps of its own. `set-glance` takes a computed field, as the document and every card already did, but not one kept `hidden`; and `toDocument` keeps a computed field in a TypeScript kind's glance, where it dropped it.
  
  A batch of links is in history, said by each link. The framework's history did have one `act_batch` that linked twelve records — one gesture, and each record it linked is in its ops — but it said the batch by its first link alone, so twelve links read as one. A gesture of several acts its caller named nothing for is now said by each act's sentence, "Link A to B; Link C to D", and past three, the first two "and N more" (`Batch.intent`, `ticksOf`, `historyInWords`, Activity and Undo's words).
  
  A hosted page carries 2.3 KB more up front (2_381 bytes minified, 789 gzipped), and handed a compiled app 1.7 KB more (1_786 / 587), under raises naming this changeset: the timeline's bindings in words, a sum's rounding and places, each kind's option labels for a template. The pages face alone 2_090 / 673 more, a visitor's form 1_317 / 451 and handed a compiled app 1_845 / 666, every face and the embed with the studio handed in 5_359 / about 1_650, the guest host about 146 / 55 on each of three measures, and Cloud's worker 9_409 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_080_480 B (1056 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged; a batch's `intent` where its caller gave none is now its acts' sentences joined rather than its first, and an op's `intent` is display only. Stored formats — unchanged; a document may carry `"places": 0` on a money field or a computed field, which a build before this one refuses by path, and a kind with more than 20 computed fields, which it refuses too. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — `places` and `{x | money: 0}` are new and optional; `set-format` takes `places`; `set-glance` takes a computed field; `add-lens` refuses a timeline that cannot draw as `lens-cannot-draw` (an error on the edit; the checker's finding of a stored declaration stays a warning, so a stored document still opens), and the finding now also says a timeline bound to a field that is not a number; no declaration that drew is refused. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: on this version `propose_change` refuses a timeline that cannot draw with the bindings it takes, money reads alike in a list, describe lines and badges say option labels, and `set-glance` takes a computed field with nothing of its own. Its `get_problems` said nothing of LifeLogics' timeline although the checker warned `lens-cannot-draw` (no columns) of it: Cloud's `get_problems` should surface warnings of `lens-cannot-draw`. Its `history` and a record's changes left out the batch of links though the framework's history has it, one gesture whose ops name each record linked — reading a batch's records from its ops' primitives (an edge's ends, not only a node's id) is Cloud's half.

## 0.1.62

### Patch Changes

- c8ac8e8: A rule can cover every record without a cost warning, Roadmap f-rule-covers-every. "Every candidate has a stance on every issue the guide covers" could be written only by counting the distinct issues a candidate's stances reach against `all('issue')`, and the checker rightly said `rule-cost` of it — the evaluator would walk every issue for every candidate — which a curated template may not carry, so the voter guide said only "has a stance on file". A rule now declares the shape instead: `{ "over": "candidate", "covers": { "every": "issue", "through": "stances", "via": "issue" }, "says": "{candidate} has no stance on {issue}" }` — `through` a relation of the rule's kind (or one that points at it) and `via` one more where the first lands on a kind in between, or `through` alone when it reaches the covered kind directly. The store judges it in one pass over the record's links (`uncovered`, a set per record held against the kind's live records), never through the expression evaluator, so there is no budget to run out of and no cost to warn of; each far record the subject does not reach is one problem naming both, and the sentence may name either by its kind beside the subject's fields. Its line reads "reaches every issue through stances, then issue", and broken, "does not reach Parks through stances, then issue". `graview check` says a relation that does not exist or does not lead from the kind to the covered one as `rule-covers-relation`, at `covers.through` or `covers.via` and in the person's voice with the way out ("add "via": the relation from stance to issue"), and a kind that is none as `rule-kind`; a rule that says both or neither of `require` and `covers`, or `covers` over the whole graph, is refused by path. A renamed kind or relation is followed in `covers`, and the rule goes with the kind it covers or a relation it walks. The same rule in TypeScript is `coverageRule` on `@graview/core/document`, which `toDocument` writes as `covers`; the studio keeps one through its round trip (`coversEvery`, `coversThrough`, `coversVia` on a rule) and writes it back as `coverageRule`, and `graview serve`'s source editor adds one beside the checkout's rules. The graview-invariant skill says the shape.
  
  Reading the shape, resolving the walk and judging it costs a hosted page 3.1 KB more up front (3_192 bytes minified, 1_029 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 2.2 KB (2_212, 717), under raises naming this changeset; a visitor's form handed a compiled app 2_207 / 724 more, every face 7_700 / 2_258, the embed with the studio handed in 7_154 / 2_117 and Cloud's worker 10_564 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_064_463 B (1040 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged; a compiled app's `RulePlan` may carry `covers` (each step with its direction) and no `require`, which a page handed one from this build reads. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — a rule gains the key `covers` and `require` becomes optional beside it (one of the two, refined by the schema and said again in `document.schema.json`); the finding code `rule-covers-relation` is new and said only of `covers`; an `InvariantDefinition`'s `judgment` and `shape` may carry `covers` and no `require`, so a host that read `judgment.require` as a string reads it as optional; no declaration that passed is refused. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: write the voter guide's rule as `covers` and drop the "has a stance on file" stand-in; a build that predates `covers` refuses it at `rules.<rule>.covers`, so Cloud stores one only on a build whose document schema has it.
- 055b1eb: A suggestion takes a vote from someone who may not accept it, Roadmap f-suggestion-votes (the framework half; Cloud's signed-out visitors, its tidying notice count and the owner's switch are Cloud's). Someone who could see a suggestion and could not make its change — a reader, a visitor by link, a seat signed out on a public app — pressed Accept in the Suggested tray and met a refusal. Now their Accept and Decline are a vote, for or against: an op of the built-in `vote-suggestion` act (`VOTE_SUGGESTION`, `{ suggestion, vote: "for" | "against" | null }`), which every store answers to and no list offers, as `keep-link` is — `mutation` the call, no primitives, the vote on the op (`Operation.vote`), the records the suggestion names as what its sentence read, so a seat kept from one is kept from the vote as it is from the suggestion. One standing vote per person, the latest, an agent's counted as the person it acts for; voting again changes it and `null` withdraws it (`voteSuggestion`); a vote is its voter's to undo, the system's, or a seat's the policy grants every act. `acceptSuggestion` and `declineSuggestion` ask the store the question Accept would meet (`mayDecide`: every call of the suggestion permitted to the seat) and, where the answer is no, keep the vote instead and answer `voted`; where it is yes they make or decline it as ever, and a store with no policy lets everyone decide as it always did. Every entry of `suggestions()` carries `tally: { for, against, mine? }` — the reader's own standing vote as `mine` — so a list sorts on it, and `votesIn` and `tallyOf` read it off a log. The tray says "Your vote was recorded" to a voter and keeps the row, the pressed button saying which way they voted, Withdraw vote beside it; to whoever may decide it shows "4 for, 1 against" beside Accept, kept current as votes land. An app may say `"tidying": { "autoAcceptAt": 4 }` at the top of its document (`GraviewApp.tidying`, `StoreOptions.tidying`, handed to the store by every host that hands it the policy): at that many votes for, the app's own actor makes the suggestion in the deciding vote's own gesture — one batch, one undo — with its lineage naming the suggestion and the voters (`lineage.voters`, at most 200), having rehearsed that the actor would be let: a record a person said to leave alone leaves the vote standing and the suggestion waiting. `graview check` warns `tidying-votes-unreachable` when the number is set and the app has no policy, since then nobody votes. The agent tools' `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion` answer `voted` from such a seat and `list_suggestions` carries each `tally`; no tool is added and no input schema moves. `a-suggestion-takes-a-vote-from-someone-who-may-not-accept-it.test.ts`, `an-app-says-a-suggestion-is-made-at-so-many-votes.test.ts` and `the-suggested-tray-takes-a-readers-vote-and-shows-the-tally-to-who-may-decide.test.tsx` hold it, and the chrome harness presses Accept as a reader on both faces and reads the tally as the keeper (aSuggestionTakesAVoteFromAReaderAndShowsTheTallyToWhoMayDecide). The graview-tidying skill says it. A hosted page carries 1.5 KB more up front (1_544 bytes minified, 486 gzipped; handed a compiled app, 1.4 KB: 1_470 and 479): the act, the vote's place on an op, the gesture's queue and who may take a vote back — the raises in `scripts/budget-raises/a-suggestion-takes-a-vote-*.json` say each entry's bytes, every one measured against a fresh build of main.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — one new optional op field, `vote`, on the op of one new built-in act, with no primitives and no new primitive; a log holding one folds the same on every build, and a build before this one keeps the field on an op it receives and lists the vote as a change that touched nothing. Stored formats — nothing moves: the op is new and absent in an older store, and a lineage may name `voters`, a key an older build keeps as it keeps any it does not know. The wire — a vote travels as an ordinary `call`; a server before this one refuses the act as unknown, so a host reads `capabilities().version` before it offers a vote to a seat. The declaration — a new optional top-level key, `tidying: { autoAcceptAt }`, refused by nothing that compiled before, and one new warning code, `tidying-votes-unreachable`. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion` gain `voted` in their answer and `list_suggestions` gains `tally`, both additive.
  
  What Graview Cloud does: on this version its readers' and link visitors' Accept and Decline in the tray become votes with no change of its own, its tidying notice may count a suggestion's `tally`, and its owner's switch is `tidying.autoAcceptAt` in the document rather than a setting of its own; votes from seats it signs out are its half to mint.
- 8f990e3: A video plays as a hero background, Roadmap f-video-hero (the framework half). A record's video was a player with the browser's controls, poster first, and nothing else: an immersive home that wanted a clip running quietly behind its words had no way to ask for one, and a worker view may not draw `<video>` at all. `<graview-video autoplay>` is now a background — muted, looping, inline and without controls, since autoplay without muted never plays and so the one word means all of it — and `<graview-video cover>` fills the box the view sized (`object-fit: cover`; the view's own stylesheet rounds and clips the box). The poster stays the value's, and the source, the poster and the controls stay the host's: `src`, `poster`, `controls`, `loop` and `muted` on a `<graview-video>` are refused as before. Where the person asked for less motion (`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`) a background stands still at its poster, fetching only its metadata, and is let go — or held again — as the setting changes while the page is open. The face's player (`Files` as a field or an image block, `VideoWay`: `autoplay`, `cover`) draws the same, with nothing said under a background. A view is handed `poster: true` among a video's facts where it has one, never the poster's path. A headless run lists each video the view places — `videos` on the place's description, each `{ record, field, text, name, source, duration, poster, autoplay, cover, playable, why }` — and says it in one line under "Videos:" in the place's words, `playable` being a source every browser plays (MP4, WebM or Ogg, what a host's upload transcodes to) with a poster to stand at, so an agent that wrote a hero confirms it plays without opening a browser; the part itself reads "The waves at dusk, a 45-second video, playing as a background, muted and looping, filling its box". The guest's, the primitives' and core's tests hold it (`a-video-plays-as-a-hero-background`, `a-place-description-lists-its-videos`), and the guest harness's pictures run places the porch's clip as a hero on every engine: played by nobody's press, muted and looping and covering its 120 px box, and still at its poster where the page asked for less motion. The guest README and the graview-view-drawing skill say it. The open kit's table and the host's player cost the guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit 1_226 / 377 more, a worker view's runtime 56 / 15 more, every face 2_721 / 939 more and Cloud's worker 3_446 bytes more, each under a raise naming this changeset; nothing a hosted page loads up front changed, and the guest host before a worker is drawn is as it was.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats and the declaration and check finding codes — unchanged. The wire — a worker view's file facts gain `poster: true` on a video that has one; a view that read none is unmoved. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; a place's description (`describePlace`, a headless run) gains the optional `videos` list and its text a "Videos:" section only where a view places one. What Graview Cloud does: nothing for a view that writes `<graview-video autoplay cover>`; its `propose_view` answer carries `videos`, which its authoring guide may point an agent at to confirm a hero plays; an app-level reel not tied to a record stays Cloud's half.
- f4da101: A view links to the Scene (FR-235), Roadmap f-view-links-to-scene; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-235. Graview Public's home view wrote `<a data-place="overview">`: the headless check read it as "It opens the place overview" — Graview Cloud hands the check the app's places as `placesOf` lists them, the Scene and the home among them — and on the page the link did nothing, and the owner got a toast that the place "overview" was no place of this app, because the live host judged a link by the registry's pictures alone. `graview.navigate("overview")` did nothing live either. Two lists, two answers. There is one vocabulary now, on `@graview/core/navigate` beside `narrowedPath`: `placeNamesOf(app, { views?, kept? })` from the declaration and `placeNamesFrom(schema, places, { pages?, kept? })` from what a face holds, one builder for both as `placesOf` and `placesFrom` are — `overview` (the Scene, titled as the declaration calls it), `home`, each kind's list by its slug, and each titled place (a declared lens, a view the host keeps) by its slug, less what the viewer is kept from; each one says what it is (`is`: `overview`, `home`, `list`, `place`) and the kind it is over. The headless check (`runWorkerViewHeadless`, `describePlace`'s press reading) and the live hosts — a worker view's (`createLinks`, through `placeVocabulary`), a frame guest's navigate and the page's own — all judge a `data-place` and a `navigate` against it, so the two cannot disagree again; a name outside it is refused with the names that exist, the Scene and the home now among them. Live, `overview` goes where "Show in the scene" goes: on the routed face the Scene's own address (`/places/overview`, the embed's way where it draws the scene, `sceneHref` where the face owns its page), carried as the link's `href`; on the Scene, the overview from above, as the bar's "The whole thing" and Up rise to it. `home` is the front page on the routed face, and on the Scene the page's door where a host draws both faces, else the overview. A lens by its slug opens its place, as before; a view's `props.places` now lists the whole vocabulary on the page, as it already did headless on Cloud. `a-view-may-name-the-scene-the-home-a-list-and-a-place.test.ts` (core) and `a-view-may-link-to-the-scene-and-the-home.test.ts` (guest) hold that the check accepts exactly what the page accepts, and the growing harness presses `<a data-place="overview">` and `graview.navigate("overview")` in a home view on the pages and in a view of a kind on the scene, landing on the Scene's overview each time with nothing said as a problem (aViewsLinkToTheOverviewLandsOnTheScenesOverviewOnBothFacesByALinkAndByNavigate). The graview-view-links skill says a view may link to the Scene and the home.
  
  A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (136 bytes minified, 27 gzipped; handed a compiled app, 153 and 46): the home's and the Scene's words on each face's way of going — the raises in `scripts/budget-raises/a-view-links-to-the-scene-*.json` say each entry's bytes. The vocabulary's builder rides the worker view's host, fetched with the first view: 2.5 KB minified on the guest host before a worker is drawn (2_489 / 961), 2_590 / 1_015 registering a view, 2_278 / 837 drawing a worker, 5_073 / 1_948 on the open kit, 3_060 / 1_450 on the pages face alone (1_402 measured, with the same room), 179 / 46 on a visitor's form alone and 187 / 50 handed a compiled app, 480 gzipped on every face (430 measured, with room for the bytes gzip moves between runs), and 2_460 bytes on Cloud's worker, each under a raise of its own.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire — unchanged in shape; a worker view's `props.places` on the page now lists `home`, `overview` and each kind's list beside the titled places (each `{ as, title, kind }`, the Scene's and the home's `kind` empty), as a headless run handed `placesOf` already listed them; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-235. What Graview Cloud does: nothing to the check it runs — it already hands `placesOf(app)`; the page catches up to it. A home that links to the Scene can keep its `data-place="overview"`.
- 24638b9: A worker view's draw budget fits the device, Roadmap f-view-budget-fits-device. The time a view had over one push of what it is shown (`pushMs`, 1 000 ms) and the page's own time drawing what it sent (`drawMs`, 100 ms of any second) were fixed numbers, so the slowest devices lost their views first: on a car's browser the home-screen views fell back to the app's own page as "took longer than 1,000 ms to draw" while the same views drew well inside a second on a laptop. The page now measures how long its first view's worker took to start — from `new Worker` to its runtime's ready, which is the device making a thread and parsing and running the view's runtime — once, keeps it (`device-pace.ts`; the second view on the page needs no measuring), and every view is given its base times that pace: 1 for a start of 60 ms or under, so a laptop keeps today's guard, rising to a tenth to a ceiling of 4 at 240 ms or more. The base is the host's own `pushMs` and `drawMs` where it gives them, else the defaults, so Graview Cloud's 3 000 / 300 scales the same way and never past four times; the message allowance, the node cap and the byte limits never scale, so a flood is a flood on any device; the headless runner's budget is the server's and stays fixed. The sentence a stopped view is given names the budget it was actually held to ("It took longer than 2,400 ms to draw what it was shown."), the mounted view says the page's `pace` once its worker is ready, and the page keeps whatever it shows — the drawing before, or nothing — until the view draws or that budget runs out: no fallback was drawn before a first draw and then swapped, and none is now. A start the network slowed (`worker: { url }` answered late) reads as a slow device too, which gives a view on a slow link a slow device's time and no more of anything else. The guest's tests hold it with a stand-in worker that says ready when told (`a-worker-views-draw-budget-fits-the-device`: pace 1 on a fast start, 4 at the ceiling, the host's base honored, the counts unscaled, the second view given the first one's pace), and `guest-sandbox --transport=limits` holds it in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: the limits page is at one pace, measured once from its first worker's start — 1 on a laptop at rest, reported rather than fixed, since three harnesses side by side can make a first start slow — and stops its spinner as slow at that pace times 1,000 ms with the sentence naming it; and a page whose views come from a script the server holds 300 ms is at pace 4, where a view that draws in 300 ms draws, one whose listener takes 1 500 ms a push draws, the spinner is stopped at 4,000 ms with the sentence naming it, and the flood is stopped at the same 120 messages. Under `GRAVIEW_SLOW=4` the suite throttles each page's main thread as the nightly's runner is, and the limits page measures its first start slower (pace 2.1 on this laptop) and holds its spinner to that — the engine throttles no worker, so the throttled main thread is what a slow device looks like to the host. The guest README and `graview-worker-view` say the budget fits the device. The guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit is 0.4 KB larger (408 bytes minified, 154 gzipped: the pace kept and fitted, the draw budget reading its allowance at each batch, the view saying its pace), fetched only when a worker view first draws, and the guest host drawing a kit worker 30 bytes minified (the shared start measuring itself) — the raises in `scripts/budget-raises/a-worker-views-draw-budget-fits-the-device-*.json` say each entry's bytes; a page's first load is unchanged.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: nothing; its 3 000 / 300 for people's pages (branch cloud/view-budget) is scaled by the page's pace as the defaults are, and it may drop that branch and take the adaptive default.
- 7d85f11: An app's opening screen is in its declaration, Roadmap f-splash-in-document. Graview Cloud kept what a page shows in its first second — while the app's code loads, gone the moment a place has drawn — as a setting of its own, so an export, a remix into a self-hosted app and an import all arrived without it. Now it is the document's: `brand.splash` is `{ "style", "image"?, "title"?, "kicker"?, "shader"? }`, the style one of `plain` (the ground, the app's mark and its name), `editorial` (the image full-bleed, the title very large at the foot), `shader` (the image drifting through one of the shaders the renderer ships — `drift`, `ripple` or `veil`, named by `shader`, never written in a document: data is never code) or `ascii` (the image as characters in the accent, the title typing on); the image a mark as the logo takes one (inline SVG, or a path on the app's own host), the title in place of the app's name, the kicker one small line over it. It is judged where the rest of the brand is: `brand-splash-image` refuses an image that could act or lives on another origin, `brand-splash-shader` a shader the renderer does not ship (naming the ones it does) and warns of one named on a style that draws none, and `brand-splash-style` an edit's style off the list (a document's shape refuses it first). `set-brand` takes `splash` whole and `null` clears it, saying "The app opens on an editorial screen while it loads."; `diffDocuments` says the same. Cloud's `custom` style — a fragment shader of the owner's own — stays Cloud's: the framework never stores code in a declaration, so a remix of an app with a custom screen lands on the editorial one.
  
  The renderer is `@graview/pages/splash`: `splashHtml(brand, { scheme?, name? })` answers `{ html, css, script }` — one element, a stylesheet scoped to it and a plain script — with no import at all, not React and nothing of the framework, bundling to under 14 KB (a test holds it, and runs it in a bare context), so a host inlines the three in its first HTML before the app's root and the first paint never waits on the bundle the screen covers for. The script takes the screen away once a place says `data-graview-drawn` (FR-188's signal), or the page fails with an alert, or fifteen seconds pass, and under reduced motion at once and with nothing moving; WebGL refused, the shader screen is the editorial one. `graview preview` draws it: `servePreview` takes `splash` (the renderer) and serves the page with the screen inlined for a document whose brand has one, and the page as built for one that has none or for a visitor's form; the `graview` tool bundles the renderer beside the page it already carries (`dist/preview/splash.js`), so it takes no dependency on the pages face. The preview harness holds that the screen is in the first HTML, on the page when the document has parsed and gone once the place has drawn, for the shader and the ascii styles; the core tests hold that an export (the document as JSON), an import (compiled again) and a remix (the compiled app written back as its document) keep it. The brand skill says the shape.
  
  Reading the shape costs a hosted page 0.1 KB more up front (144 bytes minified, 65 gzipped), under a raise naming this changeset; every face 1_946 / 722 more and Cloud's worker 3_799 bytes, each under a raise of its own; a page handed a compiled app, the pages face alone, a visitor's form and the guest host are unchanged.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged; a compiled app does not carry the screen, which is a host's first HTML's, drawn from the document. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — the brand gains the key `splash`, which a build that predates it refuses by path (`brand.splash`); `brand-splash-image`, `brand-splash-shader` and `brand-splash-style` are new and judge only the new key; every other code is unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: stores the opening screen in the document's `brand.splash` through `propose_change` with `set-brand` in place of its own setting, keeps its `custom` style and its default `graview` frame as settings of its own, and inlines `splashHtml(document.brand, { name: document.name })` from `@graview/pages/splash` in the first HTML its shell serves.
- b5ced87: Calendar events invite the people they are about, Roadmap f-calendar-guests-mapping and f-calendar-guests-recipe. A Google calendar connection kept an event's title, its times and its place, and never who was coming: a household's dinner linked to the two people it is for reached the calendar with no guests, and an invitation sent from the calendar reached nobody here. Now a connection's `fields` may name `guests` — a field of the kind holding the addresses (a `list`, or one `email`), kept as the attendees; or `{ "via": "who" }`, the kind's relation to the people, each reached person's `emails` invited while their `inviteMe` is on (`emails` and `invite` name other fields; `invite: false` says the app has no toggle and every address is invited). The relation rides the engine's edge machinery: a person's remote id is their GUEST KEY (their addresses lowercased, once each, sorted and joined with ","), so a person with two addresses is one guest with two attendees, an attendee whose address is a person's links that person through `connect-<kind>-<relation>`, and one matching nobody — the calendar's organizer, a stranger — is held as part of the relation's value (`SyncState.unresolved`) and sent back as it was, never dropped; turning Invite me on links what was held, with nothing sent for it. A person without an address or with the toggle off is no guest either way: never sent, never matched. A field of addresses is compared as the calendar keeps it (`ResourceMapping.normalize`), so "Ben@Example.com " typed here is not sent on every pass. The transport says `sendUpdates` on every insert, patch and delete — `all` with `notify: true` on `guests`, `none` otherwise — where Google used to decide, so nobody is mailed unless the declaration asks. `graview check` judges it in a person's words: `connection-guests-field` (the field, or the reached kind's `emails` or `invite`, missing or of the wrong type, with the `add-field` that fixes it as a `fixes` entry a host applies in one click) and `connection-guests-via` (not a relation of the kind, one reaching several kinds, or "who reaches person, and a person has no emails to invite"; a warning naming the app's people kind when the relation reaches another). The `graview-sync` skill carries the recipe for "invite my email on events I'm on" (`calendar-events-invite-the-people-they-are-about.test.ts`; against Google itself, `verify-google-calendar.mjs` proves two emails are two attendees, taken off when unlinked, an attendee added there linking its person, and a stranger kept through a round trip). A hosted page's first load is unchanged: the mapping is the connection's, run by a host. Cloud's worker, which runs connections and checks documents, carries 10_332 bytes more; every face and an embed handed the studio, whose editor judges a connection, carry 3_683 bytes more minified and 1_123 / 1_111 gzipped (the checker's judgment of `guests`); each under a raise of its own.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, the wire, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes: additive — a Google calendar connection's `fields` takes `guests`, which a build that predates it refuses by path; `connection-guests-field` and `connection-guests-via` are new codes, and no declaration that passed is refused. Stored formats: `SyncState` is unchanged in shape; a guests relation's held ids are addresses rather than Notion page ids, and `RemoteChange.fields` from the calendar carries `attendees` (an empty list for an event with none). What Graview Cloud does: offer `guests` where it draws a calendar connection's fields, with the people kind's relations to pick `via` from, and apply each finding's `fixes` as it does `connection-creates`'s.
- d9d288c: Every view takes the app's stylesheet (Roadmap f-app-wide-view-theme). Each custom view carried its own copy of the stylesheet, so a polished home and the record pages drifted apart, and a brand change reached the view's tokens but never its rules. A document's brand now names what every worker view inherits: `brand.views.css`, a stylesheet the host draws in each view's region before the view's own — a `<style>` of the host's between the region's rules and the view's sheet, so the view's rule wins wherever both say one — and `brand.views.motion: "fade"`, under which the host fades a view's drawing out when the record it shows changes and in again once the view has drawn the new one (nothing moves where the person asked for less motion; a shared-element transition between records is not this, and is not promised). The brand's typeface and scheme tokens reach a view as they always did; the view is told nothing of either, and is dressed again when the brand changes (`handle.setBrand`). The sheet is held to exactly what a view's own is — nothing that fetches, no `@import`, no `:host`, 64,000 characters — by the same code: the CSS sanitizer and its tables moved from the guest's open kit to `@graview/core/css` (a subpath on no page: `sanitizeStylesheet`, `sanitizeStylesheets`, `sanitizeDeclarations`, `sanitizeValue`, the `CSS_*` tables, `OPEN_MAX_STYLESHEET`), `@graview/guest`'s host and headless run draw through it, and `graview check` and `compileDocument` hold `brand.views.css` to it, saying what the open kit leaves out as a warning, `brand-views-css` ("url() in background, @import"), and a sheet too long for a stylesheet as an error. `set-brand` takes `views` — `css` and `motion`, `null` clearing either or all of it — says what it did ("Every view is drawn with the app's own stylesheet before its own.", "A view fades when the record it shows changes."), as `diffDocuments` says it, and refuses a sheet the page would draw only part of, naming the part, so an agent learns at once rather than from a view that drew without it. A headless run (`runWorkerViewHeadless`) takes `brand` and answers `css`, its stylesheets as a page draws them with the app's first, so a picture of a proposed view is dressed as the page dresses it; `graview view check --browser` audits with that. The `graview-brand` and `graview-view-drawing` skills say it; `guest-sandbox --transport=brand` holds that the view's heading takes the brand's sheet and keeps its own rule where both say one, fades where the brand asks, and loses both when the brand does.
  
  Reading the brand's key and carrying it to the Brand a page draws from costs a hosted page 0.2 KB more up front (192 bytes minified, 62 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.1 KB (136, 44), under raises naming this changeset; a visitor's form handed a compiled app 136 / 44 more, every face 3_916 / 1_840, the guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit 1_145 / 394 (the host's theme sheet and the fade), Cloud's worker 9_034 bytes, and the embed with the studio handed in 24_744 / 7_930 — the sanitizer, which that page fetched only as a view first drew, now comes with the editor's first load, since `editDocument` judges a brand's sheet as the page draws it — each under a raise of its own. The shape a page reads up front names the sanitizer's limit nowhere, so the two share no module and the page loads the chunks it loaded.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged; a compiled app carries `views` within its `brand` in `graview-compiled@1`, and a page that predates it draws the brand it knows. The wire — unchanged; nothing of the theme is handed to a view. The declaration and check finding codes — `brand.views` is new, refused by path by a build that predates it; `brand-views-css` is a new code, a warning about a key no declaration could hold before, and an error only where the sheet is longer than a stylesheet may be; the edit vocabulary's `set-brand` gains `views`. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The CSS tables (`CSS_PROPERTIES`, `CSS_FUNCTIONS`, `CSS_AT_RULES`, `CSS_KEYWORD_PROPERTIES`, `CSS_FRAGMENT_PROPERTIES`, `CSS_REFUSED_SELECTORS`, `CSS_PREFIXED_SELECTORS`) and `OPEN_MAX_STYLESHEET` are `@graview/core/css`'s and no longer the guest's open kit's, which was never an entry of the package. What Graview Cloud does: store an app's theme as `brand.views` through `propose_change` with `set-brand`, pass `brand: app.brand` to `runWorkerViewHeadless` so `propose_view`'s pictures are dressed, and show `brand-views-css` where it shows the brand's other findings.
- 509832a: Formulas join text and build a date-time (Roadmap f-formula-text-datetime). A computed event title or an act's start time could not be built: the rule language counted days and added numbers, and had no way to say "Tue 27 Oct · 9:30 AM" or to make the instant a plan item's day reaches at 9:30. Seven functions now: `concat(name, ' · ', place)` joins words, nothing joining as no words (where `+`, which already joined words, makes the whole nothing); `format(start, 'EEE d MMM · h:mm a')` says a moment by a closed pattern — `EEE`, `EEEE`, `d`, `MMM`, `MMMM`, `yyyy`, `h:mm a`, `HH:mm`, any other words in single quotes — in the app's `brand.locale` and on its `brand.timeZone`, never the reader's, since what the language builds may be kept on a record; `datetime(due, '09:30')` and its alias `at(due, startTime)` make the instant that day reaches at that wall time in the app's zone, kept as a datetime field keeps one (`2026-11-01T14:30:00Z` for 9:30 in New York on the day its clock falls back), on UTC when the app says no zone so a server and a page agree; `addMinutes(start, 90)` moves a moment, an instant staying an instant across a change of the clock; `weekday(due)` is `mon` to `sun`; `startOfWeek(due)` (or `startOfWeek(due, 'sun')`) is the day its week starts. Each is total — nothing in is nothing out — and every type mistake is a sentence. The arithmetic is one module (`expr/when.ts`), the clock the app's (`EvalContext.locale`, `timeZone`, which a compiled app passes from its brand). The checker types them from the declaration alone (`expr/typing.ts`, judged with `compileDocument` and kept off the page): `format` of a number field, `weekday` of words, a time of day not written `HH:MM`, minutes that are words and a first day it does not know are `expr-type`; a pattern piece outside the list, a pattern not in quotes and a time piece of a date that has no time are `format-pattern` — each at the expression's path, with the fix. `formulas-join-text-and-build-a-date-time.test.ts` holds them, on the days New York's clock changes.
  
  An act updates the records it is linked to (Roadmap f-acts-update-linked). `setsOther` set fields on the far record only while `connects` made the link, so a person editing a plan item could not carry the change to its event. An act `on` a kind now says `"setsLinked": { "event": { "title": { "expr": "name" } } }`: the fields are set, in the same change, on every record its subject already reaches through the relation — from whichever end the declaration puts the subject on — each expression read standing on the subject with `to` the far record (`concat(name, ' for ', to.title)`) and `$to` that record; one undo takes all of it back. The store judges each far record's own edit before the act runs (`writesLinked` on the mutation; `edit-<kind>` as the policy and W-201 read it), and refuses the act naming the record where one is not the person's to change: "Not permitted: “Rename the item” would change “Old title”, an event that is not yours to change." Under a seat's sight the act reaches only the linked records it sees. The far kind's own edit still offers a carried field, since the act carries a value to it rather than naming a step. `graview check` says `act-sets-linked-relation` (no such relation joins the subject's kind, naming the ones that do; an act on no kind) and `act-sets-linked-field` (no such field at the far end; a value it could not hold), and `computed-written` for a worked-out field, in the person's voice; the hidden-reads check counts the far kind as read. `editDocument` keeps a carried field in step with a renamed field or relation, drops it with a removed one, and drops an act left doing nothing; `add-act` takes the key. `graview describe` gains "Carried across links". `an-act-sets-the-records-it-is-linked-to.test.ts` holds it. The graview-invariant, graview-view-specs and graview-sight skills say both.
  
  A hosted page carries 7.0 KB more up front, minified (6.4 KB handed a compiled app) — 7_109 and 6_501 bytes measured, each raise rounded up to the hundred — and its scene face 7_109 bytes more before it draws: the evaluator and the date arithmetic, the compiled act's `through` effect, and the store's judgment of a far record; the typing stays with the checker. Cloud's worker carries 31_494 bytes more, unminified; each face, each visitor's form and each guest host measure under a raise of its own in `scripts/budget-raises/formulas-and-linked-sets-*.json`.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged (a `setsLinked` adds no argument; its far records are reached, never named). The wire: unchanged. Stored formats: `graview-compiled@1` may carry a `LinkedSetEffect` (`{ set, through }`) in an act's plan, which a page that predates it would not run — a host serves a compiled app from the build that compiled it, as it does; snapshots and the log are unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes: additive — an act takes `setsLinked`, which a build that predates it refuses by path; the rule language takes seven more functions, which such a build refuses as `unknown-function`; `expr-type`, `format-pattern`, `act-sets-linked-relation` and `act-sets-linked-field` are new, said by the checker alone, and no declaration that passed is refused. `effectsOf` may return a `LinkedSetEffect` beside the effects a document lists, a mutation definition may say `writesLinked`, and `EvalContext` takes `locale` and `timeZone`. What Graview Cloud does: offer `setsLinked` where it edits an act, and let a computed title or an act's value be built with the new functions; its worker's import budget is raised by name.
- 1d2b75a: A notice carries its tone as a chip, not a side accent (FR-236), Roadmap f-notices-without-side-accent; and the framework's own error toasts offer a host's "Report this" (FR-237), Roadmap f-notices-report-hook. Every toast and banner drew a thick colored border on its left side alone, and so did the warn boxes on a record's page, the problems page and a place printed as static HTML — a major AI tell, Nick said. A notice is now one hairline all round in the app's radius, its tone a small tinted chip at its head carrying a glyph (✓, !, i), hidden from a reader, who is told the tone by the status or alert the notice is announced through as before; the warn boxes keep their tint under the same hairline and radius. AA holds in both schemes (the chrome harness measures 15.9 and 15.6 against 4.5). Graview Cloud gives its own error toasts a "Report this" that opens its feedback dialog prefilled; the toasts the framework raises itself — a tidying the host refused, a take-back that failed — could carry none. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `@graview/embed/pages` and the Shell take `onReport(report)` beside `barActions`: given, every warning or error toast the framework raises draws a native button, "Report this", named for the toast ("Report this: “Up next” could not be hidden."), that hands the hook `{ message, code?, place?, record?, version }` — the toast's words, a refusal's code when it had one, the place the reader is on (a list's or a place's slug, `home`, `overview` on the scene) and the record when one is open, and `FRAMEWORK_VERSION`. The toast stays while the button has focus (the stack is held, as under a pointer) and stays once pressed, so the words are there to read beside the host's dialog. What the framework says is told apart from what the host says by the board: the app's own surfaces speak on `frameworkVoice(board)`, which marks each notice `from: "framework"`, and a host's `notify` never does, so the host's own notices carry no such button. `Notice` gains `code` and `from`; `NoticeReport` and `NoticeReporting` are the shapes; `sceneReportWhere` says where the scene's reader is. The primitives' tests (`a-notice-carries-its-tone-as-a-chip-not-a-side-accent`, `an-error-toast-offers-the-hosts-report-this`), the embed's (`a-hosts-report-this-answers-the-frameworks-own-toast`) and the pages' broken-rule box hold it, and the chrome harness holds `noticesCarryNoSideAccent` (no element in a toast or a banner has a border on its left wider than its other sides, in both schemes) and `anErrorToastOffersTheHostsReport` (a host that refuses a change to the declaration, Hide pressed on a list: the button named for the toast, the toast held while it has focus, the report handed with the list's slug and the version, the toast gone once focus has moved on; without the hook, no button). The host-page skill and the embed's README say it beside `notify`, and `docs/stability.md` says what a harness reaches.
