A hosted page carries 0.8 KB more up front (780 bytes minified, 320 gzipped; handed a compiled app, the same 0.8 KB: 780 and 320) — the framework's voice, the scene's where and the frame's hand of `onReport`; the chip, the button and the version it hands ride the notices' door — under raises named `notices-without-the-side-accent-and-a-hosts-report-this-*` in `scripts/budget-raises/`, with the pages face alone 460 / 160 and every face 40 gzipped; the scene face before it draws measured 1_062_059 B (1038 KB). Both are said as shipped: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-236 and FR-237.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire: unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-236 and FR-237. The look (not a surface): a notice's side accent is gone, its chip is `[data-testid="notice-chip"]` and its sentence `[data-testid="notice-sentence"]`, and a reportable toast carries `[data-testid="notice-report"]`. What Graview Cloud does: hand `mount` its feedback dialog as `onReport`, prefilled from the report, and keep its own toasts' "Report this" as they are.
- 0e74201: Ask AI in an app proposes a change to the app itself (Roadmap f-seat-reshape). The seat's tools read, and the right-click menu's suggestions — learned and tidying — cover records, so "we also need to track who's driving" or "split this into before and after the trip" had no path inside the app: it went to a chat somewhere else. Now the person's words are matched to structure through the same judgments as the new-app starter: `@graview/tools/reshape`, a new entry, asks a Noul per trait the app lacks (`need:<trait>`: "does what they asked for need people who own things, which this app has no part for yet?") and a Noul per part it has (`drop:kind:<name>`, `drop:trait:<name>`: "did they ask to stop keeping areas?") over one state — what they said and the app in words — through whatever `decide` the host passes (Jev, as `dream` takes one; a stub in a test); `readReshape(answers, thresholds)` reads the answers (a trait added at 0.7, a kind or trait dropped at 0.7 — dropping loses records, so the bar is never lower) with every raw Noul kept; `reshapeCard({ said, document, add, drop })` says the card in words — `said` in one sentence ("This adds people who own things and keeps categories and vendors."), `adds` (each trait with what its module brings, from a new `TRAIT_BRINGS` beside `TRAIT_WORDS`: "a person kind (or the people the app has), an owner link, Give it to someone, …"), `keeps`, `drops`, and `breaking` as far as the seat can say ("All categories records go, with their links.") — and `proposeReshape({ said, document, decide })` runs the turn, its proposal null when the reading changes nothing. The conversation draws the card beneath the seat's answer (`ChatPanel`): added, kept and dropped as chips the person turns off and on, each toggle said again by the same function so what they accept is always the reading the card shows, breaking changes above the press; Accept hands the proposal — `{ said, add, drop, card }`, the reading as the person left it, never edits — to the host's `onProposeChange(proposal) → "applied" | "declined"`, on `GraviewProvider`, `FrameOptions` (so `mount`, `<Embed>` and the pages alone take it) and the pages' context, beside `onKeepLens`. The host composes the edits from that reading: `composeReshape({ document, add, drop })` on `@graview/core/compose` runs `startingDocument` on the app's own document and answers its `edits` (which `startingDocument` now carries beside `took`, `left` and `said`: the ones that took, in order, replaying onto the base in one `editDocument` call to the document it composed) with `breaking` said from each removal (`breakingOf`) and `notDone`; the host previews them with its data and applies them as the person, as one undoable change, under its own policy, or declines. Code writes every edit, the model never a kind, a field or a rule — and the composer never rides on a page: a page that fetched `@graview/core/compose` when asked made the editor and the critic live up front, which no page carries, so the vocabulary and a document's reading alone stand on a new entry, `@graview/core/traits` (`TRAITS`, `TRAIT_WORDS`, `TRAIT_BRINGS`, `traitsOfDocument`, `hasTrait`, `traitWords`), `traitsOf` of a TypeScript app stays on `/compose` with the composer, and the document barrel says `documentOf` and `rememberDocument` from where they live rather than through the writer. A host's model with tools is offered `propose_change` on its turn — the Roadmap's `propose-change`, spelled as the surface spells its tools — told the card and never an edit, and the card it proposed travels on the reply. The tool exists only for a seat whose role may change the declaration (`mayChangeDeclaration` on `@graview/tools`, the judgment the studio's door now shares: an owner or editor, never a viewer) with a host that handed the hook and a model that decides, over an app with a document to change — the one it was compiled from (`documentOfApp`); an app declared in code remembers itself first (`rememberDocument(app, toDocument(app).document)`, as Things does for its harness) or is offered none. For any other seat there is no door and no card, nothing refused. The panel reads the words for a change to the app conservatively ("we also need", "keep track of who", "add a field", "no longer need … any more"), and a reading that adds and drops nothing goes on to the usual answer. `graview serve`'s own seat passes no hook: the served page has no path that writes its document. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (145 bytes minified, 70 gzipped: the provider and the frame carry the hook, and the barrel exports `documentOf`); handed a compiled app, 0.2 KB (197 and 197); the pages face alone 334 / 218 more, the embed with the studio handed in 702 / 950, every face 23_150 / 9_033 across the doors a face can fetch — the reading, the card and the seat's tool riding the seat's model, never up front — and the guest host registering a worker view 0 / 1, its own bytes unchanged and the chunk names its imports carry gzipping a byte larger beside the new doors, each under a raise in `scripts/budget-raises/the-seat-proposes-a-change-to-the-app-*.json` naming this changeset. `the-seat-proposes-a-change-to-the-app-from-words.test.ts`, `the-seat-offers-a-change-to-the-app-only-with-a-host-to-apply-it.test.tsx` and the starting-document test hold it, and the seat harness drives the keeper's "we also need who's driving" on Things to the card and through Accept, with a member drawing none. The agent-seat and studio skills say it.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — unchanged; `startingDocument` answers one more key, `edits`, and `composeReshape`, `breakingOf` and `TRAIT_BRINGS` are additions on `/compose`, `@graview/core/traits` a new entry carrying what `/compose` already named. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged on `graview mcp` and `toolDefinitions`: `propose_change` is listed only on a seat's turn whose context carries `reshape`, which only a host with `onProposeChange` hands it. What Graview Cloud does: pass `onProposeChange` to `mount` for an owner's or editor's seat; on its server, `composeReshape({ document, add, drop })` from the proposal, preview the edits through its own propose_change and apply them through apply_change as the person; decline for a plan without structure changes.

## 0.1.61

### Patch Changes

- c9fd45a: A choice takes several of its options at once (FR-233), Roadmap f-multi-select-enum; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-233. An enum took one option and a list took free words, so a memo's recipients — the CTO, the developers, QA, product — had nowhere to be several fixed options. A field is now `{ "type": "enum", "multiple": true, "options": [...] }`: the one shape an enum already has, so its `options`, its `optionLabels` (0.1.56) and `set-options` carry over unchanged, rather than a `list` with `options`, which would have given options a second home on a type whose `of` already says what it holds. It compiles to a list of the options (`z.array(z.enum(...))`), which `fieldSpecOf` reads back as the same field and the generated types say as an array of the union. A record's facts, a card, a glance, a change's sentence and a sheet (`/csv`, `/words`) say each option it holds by its label, "CTO, QA"; a form on the routed face asks with a box per option and sends the list of those ticked, in the options' order; a record's page changes it in place as the same boxes; the scene's ask strip already toggled several of a choice. A list filters by one of its options (`/arrange`, the filter chips and a view's `where`) and is neither sorted nor grouped by it — a record would stand in several groups — which `graview check` says of a list declared grouped by one and of a board bound to one. An import (`/import`, `import_rows`) reads a cell that lists them, "CTO; QA" or "developers, product"; a Notion connection holds it in a multi-select (`notionFieldType`, `several choices` in `NOTION_FITS`), never a select; a derived act's JSON Schema and tool schema ask for an array of the enum (`/json-schema`); a migration keeps the options that fit when a field changes between one and several (`coerce`). The studio's meta-schema has `multiple` on a field, `add-field` and `retype-field` take it, `set-multiple` changes it alone, and a declaration's list of an enum round-trips through the studio's graph, its source and its edits. `multiple` on a field that is not an enum is refused as a shape finding at `kinds.<kind>.fields.<field>.multiple`, in `graview check` and in `document.schema.json` alike; a default outside the options, or a single one where a list is wanted, is `default-option`. The graview-node-kind skill says the shape in one line. `a-choice-takes-several-options.test.ts`, `a-form-asks-for-several-options-as-boxes.test.tsx`, `several-options-changed-in-place-as-boxes.test.tsx` and `a-choice-of-several-round-trips-through-the-studio.test.ts` hold it, and the field-types harness opens a rehearsal's sections on the page and in the form.
  
  Reading the shape, compiling the list and saying its options costs a hosted page 0.5 KB more up front (522 bytes minified, 278 fewer gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.2 KB (161 more minified, 271 fewer gzipped), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 2_246 / 565 more, a visitor's form alone 709 / 126, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 863 / 102, every face 1_480 gzipped, the guest host 36 / 12 registering a view, 6 drawing a worker and 72 on the open kit, and Cloud's worker 4_641 bytes, each under a raise of its own; the scene face before it draws measured 1_054_746 B (1031 KB).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged; a record of such a field holds a JSON array of its options, as a list field does. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — a field gains the key `multiple` (an enum's only; on any other type it is refused as `shape` at `kinds.<kind>.fields.<field>.multiple`, as `options` is), `default-option` also judges a list default, `retype-field` takes `multiple`, and a Notion `connection-field-type` finding names `several choices` for such a field; every other code is unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas — a form field of a list of an enum is now one `choice` control with `multiple: true` where it was a `list` of `choice` controls (`FormField`); a tool's input schema for the argument is the array of the enum it already was. What Graview Cloud does: declares `"multiple": true` on an enum that takes several; draws a `choice` form field with `multiple` as checkboxes if it draws its own forms.

## 0.1.60

### Patch Changes

- 4eda24b: A day of a series changed apart mints no id from a field a sight keeps. "Change just this one" (`detach-<kind>`) and "Change from here" (`split-<kind>`) make a record of their own from the series, and minted its id from the series' label with no kind before it (`board-call-re-layoffs`), past the rule every other minted id goes by since an id came never to quote a field a sight keeps: where a sight's `hide` or `fields` kept a meeting's title from a role, that role read the title in the id it was served. Now they mint as the rest do — saying their kind, and from the kind's name where the label quotes a field some sight may keep from some role (`meeting:meeting`), from the label where none does (`meeting:board-call-re-layoffs`). There is one rule for it (`quotesKeptField`, beside `mayKeepField`): `freshId` asks it of its arguments and of a record it is handed third (`freshId(label, kind, record)`), which an act of the app's own may pass where its name was worked out from a record rather than an argument; a document's act asks it of the record it makes, by the document's own policy, so an act compiled outside a store holds to it too; and an import asks it of the row's fields. A name that holds a kept field's words among others, not only one that is the field, is now minted from the kind's name too: a tip titled "Acme renewal at risk" whose client "Acme" the public may not see is `tip:tip`. Core's tests hold it (`a-day-changed-apart-mints-no-id-from-a-kept-field.test.ts`, and `an-id-never-quotes-a-field-a-sight-keeps.test.ts` for a document's act, in a store and outside one). The `graview-sight` skill now says what a seat reads beside a record keeps to its sight: an id never minted from a kept field, a record withheld whole refused as one not there, the app's own actor's suggestion basis withheld where it read a kept field, and a sight with `hide` or `fields` not counting as a worker view's total sight. The store mints on every page, so a hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (126 bytes minified, 73 gzipped; 134 / 77 handed a compiled app), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 102 / 53 more, a visitor's form 102 / 56, one handed a compiled app 134 / 64, and Cloud's worker 239 bytes, each under a raise of its own.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. `MutationContext.freshId` takes an optional third argument, the record a name was worked out from. A record made by `detach-<kind>` or `split-<kind>` gets an id that starts with its kind, and from the kind's name where some sight keeps the field its label reads; a record a document's act makes, or an act of the app's own makes from a kept argument, gets one from the kind's name where its name quotes a kept field among other words; ids already held are kept. What Graview Cloud does: nothing.
- 4e41a99: A loopback `graview serve` or `graview preview` believes a seat only from a request carrying its run's key. Each kept a page on another origin out, and a name rebound to 127.0.0.1, but another account on this machine, or any program that is no browser, sends no `Origin` at all, and it was believed as any seat but the system's: it read the whole store, records the policy hides included, and wrote to it as any member — or, on a preview, as the page's own seat. Now each run of `graview serve`, `graview preview` and `graview shots` on loopback mints a key (32 random bytes, `mintServeKey` in `@graview/ship/cli`; `serveKey` takes `GRAVIEW_SERVE_KEY` instead when it is set and at least 16 characters, for a harness that starts its own server) and says it only in its terminal, in every address it prints (`address: http://localhost:5196/?graview_key=<key>`; the preview's page, scene and forms lines too). A request is believed only when it carries the key: in `x-graview-key` from a program, in the `graview_key` query parameter on a page's WebSocket (which cannot set a header) or a page's leave beacon, or in the cookie a browser is given for opening a printed address (`graview_key_<port>`, named for the server's port because a browser sends a cookie to every port of a host; `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`; a value made from the key rather than the key; and the address tidied by a 303), each compared in constant time (`sameKey` and `tradeKey`, shared with the dev server's doors). Without it every route but health answers 401 with where the key is, and so does the live wire's upgrade; a browser's preflight passes, and the key is never written into an answer, nor handed past the door to a route or a host's `answer`. A new run is a new key. `serveStore({ key })` and `servePreview({ key })` turn it on for any host, and a preview's `address` carries it; `SERVE_KEY` names the header, parameter and cookie, and `servedAddress` takes a printed address apart into where the store is and its key, from the main, browser and runtime entries. `openRemote`, `remoteFriction`, `remoteSubscriptions` and `remoteViews` take the printed address whole and send its key as the header, so `graview mcp --remote-url` and `graview apply --remote-url` do too, and say where they act without the key. `graview shots` opens each page at its address with the key. The AGENTS.md `graview create` writes says to open the Pages address `graview preview` prints, since it carries the key. A request for an address no URL parses (`//[`) is refused rather than ending the server.
  
  The rota page's `?server=` takes the printed address; opened without the key — as the desk's "Data in a folder you can open" stop opens it, since the desk never starts the server nor knows its key — it says what the server said and asks for the address `pnpm serve:rota` printed, and opens the roster from it, and the desk's stop says so beside its link. The architecture face recipe's dev server takes the preview's printed address as `GRAVIEW_PREVIEW` and carries the key on what it proxies, handing its page only where the preview is.
  
  `a-loopback-serve-believes-a-seat-only-with-its-runs-key.test.ts` holds that a program with no Origin and no key claiming a member's seat is refused, that the header, the query and the traded cookie are believed, that the live wire keeps to the same rule, that `openRemote` opens live from the printed address, that the key appears in no answer, and that an unparseable address is refused; `a-loopback-preview-believes-its-page-only-with-its-runs-key.test.ts` that a keyed preview answers nothing but health without the key, lets its page in by the cookie named for its port, serves the key in neither its page nor its document, and takes `GRAVIEW_SERVE_KEY`. The harnesses that start a served store or a preview (served, desk, preview with shots, date-inputs, scene-at-scale, typed-together, face-recipe) name the run's key, send it from Node and let their browsers in by the printed address, as a person would (`scripts/lib/serve-key.mjs`, which every harness browser is wrapped in); the served harness says a seat without the key, or with another run's, is refused, the desk harness that its stop asks for the printed address and opens the roster from it, and `pnpm smoke` that the installed `graview preview` refuses a request without the key. The graview-ship, graview-agent-seat, graview-host-page and graview-visitor-forms skills say how.
  
  A hosted page carries 0.5 KB more up front, minified, with or without a compiled app handed it (498 and 500 bytes, 178 gzipped): `openRemote` taking the printed address apart and carrying its key.
  
  Compatibility: the wire — a loopback `graview serve` or `graview preview` answers 401 on every route but health, and on the live wire, to a request without its run's key, and the store's CORS answer allows the `x-graview-key` header; `servePreview` and `serveStore` ask for no key unless handed one; `graview serve --trust-seat-headers` behind a trusted network, and a host with its own `seatOf`, are unchanged. Ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged.

## 0.1.59

### Patch Changes

- 85f0794: A dev server's doors answer only the page the person who started it opened. The studio, local, model, decision, files and views doors asked one thing of a caller, `sameOrigin`: no other site's `Origin` or `Sec-Fetch-Site`, and a Host that is this machine. A browser always says where a request came from, so that kept other sites' pages out; a program that is no browser says nothing, and to that check it looked like the page — another account on this machine, a container sharing its loopback, or anyone on the network when the server was started with `--host` (any IP address passed as this machine) could write TypeScript into `src/domain` through the studio door, run the operator's own `claude` through the local door, and spend `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` through the model and decision doors. Now every door also asks for a key (`fromThisApp` in `@graview/ship/dev`): kept in `~/.graview/door-key`, mode 0600, made the first time and taken again by the next server (`GRAVIEW_DOOR_KEY` sets one outright), said only in the terminal as the address to open (`Graview's doors: open http://localhost:5173/?graview_door=<key>`), and traded there once for an `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict` cookie, the address tidied (`doorLogin`, which every door's plugin installs through `openDoors`). The key is never written into anything the server serves. Without it a door answers 403 with where the key is; only the probe the page itself asks on load (a GET to the model, local and decision doors, from this server's own page) is answered 200 as closed, with how to open it (`DOOR_CLOSED`), so a page opened at the plain address says so in its seat and logs nothing. A door now takes only this machine's loopback as where its page is (`localhost`, `*.localhost`, `127.x.x.x`, `[::1]`, `loopbackHost`); an address on the network answers only when `GRAVIEW_DOOR_HOSTS` lists it. `mcpDoor` takes `admit`, asked of every request beside its origin — on a dev server, `fromThisApp`. Ship's new door test holds that a request with no Origin and no key is refused on a loopback and a network Host and writes no file, that the printed address lets the page in and the key appears in no response, the page's included, that a wrong key sets nothing, that a probe from the page without the key is told the door is closed while a POST, another site and a network Host are still refused, and that the key file is kept 0600 and read again; core's sync test that `mcpDoor` asks `admit`; the door tests send the cookie a page holds. `verify-files` opens its page from the address with the key, as a person would. The graview-desk and graview-sync skills say how.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; `sameOrigin` answers as it did, so `graview preview` and a served store are as they were. A dev door refuses a page not opened from the printed address, and one reached by a network address `GRAVIEW_DOOR_HOSTS` does not list.
- d7d58b8: A loopback store believes a seat only from its own page. `graview serve` on 127.0.0.1 believes the seat a request names in its headers, because only this machine reaches it — but a page on another origin in this machine's browser reaches it too, and every answer said `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, the live wire took its seat from the upgrade's query, and `x-graview-kind: system` was read as the system's seat, which passes every policy and every sight. A page anywhere the browser would let reach loopback (another local dev server, a name rebound to 127.0.0.1) read the whole store, records the policy hides included, and wrote to it as the system. Now the loopback `graview serve` keeps to its own origin and host, as `graview preview` does (`ownOrigin`), on HTTP and on the live wire, and lets in only the origins `--allow-origin` names (`ServeOptions.allowOrigins`), at a host that is this machine. A store that keeps to its own origin names no origin but an allowed one in its CORS answer, echoed with `Vary: Origin`, in place of `*`. And a header names the system's seat only where the host says so: `trustSystemSeat` on the handler, `seatFromHeaders(request, { system })`, which `--trust-seat-headers` turns on; anywhere else `x-graview-kind: system` is read as a person's seat. `graview serve --trust-seat-headers` behind a trusted network believes every header as it did. The rota harnesses and `pnpm serve:rota` name the rota dev server's origin. `a-loopback-store-believes-a-seat-only-from-its-own-page.test.ts` holds it.
  
  A program on this machine still reaches the loopback store as any seat but the system's: `graview mcp --remote-url` and `graview apply --remote-url` work as before, and `graview apply --template --remote-url` against a loopback `graview serve` runs as a person unless the server was started with `--trust-seat-headers`.
  
  Compatibility: the wire — a loopback `graview serve` refuses 403 a request whose page is on another origin unless `--allow-origin` names it, answers no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, and reads `x-graview-kind: system` as a person's seat without `--trust-seat-headers`; `createStoreHandler({ trustSeatHeaders: true })` reads it as a person's seat unless `trustSystemSeat` is said. A host with its own `seatOf` is unchanged. Ops and primitives, stored formats, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged.
- d1d4e11: Starting fresh is asked for in the tab that does it, never by a link from somewhere else. Every app `graview create` writes opens its store with `fresh: browserStartsFresh()`, which read `?fresh=1` in the address as asking — so any site, or a message, that sent a person to `https://<app>/?fresh=1` erased their records, their history and their undo with nothing to take back, and `forgetFreshParam` then tidied the flag away. Now the Start fresh controls (the workbench's and the routed face's, and the seedbed's "Load the example garden") keep a one-time value in this tab's `sessionStorage` and go to `?fresh=<that value>` (`freshAddress` and `FRESH_KEY` on `@graview/core`; `freshHref` in `@graview/ship/browser` makes the same address), and `browserStartsFresh` honors the address only when it carries the value the tab kept, forgetting it as it reads it. A `?fresh=1` from elsewhere, a guessed value, or the same address loaded twice discards nothing, and `forgetFreshParam` drops `fresh` from the address whatever it says. A driven browser is unchanged: it starts fresh unless it says `?remember=1`, and takes `?fresh=1` as asking, so every harness and `graview create`'s scaffold work as they did. Ship's browser-adapter test holds that a store opened under `?fresh=1`, `?utm=x&fresh=1&remember=1` or a value the tab did not keep still holds what a person made, and that the control's own address starts fresh once. The graview-ship and graview-seed skills say Start fresh rather than `?fresh=1`.
  
  The one-time value costs a hosted page 0.3 KB more up front (286 bytes minified, 189 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.3 KB (286, 117), under raises naming this changeset; the pages face alone 239 / 96, a visitor's form handed a compiled app 286 / 133, every face 175 / 123 and the guest host registering a worker view 4 gzipped, each under a raise of its own, and the scene before it draws 230 bytes more, under 1023 KB.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, the wire, the declaration and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. Stored formats — unchanged: what the browser adapter keeps is read and written as before; only what asks for it to be discarded changed. A host whose own control navigates to `?fresh=1` in a person's browser goes through `freshAddress` (or `freshHref`) now, or the address is not honored.
- b8624ae: The session the local door spawns reads only its own folder, takes its prompt as words, and is handed only the environment it needs. `localIntelligence` ran `claude -p <prompt> --tools Read --allowedTools Read` in a temporary folder with the dev server's whole environment but `CLAUDE*`: the working directory was the only bound on what Read could open, and the prompt — written from the graph, where other people's words are — could ask it to read `~/.ssh` or a checkout's `.env` and hand the contents back as a plan's arguments. Now Read is allowed only beneath the session's own folder (`--allowedTools "Read(./**)"`), the prompt comes last after `--` so words that begin with a dash are never a flag, and the environment is a list by name — `PATH`, `HOME`, the user, shell, locale and temporary folder, the proxies and certificates a network needs, and the CLI's own `ANTHROPIC_*` — so another service's key or a database's address the dev server holds never reaches a session a page's words steer. Ship's local-door test holds the argv, a prompt of flags kept after `--`, and an environment of dummy secrets reduced to what the CLI needs; no test runs the real CLI. The graview-desk skill says what the session is given.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. A host whose own `argv` builds on `argumentsFor` finds the prompt at the end now, after `--`.

## 0.1.58

### Patch Changes

- aa50c1d: A Notion database can be kept in step with an app, declared as a connection the way a Google calendar is (Roadmap f-notion-connection). `connections.<name>` with `"system": "notion"` names one `database` (its 32-character id, or `{ "env": "TASKS_DATABASE" }`) and one `kind`, and `properties` maps a field to a property by its name in Notion (`"stage": "Stage"`), or as `{ "name", "type" }` so the checker judges the type; without `properties`, each field finds the property of its name, ignoring case and punctuation, and the kind's name field is the database's title. A database that holds several data sources — Notion's tables gathered under one name since its 2025-09-03 API, which the transport speaks — says which with `source`, its name in Notion or its id; one of a single source needs none, and a connection that names none of several, or one the database does not hold, is refused with a sentence naming them (`the database Reindata holds 4 data sources — Reindeer Fleet, … — so the connection says which with source`). Auth is an internal integration's secret: `graview serve` and `graview preview` read it from `NOTION_TOKEN` (`<auth.env>_TOKEN`, or the `auth.file` the declaration names), and a host hands it through `runConnections`' `token` (the request carries the declaration, so a host can tell a Notion connection's from a Google one's) and the database's id through the new `database` seam. The secret is never written in the document, the op log, the sync state or an export, and `graview check` refuses one written anywhere in a connection (`connection-secret`).
  
  The transport is `notion({ databaseId, token, … })` on `@graview/core/sync`. A pull asks for the pages edited since the last `last_edited_time` it saw, oldest first, following `next_cursor` to the end, and reads the whole database when it has no cursor and every ten minutes, when a page it had linked and no longer finds — archived, in the trash, moved out — is gone here through `remove-<kind>`. A push makes a page for a record made here, sets the properties of one edited here, and archives the page of one removed here. Title and rich text, number, select and status (an `enum` field, its option matched to Notion's ignoring case and punctuation, so `in-progress` is "In progress"), multi-select (a `list`), date (a `date` field holds the day, a `datetime` field the instant in UTC), checkbox, URL, email and phone go both ways; people are read as their names and never written; formulas, rollups and files, and a relation no edge holds, are left alone and said once (`left alone in Notion: Total (formula: …)`). Words are written as plain text in Notion's 2000-character pieces, a pair of surrogates never split. A version is the page's `last_edited_time` with a hash of its values, so our own write coming back is an echo and somebody's edit in the same minute (Notion keeps the time to the minute) is still news; a field both sides changed is held as the engine holds any. Notion's 429 is waited out as its `Retry-After` says, up to a minute a pass; a refused secret is said by the name it is kept under ("Notion refused NOTION_TOKEN (HTTP 401) …"), and a database the integration cannot see as one to share with it. A real workspace is several tables joined by relations, and they are kept as edges (Roadmap f-notion-several-tables): an app declares one Notion connection per table, each to its own kind, and a key of `properties` may name one of the kind's edges, mapped to a relation property — `"reindeer": "Reindeer Fleet"`, or `{ "name": "Reindeer Fleet", "type": "relation" }` — whose target kind is another Notion connection's; without `properties`, an edge whose name a relation property has is kept when every kind it reaches is. A link made in Notion lands through `connect-<kind>-<edge>` and one taken away through `disconnect-<kind>-<edge>`, credited to Notion; one made or taken away here sets the page's relation, the whole list, and comes back as an echo. A relation's value is the Notion ids of the pages it reaches, so it is compared, echoed, held in conflict (under the connection's `conflicts`, the whole relation at once) and sent as a field is; Notion lists a relation's first 25 pages on a page, and the rest are read through the property's own endpoint. A page reached that no connection has linked yet — its table not read yet — is held in the sync state (`unresolved`) and linked once it is, with nothing sent, so the order the connections run in does not matter: they share one set of links (`sharedLinks()`, a `LinkResolver` over every engine), and `runConnections` gives every connection the same one and, after each pass, has each engine link what it held (`engine.resolveHeld()`) — so a host running `runConnections` gets it with nothing to do, and one making engines with `connection()` passes them one `links`. A relation of one keeps the first page Notion lists and says the rest once. A dual relation is kept on the side whose kind declares the edge; declaring both sides keeps both, and a link made on either side converges with nothing more sent. A read-only connection refuses a link made or taken away here on the edges it keeps, as it refuses an edit.
  
  A `SyncReport` now carries `problem`, what the remote said when it could not be reached, and a served store's line says it: `board: could not reach Notion (notion: …); will try again`.
  
  `scriptedNotion()` is a Notion in memory — retrieve, make and archive a database and the data sources it holds (`source(databaseId, …)` adds another), query a source, make, read, change and archive a page, relation properties to another source with a dual twin kept in step and a relation's pages past 25 read through the property item endpoint, with 401, 404, 400 and `limit(n)`'s 429 as Notion answers them — which core's and ship's tests run against: every property type, a round trip both ways, every page of a 205-page query, our write not echoed, a conflict held, an archive each way, the rate limit, a refused secret, an unshared database, a database of two sources read through the one named and refused when none is, long words, a whole read that loses and duplicates nothing, and two tables joined by a dual relation — a link made in Notion arriving as an edge, one made here written to Notion, a page read later linked, both sides converging with nothing sent, a relation of one, a relation both sides changed held, 30 pages of one relation followed, a read-only connection refusing a link, and two connections linked in one `runConnections` pass whichever runs first. `scripts/verify-notion.mjs` runs the same claims against a real workspace (credentials in `~/.graview-notion-test.env`, never printed; not in `pnpm verify` or CI), making its own database inside a page shared with the integration, adding a second data source to it, making two more tables joined by a dual relation and keeping links both ways, and trashing all three at the end — 28 claims, all held against a live workspace — and `--stub` runs them against the scripted Notion. The graview-sync skill and the core and ship READMEs say how to declare one. The transport and the scripted Notion stay on `@graview/core/sync`, which no page reaches; the checker's judgment of a Notion connection rides with the studio, so every face carries 5,635 bytes more minified (1,686 gzipped), under a raise of its budget (`scripts/budget-raises/a-notion-database-is-a-connection-every-face.json`), and judging a relation kept as an edge 2,449 bytes more (690 gzipped), under a second (`scripts/budget-raises/notion-relations-are-kept-as-edges-every-face.json`); a hosted page's first load stays within its budget.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, the wire and derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. Stored formats — additive: a `SyncState` is format 1 as before and may carry `unresolved`, the relations' pages not linked yet, which a build that predates it drops and reads again on the page's next change; a Notion connection's `cursor` is its own JSON inside it. A `ResourceMapping` may say `edges` (our edge → their name), and `SyncOptions` gains `links`, `connectInbound` and `disconnectInbound`. The declaration and check finding codes — additive: `connections.<name>` may say `"system": "notion"`, which a build that predates it refuses by path, a Notion connection may say `source`, a key of a Notion connection's `properties` may name one of the kind's edges, and a property's `type` may be `relation`, and the checker gains `connection-properties`, `connection-relation`, `connection-relation-target` and `connection-connect` (errors), `connection-read-only` and `connection-disconnect` (warnings) and `connection-secret` (error, said only of a connection that holds a secret). `ConnectionRun.calendarId` is optional now, beside `databaseId`. What Graview Cloud does: keeps each workspace's Notion integration secret and the database's id with its other credentials and hands them to `runConnections` through `token` (answering by `request.declaration.system`) and `database`; says `report.problem` where it says a connection could not reach its calendar; offers `system: "notion"` only on a build that has it; and, for several linked tables, runs every Notion connection of an app through one `runConnections` (or one `sharedLinks()`), so a relation finds the record another connection keeps.
- 039af05: A Notion connection lands each change through an act that fits it, found by an app that declared more than the connection needed (North Pole). A page made in Notion becomes a record through an act that creates the kind, takes no subject and requires nothing but fields the connection maps — `add-<kind>` when it fits, else the first declared act that does (`creatingAct(app, kind, mapped)`, the same choice the checker makes). It took `add-<kind>` or else the first act that created the kind, and North Pole's first was `log-health-check`, which logs a check on a reindeer: every page from Notion was refused ("names a record that is not there") and `graview check` said nothing. Now, when every act that makes the kind takes a subject or asks for a field the connection leaves out, `graview check` says so at `connections.<name>.kind` (`connection-creates`: "a page made in the database becomes a health check through an act that makes one from the page's fields alone, and log-health-check acts on a reindeer"), naming what to declare. A Google calendar's connection chooses the same way.
  
  A change in Notion to a field another act writes lands too. It lands through the derived `edit-<kind>`, which covered only the fields no declared act writes, so a toy whose status `set-toy-status` moves and whose count `add-made` adds to could not be kept in step, and the checker refused the connection (`connection-edit`) until the app declared its own `edit-toy` writing every field. A kind's fields a declared connection maps are now kept in step (`keptInStep` on its definition, which the compile writes from `connections`), and its derived edit writes them whoever else does. Inbound changes keep one write path — a named act, judged by the policy and the invariants, logged and undoable, credited to Notion — rather than a second, system-only one that would bypass both; and a field Notion can set to anything is not one only a named step changes, so the record's own edit says so. A kind without a connection derives the edit it did. `connection-edit` is now said of an app's own `edit-<kind>` that leaves a mapped field out, and of a mapped field declared `fixed`.
  
  A page that cannot become a record is said once. A blank row (no name, no date) was refused on every pass, the serve log repeating it every 30 seconds; the engine now keeps it in the sync state as skipped (`SyncState.skipped`, by the page's id, at the version refused), says nothing more of it, and makes it again only once its version changes in Notion — once it is filled in, it arrives.
  
  `graview create --template` writes `tests/domain.test.ts` with the `checkApp` and `instantiateTemplate` it calls imported from `@graview/core/check`; `src/domain/app.ts` imported them instead and used neither. A scaffold test now compiles every TypeScript file a project starts with and fails on a name used and not imported, or imported and not used, with a template and without.
  
  `scripts/verify-notion.mjs` holds four claims more against a workspace — the checker passing a connection whose fields another act writes, a page landing through the act that fits it, a status changed in Notion landing through `edit-<kind>`, a blank row said once and arriving once filled — 32 claims, all held live. The graview-sync skill says what a change lands through. The compile and the checker a page that compiles its document carries cost a hosted page 1.3 KB more up front (1,281 bytes minified, 406 gzipped), and a page handed a compiled app 0.2 KB (182, 38), under raises naming this changeset; every face 715 bytes more gzipped, the pages face alone 118 / 14 and a visitor's form handed a compiled app 182 / 66, each under a raise of its own. Cloud's worker grows 5,092 bytes, past the 562 its budget had left: its bytes are now raised by a file like the bundles' (`"budget": "Cloud's worker"`, `bytes`), never by an edit of the base, and this is the first.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives and the wire — unchanged. Stored formats — additive: a `SyncState` is format 1 as before and may carry `skipped`, which a build that predates it drops, saying a blank page once more. The declaration and check finding codes — no new code: `connection-creates` is also said when every act that makes the kind takes a subject or requires a field the connection does not map, and `connection-edit` no longer of a mapped field another act writes. A compiled kind's plan may carry `keptInStep`. Derived tool names and input schemas — a kind kept by a connection has an `edit-<kind>` that takes every mapped field, where it took only those no other act writes; a kind without one is unchanged. `creatingAct` on `@graview/core/sync` takes the fields the connection maps. What Graview Cloud does: nothing for the acts; a host that keeps a connection's `SyncState` keeps `skipped` with it.

## 0.1.57

### Patch Changes

- 6e51c72: A computed value is said as its format, can be rounded, and can be kept off the record's page while it still ranks a list (FR-217), as Roadmap f-computed-format asked. A season's `pct = wins / (wins + losses + ties)` read "0.8235294117647058" under Details: `set-format` refused it ("season has no field pct"), the rule language had no `round()`, and a share worked out only to rank seasons could not be kept off the page a lens sorted by it. Now a kind's computed field takes `format` (`"money"`, `"percent"` or `"duration"`) and a `unit`, as a number field does — `{ "expr": "wins / (wins + losses + ties)", "format": "percent" }` — and the record's page, a card, a row of a list's CSV and a view spec's field block say it so ("82%", "$785,714.29", in the brand's currency and locale); a declaration in code says the same on `defineNode({ computed })`. `set-format` on a computed field sets or clears them (`season's pct is worked out as before and said as a percent, kept as a fraction — 0.15 reads 15%.`), refuses `"markdown"`, and keeps the field short as its bare expression once nothing else is left. `round(x)` and `round(x, places)` round half away from zero, nothing staying nothing, and the checker, the rule's line in words ("… rounded to 3 places") and the rule language's own function list know it. A computed field with `hidden: true` — `set-computed { "hidden": true }`, `false` or `null` to show it again — is left out of what a record says of itself (its page, its card, its CSV columns) and is still worked out for a list's or a lens's sort, a rule, a template and a view. Core's test formats a computed share as a percent and a ratio as money on the page, in the CSV and in a field block, rounds one, and hides one from the page and the CSV while it still sorts the list. The graview-view-specs and graview-invariant skills say it. A hosted page carries 0.7 KB more up front, with or without a compiled app handed it, for this and a date emptied in place together (`scripts/budget-raises/computed-format-and-clear-in-place-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes: additive — a computed field's long form takes `format`, `unit` and `hidden`, `set-computed` takes `hidden`, `set-format` takes a computed field's name, and the rule language knows `round`; a build that predates it refuses the new keys and reports `round` as an unknown function. The wire: unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-217. What Graview Cloud does: lets propose_change's `set-format` name a computed field and `set-computed` carry `hidden`, and mentions `round(x, places)` and a computed field's `format` where it teaches the rule language to a model.
- 67e4b0d: A text block says how many lines a card or a row shows of it (FR-218), Roadmap f-card-text-clamp. A card cuts a text block at three lines and a row at one, so `{"text": "{awards}", "tone": "accent"}` on a footballer's card stopped at the third award at 390 pixels and nothing showed the rest in place. A text block may now say `"lines": 6` — that many lines before the text is cut short — or `"lines": 0` to show it whole, on whichever slot draws it; said nowhere, the card keeps its three and the row its one. `graview check` refuses any other value at the block's path (`view-lines`: `lines is 13, and a text block's lines is a whole number from 1 to 12, or 0 to show it whole`), refuses `lines` on any block but text (`unknown-key`), and no longer warns `view-whole-text` of a long text a block measures on purpose. `@graview/core/document.schema.json` says it. The graview-view-specs skill says so. A hosted page carries 0.4 KB more up front (409 bytes minified, 169 gzipped), the pages face alone 555 more (174) and every face 952 (375), each under a raise of its budget (`scripts/budget-raises/a-text-block-says-how-many-lines-it-shows-*.json`), and the scene before it draws 955 more, under 1009 KB. Primitives' test draws a card's text with `lines: 6` and with `lines: 0` and finds each said on the element, and the default when it says nothing, and the check takes 0 to 12 and refuses -1, 2.5, 13, "6", true and null. A "more" control that opens the rest in place is not part of this: a card is a link to its record, whose page says the text whole.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes — additive: a text block's `lines`, and the finding `view-lines`; a build that predates it refuses `lines` as a key a text block does not say. The wire — additive: `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-218. What Graview Cloud does: lets an agent's view spec say `lines` on a text block once `capabilities().shipped` has FR-218.
- dda9073: A view of many may be its kind's list page (FR-203). In Graview Cloud an agent designed a view of a team's players and could not make it the players' list: a view of many was drawn at its own `/places/<slug>` when titled and nowhere when not, and `/players` always kept the app's own list. A worker view whose manifest says `replaces: "page"` on `cardinality: "many"` is now drawn on its kind's list page under the list's heading in place of the list — no arranging line, no rows, no forms: what is done there is what the view offers — as a view of one that says so is a record's page (FR-149); a titled one is its own place as well, which then offers no "All players as a list" back to itself. Should it fail, the kind's own face is drawn in its place. The list page reads whichever view the kind draws at many × full as the host's tidying leaves the registry, so a view the host deleted or hid stands in for nothing and the list is the app's own again; the host decides who may set the key, as for a view of one. `checkManifest` accepts the key on a view of many and refuses it only on the home's view, and `describePlace` says a list page so replaced as its heading and the view (`drawnBy: "view:<name>"`), with no form. The graview-worker-view skill and the guest guide say so. Pages' test opens `/players` with a view of many that replaces the page and finds the view and no rows, and without the key finds the derived list; guest's registers a real worker view each way and opens `/deliverables`; core's describes both. The pages face alone carries 840 bytes more minified (170 gzipped) and every face 843 (191), each under a raise of its budget (`scripts/budget-raises/a-view-of-many-may-be-its-kinds-list-page-*.json`); a hosted page's first load is unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-203.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives and stored formats — unchanged. The declaration — unchanged; `describePlace` says a kind's list page a host's view of many replaces as that view. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The wire — additive: a worker view's manifest may say `replaces: "page"` on a view of many, which an earlier build refuses; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-203. What Graview Cloud does: lets an agent's list view say `replaces: "page"` once `capabilities().shipped` has FR-203, gated as it gates a view of one's.
- 3e9d537: The graview-worker-view skill says to draw a choice by its label (Roadmap f-skill-choices). A record's enum field holds its option's id, and 0.1.56 hands a view `props.choices` — kind → field → option → label — to say it with, but the skill never said so, so views drew `state-sponsored`. It now says `props.choices.actor.motive[node.motive]`, and in a few words what this release lets a view do: choose the kinds or fields it is handed pictures of (`["circuit"]`, `{ circuit: "photo" }`) within the 12 MB they share, name a `<section>` by its own heading with `aria-labelledby`, and style a disclosure's triangle with `summary::marker` and `::-webkit-details-marker`. graview-view-drawing no longer says a section is never named. Both stay under the skills' 11,000-character limit, the worker view's by tightening its gestures, limits and examples paragraphs.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged for a host and an app — the skills' guidance only; a view written to it uses what 0.1.56 and this release already hand it.

## 0.1.56

### Patch Changes

- 3149e79: The shade a ground refusal names is kept when it is sent back (Roadmap f-ground-suggestion-passes). On 0.1.54, `set-brand { ground: { light: "#f4efe6", dark: "#9a9a94" } }` was refused with "#343431 would pass", and #343431 was then refused too, for faint ink on the cards at 3.6–4.2:1: the shade had been judged against ink, muted ink and the accent alone, while the compiled palette (`checkBrandContrast`) holds every pair. A ground is now judged on every pair the check holds a palette to — ink, muted and faint ink, the accent, the status colors and the lit edge, on the ground, the cards, the receded and flagged cards, the floating sheet and the bar, in both schemes — and the shade a refusal names is searched on that judgment with the app's accent kept as set-brand keeps it, so sending it back is kept and its palette passes the check. The refusal names the pair that fails ("In the dark scheme, faint ink #97a0b6 on a card (#…) on the ground #343431 is 3.6:1; 4.5:1 is needed — #… would pass."). A ground's lit edge is drawn at the alpha that clears 3:1 on its cards, as an accent's always was, so an edge is never the reason a ground is refused. A core test sends back every shade named over a spread of light and dark grounds, with and without an accent, and holds each kept with a palette `checkBrandContrast` passes. A hosted page carries 0.8 KB more up front (824 bytes minified, 236 gzipped; 244 handed a compiled app), with f-dark-brand-accent's dark page beside it: the compile draws the brand on every page.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes — `set-brand` and `brand-ground` refuse a ground on which faint ink, a status color or the lit edge does not read, where they judged ink, muted ink and the accent alone; a ground that passed before and whose compiled palette `checkBrandContrast` passed is still kept. Ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged.
- d97578e: A worker view is handed its records' computed values (Roadmap f-computed-in-views). A kind's computed values showed on the built-in pages and were missing from the records a custom view was handed, so a view counted again from the edges what its page already said. Now every record a view is handed — its own record, the members it is drawn for and the records it reads — carries each computed value beside its fields, by name (`props.node.squad`), always, with nothing to ask for in the manifest: a number, text or a yes as itself, a record as `{ id, kind, label }`, a set as a list of them; one that cannot be worked out is left off, as a field with no value is. They are worked out over the viewer's graph, as every page works them out, so a field the viewer's sight hides and a record it may not see add nothing to one: a parent who may not see a player's phone is handed "no number", and a team's count leaves out the prospects they may not see. The evaluator is a chunk of its own, fetched before a view whose kinds have computed values starts (`readsComputed` on `@graview/guest/host/views`), and a view run headless is handed them too. The guest host carries about 3.1 KB more before a worker is drawn (1.4 KB gzipped), and as much registering and drawing one, since the modules that chunk shares with what loads first keep what it reads, under raises of those budgets (`scripts/budget-raises/a-view-is-handed-computed-values-*.json`). Guest's test hands a view a team and its players as a coach and as a parent and holds the parent's computed values to what the parent may see, with no hidden phone or record anywhere in what the view is handed, headless or not. The graview-worker-view skill says a record carries its computed values.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. A view's records carry more keys: a computed value's name, which no field of the kind may share. Additive on `@graview/guest/host/views` (`readsComputed`, `ComputedOn`; `WorkerViewPropsInput.computed`). What Graview Cloud does: nothing in its code; a view can read `props.node.<computed>` once it builds on this.
- 3149e79: An accent is judged on the ground it is drawn on, and a black brand keeps its gold (Roadmap f-dark-brand-accent). `set-brand` refused a black brand with the Steelers' gold `#FFB612`, so the app took a darker `#916a00` it never chose: the accent was judged on Graview's paper whatever ground the edit or the app set. Now a light scheme given a dark ground is drawn as the dark page it is, in the dark scheme's inks, and `set-brand` judges an accent on the ground it is actually drawn on — this edit's, or the app's — so `{ "accent": "#FFB612", "ground": "#000000" }` is kept as given, and gold set later on an app that already stands on black is kept too. Where the pair fails on paper, the refusal answers with a pair that passes as well as with the darker shade: `{"op": "set-brand", "accent": "#976800"}, or keep #FFB612 on a dark page: {"op": "set-brand", "accent": "#FFB612", "ground": {"light": "#0a0f1f", "dark": "#0a0f1f"}}`, and `graview check` says the same (`brand-accent`); both, sent back, are kept. A shade suggested for an accent sent with a ground carries that ground. Where an accent's hue is the warning color's, the warning drawn in its place is the bad status of the scheme it is drawn in, so a dark page's warning reads. The graview-brand skill says so.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes — `set-brand` keeps an accent that reads on the app's own ground where it judged it on Graview's paper, and a `brand-accent` or `set-brand` fix may name a dark page as well as a shade; a document whose light ground is dark compiles to a dark page in the light scheme (its ink, cards and status colors the dark scheme's). Ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged.

## 0.1.55

### Patch Changes

- 3648ead: An app says who it is for and what they come to do (Roadmap f-app-jobs). Nothing in a declaration said whom it served or what they come to it to find out, so every look was assembled field by field from a type table and a model was told the kinds and the acts and nothing about the people. A document takes `purpose` now, and `defineApp` the same shape: `for`, a sentence ("The couple and their planner, mostly on phones"), and up to twelve `jobs`, each a question in the people's words (`ask`) answered by the records of one `kind` a rule-language condition holds for (`where`, the record as its subject, read in a TypeScript app exactly as in a document), in an order (`sort`, a field, a computed field or `label`, or `{ by, direction }`), with the act people usually take next on one (`act`), for some `roles` or everybody. `graview check` holds each to the declaration, in a document at `purpose.jobs.<n>.<key>` and in a TypeScript app at `defineApp(…).purpose.jobs[<n>]`: a kind the app does not have (`job-kind`), a `where` that does not read or names what the kind does not have (`job-where`), an order by nothing or by a list or a picture (`job-sort`), an act the app does not have or one that acts on another kind (`job-act`, `job-act-kind`), a role it does not know (`job-role`), a name used twice (`job-name-taken`) and more than twelve (`jobs-too-many`). A page reads `purpose` loosely, as it reads `connections`; a hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (81 bytes, 30 gzipped; handed a compiled app 135 and 66, and a visitor's form handed one 68 and 29), under raises measured beside the friction log's (`scripts/budget-raises/the-jobs-with-the-friction-log-*.json`); its shape is the checker's (`PurposeSpec`) and the document's JSON schema says it to an editor. Four edits change it — `set-purpose`, `add-job`, `edit-job`, `remove-job` — each judged as the checker judges and said ("A new job, "What's cheap?": answered by the vendors where cost < 500."), and the edits that rename or remove carry jobs along: a renamed kind, field, relation or act is renamed in a job's kind, `where`, order and act; a removed kind takes its jobs, a removed field or relation takes the jobs whose `where` reads it and the order by it, and a removed act leaves the job asking without it. `@graview/core/jobs` is a new entry: `jobsOf(app)` (each order with its direction, the kind's plural beside it), `jobsFor(jobs, principal)` (a seat's roles) and `answerJob(store, job, principal)`, which answers `{ count, ids }` read through `store.seenBy(principal)`, so a record kept from the seat is never counted or named and an archived one is set aside. `graview describe` opens with "Who it is for" (the sentence and each job's ask, only a seat's own under `--as`), and `aiContextOf` takes the app's `purpose` and tells a model `for` and the jobs whose roles include the viewer's and whose kind they may see (`state.purpose`); the inline AI hands it the app's (`InlineAsk.purpose`). The studio carries an app's purpose through, a renamed kind's jobs following it; `toDocument` writes it, leaving out a next act the document could not carry. The todo example says it is for a household and asks "What's late?" and "What still needs a slot this week?". The graview-new-app skill says it. Core's tests hold that a purpose is read from a document and from `defineApp` alike and given back, that each code is said at its path, that a job is answered in its order held to the seat's sight, and that every edit carries jobs along or lets go of what a removal takes.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The declaration — additive: `purpose` on a document and `GraviewApp`, and `set-purpose`, `add-job`, `edit-job` and `remove-job` among the edits. Check finding codes — additive: `job-kind`, `job-where`, `job-sort`, `job-act`, `job-act-kind`, `job-role`, `job-name-taken`, `jobs-too-many`. The JavaScript API — additive: `AppPurpose`, `JobDeclaration` and `JobSort` on `@graview/core`; the `@graview/core/jobs` entry (`jobsOf`, `jobsFor`, `answerJob`, `jobIsFor`, `MOST_JOBS`); an optional `purpose` on `AiContextOptions` and `InlineAsk`, and `purpose` on `AiContextState`; a "Who it is for" section at the head of `describeApp`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; `describe_app` says an app's purpose through `describeApp` as it is, a seat's model is told it once Cloud hands `aiContextOf` the app's `purpose`, and `propose_change` takes the four new edits through `editDocument`.
- a2c056b: Apps improve from use: the app's own actor suggests changes to the app itself, accepted or declined like any suggestion (Roadmap f-learned-suggestions). The actor suggested changes to records, and the friction log kept what people could not do, but nothing turned the one into the other. `@graview/core/learn` is a new entry: `learnFromUse({ document, friction, log, records, declined, opens, minPeople })` reads and writes nothing and answers `AppSuggestion[]`, each `{ key, edits, said, why, evidence: { people, times } }`, offered only when at least `minPeople` (3, never fewer than 2) distinct people show it — an agent counted as the person it acts for, the app's own actor and the system as nobody. Six judges: another word for a kind or a field (words several people searched with and found nothing that nothing in the app is called, aimed at the field people changed next, within ten minutes, or the kind they changed or searched in: `add-alias`); a choice field (a text field whose values across six or more records are two to eight values each written twice, by enough people: `retype-field` to a choice of them); a date field (three in five of a text field's values carry a date in words: `add-field` of a date beside it, and nothing is moved); one act for a sequence (two acts one person takes within five minutes on one record, composed into one `add-act` when both are document acts that only set fields of the same kind and share none, the second with no condition of its own — otherwise said in `learningFrom(…).skipped` with why: an act that is code or derived, one that creates or links, two that set the same field); a clearer description for an act agents call wrongly (`tool-invalid` events with the form that worked: `add-act` with `replace` and the form said in its description, for a document act only); and a place nobody opens, from counts a host passes as `opens` (the framework sees no place open): `set-lens-hidden`, so it is still reached by its address. `key` is the same on every run, so `declined` — keys, or the decisions `declineAppSuggestion` makes — keeps a suggestion from being offered again however the evidence grows. `acceptAppSuggestion(document, suggestion)` applies its edits through `editDocument` as one change and answers the document, what the editor said, the fills, the lineage `{ basis: why, suggestion: key, actor: "learns from use" }` and the decision to keep; an edit that no longer applies is refused with the editor's findings. `memoryLearned` keeps decisions in memory, one per key.
  
  To keep another word, a kind and a field take `aliases` (up to twelve, each up to three lower-case words): in a document `kinds.<kind>.aliases` and `kinds.<kind>.fields.<field>.aliases`, in `defineNode` `aliases` and `fieldAliases: { <field>: [...] }`. Search finds a kind by its alias; `aiContextOf`'s schema digest says a kind's `aliases` and its fields' `fieldAliases` (a field the seat may not read is not named); `graview describe` says them under "Other words people use"; the friction digest no longer counts an alias as an unknown word; `toDocument` writes them. Two edits change them — `add-alias { kind, field?, alias }` and `set-aliases { kind, field?, aliases | null }` — and the checker refuses an alias another kind is called (its id, noun, plural or alias), one another field of the same kind is called (its name, label or alias), one said twice, and in TypeScript a `fieldAliases` naming a field the kind does not have: `alias-taken` and `alias-field`, errors, in a document at `kinds.<kind>[.fields.<field>].aliases.<n>` and in TypeScript at `defineNode("<kind>").aliases[<n>]`.
  
  Declines live beside the store, not in the op log: the log holds changes to the records, and its decline is an undo of a suggested call, while a declaration suggestion changes nothing to undo and its acceptance is the document's next version, kept wherever the host keeps those. `graview serve`'s directory keeps `learned.json` beside `friction.json` (`fileLearned`, `learnedFileOf` on `@graview/ship`); Graview Cloud keeps its own beside an app's document versions and passes them as `declined`. `graview learn <entry> [--data | --sqlite | --remote-url] [--friction] [--opens] [--min-people] [--json]` (in `@graview/tools/cli`, dispatched by `graview`) reads the store's log and records, `friction.json` and `learned.json`, and prints each suggestion with its edits and key, and what it saw and could not suggest; `--decline <key>` keeps the answer, and `--accept <key>` applies a document entry's edits through the editor, checks the app it makes and writes the file (a TypeScript app is told to make them in its source). `graview mcp` on a store on disk lists `list_app_suggestions`, a read, for a runtime handed `learned` (`ToolRuntimeOptions.learned`, `toolDefinitions(…, { learned: true })`); a seat's context never carries them. The ship README says what the app learns from the friction log and where its answers are kept, and the graview-tidying skill teaches it.
  
  Core's tests hold that one person searching a word a hundred times suggests nothing, that a declined alias is never suggested again, that learning changes nothing and accepting applies one change with its lineage, that a choice one person wrote and values that do not repeat suggest nothing, that dates are never moved, that a sequence the act language cannot compose is skipped and said, that a place is hidden only from counts while another is opened, that a tool's description is suggested only for a document act, and that aliases are read, said, edited, checked and written back alike in a document and in `defineNode`; ship's keeps a decline in `learned.json` across processes; tools' runs `graview learn` to print, decline and accept, and lists the seat's read only when handed it. A hosted page carries 0.3 KB more up front (342 bytes, 119 gzipped; handed a compiled app 0.1 KB, 146 and 50) — the document shape's `aliases` and a compiled kind's — under raises filed for them (`scripts/budget-raises/apps-improve-from-use-*.json`), as do the pages face alone (53, 33; search finding a kind by its alias) and a visitor's form handed a compiled app (146, 46).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, the wire — unchanged. The declaration — additive: `aliases` on a document kind and field and on `defineNode` (with `fieldAliases`), and `add-alias` and `set-aliases` among the edits. Check finding codes — additive: `alias-taken`, `alias-field`. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged; a runtime handed `learned` lists one more read tool, `list_app_suggestions`, and no other surface moves. Stored formats: `learned.json` beside a store on disk, outside the op log and every export. The JavaScript API — additive: the `@graview/core/learn` entry (`learnFromUse`, `learningFrom`, `acceptAppSuggestion`, `declineAppSuggestion`, `memoryLearned`, `keptDecisions`, `LEARNING_ACTOR` and their types); `aliases` and `fieldAliases` on `NodeDefinitionSpec` and `SchemaDigestKind`; `fileLearned` and `learnedFileOf` on `@graview/ship`; `learn` and `LEARN_USAGE` on `@graview/tools/cli`; `ToolRuntimeOptions.learned` and `ToolDefinitionsOptions.learned`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; `propose_change` takes `add-alias` and `set-aliases` through `editDocument`; it can call `learnFromUse` with an app's friction, log and records, its own decisions as `declined` and its Analytics' place opens as `opens`, show each as a suggestion to accept (through `acceptAppSuggestion`, kept as a document version with the lineage) or decline, and hand its seat's MCP the read tool through `learned`.
- b73b543: The home opens on the jobs, and one design is composed from the declaration rather than written into it (Roadmap f-design-composer). An app looked generic until somebody told a model, several times over, to make it usable and beautiful, and a host's answer was a table from a field's type to a lens that only ever added. An app that says what its people come to do (`purpose.jobs`) and draws no home of its own now opens on it: the first job with records waiting is the page's title, with how many answer it as the seat sees them, its first five records and the job's act on each (the record page's own act and form); the other jobs follow smaller, one with nothing waiting is a single quiet line, and the gallery of places comes after them, smaller. The rest of a job is the kind's list: an address narrows a list by a field's value, not by a job's rule, so the link says "All tasks". An app with no jobs keeps its home exactly. The embed hands its pages the app, so a hosted document's home answers its jobs too. `@graview/core/compose` proposes the rest as one change: `inferJobs(document | app, { records? })` reads at most three jobs off a declaration, conservatively — a choice that ends and the date a record is due by ("Which vendors are due within a week and not yet booked?"), a relation of one that a quarter or more of the records lack ("Which features have no area?"), a status whose first value a record waits at ("Which requests are still open?") — never drawn until a person declares them; `composeDesign(document | app, { records? })` answers `{ edits, said }`: those jobs where the app says none, the critic's own edits asked again until it has none left, and an accent from a fixed palette by the app's name where the brand has none. Applied, every document in this repository and its fixtures compiles and the critic warns of nothing. Said of the Graview Roadmap app: two jobs ("Which releases are still in draft?", "Which requests are still open?"), the four "Stage: X" places removed, five slices kept off the bar, and the open requests listed on its home. Said of todo: nothing, its jobs declared and its brand its own. `design-job-unanswered` is now said only of a declared home that lists none of a job's kind, and carries the `set-home` that answers it; the graview-pages skill says how the home opens. A hosted page carries nothing more up front, the jobs home being fetched with the pages; the pages face alone carries 5.9 KB more (6,084 bytes, 1,898 gzipped), the jobs answered as the home first draws (`scripts/budget-raises/the-home-opens-on-the-jobs-pages-face.json`).
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes — `design-job-unanswered` is no longer said of an app with no declared home (the derived home answers its jobs), and now carries a `set-home` edit; `@graview/core/compose` is new (`composeDesign`, `inferJobs`, `accentFor`, `ACCENT_PALETTE`, `MOST_INFERRED_JOBS`), its edits held to `editDocument` and the critic (`docs/stability.md` §4). The look: the derived home of an app with jobs leads with them (`[data-testid="jobs"]`, `job`, `job-row`, `job-act`, `job-quiet`), its standing a line after them, and the gallery under the jobs is smaller (`[data-testid="gallery"][data-small]`). Ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged.

## 0.1.54

### Patch Changes

- 094255d: An app stands on a ground of its own. Apps on Graview Cloud that each chose an accent still looked alike, every one on the same off-white, because the brand could set the accent, the scheme, the faces, the shape, a hue per kind and the logo, but not the page background. A document's brand takes `ground` now: one color, the light scheme's (`"#eef3f8"`), or one for each scheme (`{ "light": "#f4efe6", "dark": "#1a1612" }`), and `set-brand` sets it, `null` going back to Graview's ("The app's page background becomes #eef3f8."). What stands on the ground is drawn from it — the panel a card is, its muted fill, the edge, the bar and a floating sheet — so a card still stands off it; a light ground alone gives the dark scheme its hue taken down to a ground (`darkGroundFor`), never the light color itself. Views read it through the tokens they always did (`--graview-ground`, `--graview-panel`, `--graview-panel-muted`, `--graview-edge`, `--graview-float`). A ground is judged as an accent is (FR-126): one on which ink, muted ink or the accent drops below 4.5:1, on the ground or on a card, in either scheme, is refused by `set-brand` and warned of by `graview check` (`brand-ground`), with the pair, its ratio and the nearest shade of that ground that passes — "Muted ink #586174 on the ground #9a9a94 is 2.1:1; 4.5:1 is needed — #dcdcda would pass." — and a page keeps Graview's ground rather than draw one nobody can read on. The accent is judged on the app's ground: `accentProblem(accent, ground)` and `documentSchemes(accent, ground)`, and a ground that would leave the app's accent unread is refused with the accent's shade that would read. A TypeScript brand builds on one with `brandFromAccent({ accent, base: groundSchemes("#eef3f8") })`, and its contrast is measured as any palette's. A hosted page carries 2.2 KB more up front, and one handed a compiled app 2.0 KB more, under raises of their budgets (`scripts/budget-raises/an-app-stands-on-a-ground-of-its-own-*.json`), and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 1003 KB. The graview-brand skill says it. Core's tests hold that the ground is kept as given and drawn from, that the dark ground is the light one's hue and not the light one, that ink, muted ink and the accent read on the ground and on a card in both schemes, that `null` gives back Graview's schemes, and that a ground refused names the pair, the ratio and a shade that is then taken; `pnpm verify brand` holds that a ground of the app's own is drawn on both faces in both schemes, the derived one in the dark, with a card apart from it (`theAppsOwnGroundIsDrawnInBothSchemes`).
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, derived tool names and input schemas, the theme's tokens and the embed — unchanged. The declaration — additive: `brand.ground` on a document and `ground` on `set-brand`. Check finding codes — additive: `brand-ground`. The JavaScript API — additive: `groundSchemes`, `darkGroundFor`, `groundProblem`, `groundReads`, `Ground` and `GroundRefusal` on `@graview/core`, and an optional `ground` on `accentProblem` and `documentSchemes`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; its settings picker offers a page background beside the accent and sends `set-brand` `ground`, showing the refusal's sentence and offering its shade.
- 094255d: What was done while the reader was away leaves out what they said "not now" to, and may come after the first draw (FR-212). `whileAway(store, since, principal)` from `@graview/tools/tidy` counted in `waiting` the suggestions the reader had put out of sight, so its line said more waited than the tray showed; it takes `{ dismissed }` now, as `suggestions()` does (`whileAway(store, { seq }, principal, { dismissed })`), and a "not now" whose time has passed hides nothing. And `whileAway` was a `mount` option read once, so a host that works it out from the store it opens had to hold the page back to say one line on the tray: `EmbedHandle.setWhileAway(away)` and `PagesEmbedHandle.setWhileAway(away)` hand it over after the first draw, the Suggested tray saying it from the next render and nothing again for `undefined`; a React host's changed `whileAway` prop on `<Embed>` already reached the tray, and a test holds it now. The graview-tidying skill says both. Tools' tests hold that `waiting` and the line leave out a dismissed suggestion and keep one whose "not now" has passed; embed's hold that the tray says what the handle hands it after the first draw, on the whole embed and on the pages alone, and nothing once it is taken away, and that a changed prop reaches it without a remount.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas, and the theme — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: a fourth `options` argument on `whileAway`. The embed — additive: `setWhileAway` on `EmbedHandle` and `PagesEmbedHandle`. What Graview Cloud does: mounts without waiting for `whileAway`, calls `handle.setWhileAway(whileAway(store, { seq }, principal, { dismissed }))` once it has the store and the reader's dismissals, and again when either changes. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-212.

## 0.1.53

### Patch Changes

- e30703c: A view that draws its own heading is not titled twice. Book Manuscript's mind map, on Graview Cloud at 390 pixels: its place said "Mind map", "1 purpose" and "All purposes as a list", and then the view said "Mind map" again as its own heading — a quarter of a phone's screen before the view (Roadmap f-place-header-repeats-view-title). A worker view's manifest may now say `heading: "own"`: its place on Pages keeps its title for a screen reader and the keyboard (`PageTitle` takes `unseen`), draws neither it nor the count, and the way to the kind's list stays. A manifest that says it on a view that is not a place, or says anything but `"own"`, is refused with why. The graview-pages skill says so. `pnpm verify address` asks, at 390 and 1440, that a custom page view's heading is said once while any other place keeps its title and count.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `WorkerViewManifest.heading` (`"own"`), and `PageTitle`'s `unseen`.
