What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; lets a view's manifest carry `heading: "own"` through propose_view and the views guide, and a view that draws its own heading says it.
- e30703c: A view's place fits a phone. On Graview Cloud at 390 pixels, a view with something wider than the screen in it — an SVG 640 wide in a box that scrolls sideways, a row of tabs — widened the page: the region a view draws in (`[data-worker-view-place]`) was a grid item at its content's min-content width, so the home grew to 674 pixels and a race page to 1,040 in a 350 column, and the page clipped on the right. The region is now `min-width: 0`, so what is wide scrolls inside the view and the page never widens (Roadmap f-view-place-min-width). `bleed: true` took the gutter back on the home and a record's page but did nothing on a view titled as a place; a place now draws a view that bleeds edge to edge on a phone as they do (f-bleed-on-places). And where a view that bleeds did not draw — refused, past its limits, or not fetched yet — what is drawn in its stead ran edge to edge too; it is now marked (`data-worker-view-fallback`, laid out as if it were not there) and the bleed passes it by, so the app's own page keeps its gutters (f-fallback-lens-loses-gutter). The graview-view-drawing skill says a place bleeds too. `pnpm verify address` asks four more things, at 390 and 1440: a view's wide content never widens the page on a phone, on a place and a record's page; a place declared bleed draws edge to edge; a fallback page keeps its gutters; and, as before, the home and a record's view that bleed span the phone. The pages face alone carries 0.6 KB more, minified (0.1 KB gzipped), and the guest host 0.4 KB more when a view is registered, under raises (`scripts/budget-raises/views-on-phones-and-place-chrome-*.json`) shared with the next two changes.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — unchanged; a view drawn in a worker view's stead stands in a `div[data-worker-view-fallback]` with `display: contents`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; a view's `contain: inline-size` workaround is no longer needed, and `describe_place` may say a place's view bleeds where its manifest says `bleed: true`, which now holds on a place as on the home and a record.
- 6afe99b: A worker view draws more of what it asked for, and says why when it cannot. A sideways scroller may be `role="region"` with an `aria-label`, as axe and the WAI's guidance ask: the open kit refused `region` with the landmarks of the app's own chrome, so views fell back to `role="group"`; now a region is drawn while it has a name (`aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`) and held back while it has none, whatever order the view gives its attributes in (`NAMED_ROLES` on the open kit), and `main`, `banner`, `navigation`, `contentinfo`, `complementary`, `search`, `form` and the notices stay refused. An `<a data-record>` or `<a data-place>` inside a view's SVG — a timeline's dot, a map's pin — was not drawn while the view was kept; now it is a link as an HTML one is, with the address the face goes to as its `href`, the keyboard where there is none, and a click or Enter followed in the app. It takes no `href` of the view's: a link out is an HTML `<a href>`, which the host opens with no opener and no referrer. A card bound to a record that carries an `<a>` to the same record or place on its heading — the pattern the guide teaches — was refused as a link inside a link, while the same markup on an `<li>` passed; now the host gives such a card, a `<div>` and an `<li>` alike, no role and no tab stop (`carriesItsLink` on `host/pressable`), so the `<a>` is the keyboard's and the screen reader's link and the card a bigger target for a pointer and a finger, followed on a press anywhere inside it. A card that is the only link around a button, or around a link elsewhere, is still found, and the audit's fix now says to put the `<a>` on its heading; a box that scrolls is told `role="region"`. A view's CSS may now write `mask-image` and its `-webkit-` and longhand kin (a gradient: every `url()` and `image-set()` in a mask is refused as anywhere else), `-webkit-text-stroke`, scroll-driven animation (`animation-timeline`, `animation-range`, `scroll-timeline`, `view-timeline`, `timeline-scope`, `scroll()`, `view()`), and `filter: url(#id)`; and its SVG may draw `filter` with `feBlend`, `feColorMatrix`, `feComponentTransfer` and its `feFunc*`, `feComposite`, `feDisplacementMap`, `feDropShadow`, `feFlood`, `feGaussianBlur`, `feMerge`, `feMorphology`, `feOffset` and `feTurbulence` — each made of the view's own pixels. Where a person asks for less motion, every animation a view draws runs once in a millisecond, on no scroll, and every transition as short: the region's own sheet says it `!important` in a layer of the host's, which outranks anything a view's sheet can say, and a style attribute says a motion property without `!important`. Two stay out, and a check now says why: `@property`, which registers a property for the whole page, the app's own `--graview-*` among them; and SMIL (`animate`, `animateTransform`, `animateMotion`, `set`), which sets an attribute after the host judged it and moves past a reduced-motion setting — `@keyframes` does both jobs. `<feImage>` is refused because it loads an image. `sayRefusal` (and so `graview view check` and a headless run's problems) says these reasons. The graview-view-drawing and graview-view-links skills teach the card with its link on its heading, the SVG link, the named region and the new CSS. A worker view's open kit, fetched when a view first draws, is 5.4 KB minified larger and a view's runtime 1.6 KB, under two raises (`scripts/budget-raises/what-a-view-may-draw-open-kit.json`, `what-a-view-may-draw-runtime.json`); a page's first load is unchanged. Guest's tests hold that a named region is drawn and an unnamed one held back and said, that an SVG link carries its address and is followed on a click and Enter, that a card carrying its link passes the audit as a `<div>` and an `<li>` while a real nested control is still found, that a mask, a stroke, a scroll timeline and a filter are kept and their urls are not, and that `@property`, SMIL and `<feImage>` are refused with why; `guest-sandbox --transport=open` holds each in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged. The wire — unchanged. The declaration and check finding codes, derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. What a host's views draw — additive: a view that drew `role="region"` with a name, an SVG `<a data-record>`, a mask, a text stroke, a scroll timeline or an SVG filter now has them drawn where they were refused; a card that carries its link on its heading loses the `role="link"` and `tabindex` the host gave it; under reduced motion a view's animations and transitions are held still. The JavaScript API — additive: `NAMED_ROLES` on the open kit, `carriesItsLink` on `host/pressable`; `REFUSED_ROLES` no longer lists `region`, and `NEVER_DRAWN.svg` no longer lists `a` or `filter`. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; `propose_view`'s problems say why `@property`, SMIL and `<feImage>` were refused, and its views guide can say the card's link goes on its heading, an SVG dot is `<a data-record>`, and a scroller is `role="region"` with `aria-label`.

## 0.1.52

### Patch Changes

- 1a65f86: A list is exported as it is shown (Roadmap f-view-export-csv). A kind's list and a lens that lists records — a calendar, a timeline, a coverage, a board, a status board — offer a quiet, short "↓ CSV" press named "Export CSV" — beside a kind's heading, after adding one, and at the end of a lens's arranging line, and the place menu offers it too: either writes the records the place keeps, in its order, with its filter, its words and its groups — a group drawn shut included, since shutting one is how it is drawn — a "Group" column first when it is grouped, then each record's name, then the fields its page reads, computed ones too, each said as the page says it (money in the app's currency, a day in its locale, a choice as declared), headed by the fields' labels. Never more than the seat sees: the rows are the records the seat is served, so a field its sight keeps is in no row, and a field in no row is no column. The file is named for the place and the day ("Questions — 2026-10-23.csv"), with what a file system refuses replaced; the browser downloads it, or a host that saves files itself passes `onExport(csv, filename)` to the provider, `PagesApp`'s context or `mount`. Whoever may change the declaration is offered "Turn off export" in the place menu, and "Turn on export" where it is off: `export: false` on a lens (`LensDeclaration.export`) or on a kind's list (`pages.lists.<kind>.export`) offers it to nobody, set by the edit `set-lens-export` (`{ title, on?, export }`, on a kind's list by its kind or plural; refused on a lens that lists no records), read by a lens as it is drawn and by a list from the declaration, said by `diffDocuments` and `graview describe`, and written by `toDocument` only when it is off. `graview check` warns of an `export` that is not true or false (`lens-export-not-a-boolean`, `pages-list-export-not-a-boolean`) and of one on a lens that lists no records (`lens-export-lists-no-records`). The list page draws its rows from `shownList` on a new `@graview/core/shown` — the horizon unless the past is asked for, a rank worked out over what the seat sees, the declared groups in order with the closed ones shut — and the export writes the same rows, so the two cannot disagree. The writer is on a new `@graview/core/csv`, fetched with the first press: `toCsv` (RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, a byte-order mark so a spreadsheet reads accents), every cell that a spreadsheet would run as a formula — starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return — written with an apostrophe in front (`csvSafe`), `listCsvRows` and `listCsv`, and `csvFileName`. The studio's lens takes `export`, and its change is said as `set-lens-export`. The graview-lists skill says how. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front (the provider's `onExport`), under raises of the hosted page's budgets and the pages face alone's (`scripts/budget-raises/a-list-is-exported-as-it-is-shown-*.json`; the pages face's is mostly `shownList`, which the list now draws with), a visitor's form alone carries 6 bytes more and needs none, and the scene face's limit before it draws moves to 996 KB. Core's tests hold that the writer quotes, guards and is read back by `parseCsv`, that the rows are the page's order and groups, a shut group's rows among them, that a field the seat's sight keeps is no column, and that the switch is edited, checked, carried, written and said; embed's, that Export CSV is on a list's line and in the place menu, hands the host the rows the page draws, and is offered to nobody where it is off, with the owner's switch.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives — unchanged. Snapshot and log formats — unchanged; `KeptViews.lens` may be handed `set-lens-export`. The wire — additive: `/graview/views` takes `{ lens: { op: "set-lens-export", title, on?, export } }`. The declaration and check finding codes — additive: `export` on a lens and on `pages.lists.<kind>`, the edit `set-lens-export`, and the findings `lens-export-not-a-boolean`, `lens-export-lists-no-records` and `pages-list-export-not-a-boolean`. Derived tool names and input schemas — unchanged. The JavaScript API — additive: `@graview/core/shown` (`shownList`) and `@graview/core/csv` (`toCsv`, `csvCell`, `csvSafe`, `CSV_BOM`, `listCsvRows`, `listCsv`, `csvFileName`); `LensDeclaration.export`, `KindList.export`, and the `set-lens-export` member of `LensTidyEdit`; `OnExport` and the provider's `onExport`; `PageContext.onExport`; `FrameOptions.onExport`; `ArrangeBarProps.end`, `ArrangingOptions.exportable`, and `ExportPress`, `useShownListExport`, `shownListSaid`, `exportShownList`, `PlaceExportContext` and their keys on `@graview/primitives`; `saveShownList`, `switchExport`, `ShownListSaid` and `ListWords` on `@graview/core/csv`, where a list hands in how a record is said (`labelOf`, `readableFields`, `withComputed`) so the module fetched with the press imports none of them. What Graview Cloud does: takes this build; a host that saves files (or a guest frame that may not download) passes `onExport` to `mount`, and its views route passes `set-lens-export` to the declaration as it does `set-lens-hidden`.

## 0.1.51

## 0.1.50

### Patch Changes

- 14a74ed: Edits can reach readers when they are published rather than as they are made (Roadmap f-publishing-lifecycle). A declaration says `publishing: { mode: "live" | "on-publish", working: [roles], publishers?: [roles] }` — the document's key, `defineApp`'s, and `StoreOptions.publishing` on a store, which `openStore`, `openRemote`, the embed and the CLIs hand on. `"live"`, or nothing, is every app as it was. Under `"on-publish"` a seat whose roles are `working` (and an agent acting for one) is served the head and edits it in real time; every other seat — a visitor, an embed, a static page from `@graview/pages/static`, another app's linked read, a describe for a reader — is served the app as it stood at the last publish (`pastOf(store, publishedSeq)`, as its sight reaches it), or as it began before the first. Writes always land on the head: a visitor's form answer is in the working state and reaches readers with the next publish. A publish is the built-in act `publish` (`{ through? }`), offered to `publishers` (absent, `working`) and to an agent acting for one: one op by the person who published, touching no record, whose `publishedThrough` says the seq readers are served through, and the last one standing is `Store.publishedSeq` — so undoing a publish takes readers back to the one before. A reader's view follows the published seq and tells its subscribers only when it moves; `pastOf` refuses a reader a moment past it. `seenBy(principal, { as: "published" })`, the provider's `as` and `?as=published` in the page's address show a collaborator what readers see. `unpublished(store)` (`@graview/core/history`) says the gestures waiting, in words, for a badge and a diff. A checkpoint pins the published seq. On the wire a reader's `welcome`, `/graview/state` and `/graview/since` carry `servedThrough` (a cursor, opaque as every position on the wire), its socket is sent the ops between two publishes when the second lands, and the whole state when one is taken back. `graview check` judges a role `publishing` names that the policy does not (`publishing-unknown-role`), nobody working (`publishing-nobody-working`), a publisher not served the head (`publishing-publisher-not-working`) and an app act named `publish` (`publishing-act-taken`); `graview describe` says the mode; the graview-ship skill says how.
  
  Two fixes in the same place. A socket whose `hello` names a seq behind the compaction horizon was sent the ops from the horizon on as though those before had been sent; it is now welcomed with the whole `state`, and `/graview/since` answers such a seq with `reset: true` (and the ops after the horizon, so an older client still sees the gap); `openRemote` takes the state on either. And a seat's view was kept by the person an agent acts for, so a second agent for the same person was handed the first agent's view — its `seenFor`, `wouldChange` and `preview` judged as the first; the view is now kept per agent.
  
  Compatibility: additive — `publishing` (document and declaration), the op field `publishedThrough`, `Store.publishedSeq`, `seenBy`'s options, the wire's `servedThrough` and `reset`, an unasked `welcome` with `state`, `PUBLISH`, `onPublish`, `servesReaders`, the entry `@graview/core/publishing` (`publishMutation`, `publishRefusal`, `publishedSeqOf`), `unpublished` and four check finding codes are new, and a live app is served as before. A build that predates publishing refuses a document carrying `publishing` by its path, so an app that publishes is never opened by a build that would serve its readers the head, and folds an op carrying `publishedThrough` as a marker that touches nothing. Ops, primitives, stored formats, the document format version, `WIRE_PROTOCOL` and the derived tools of a live app are unchanged; an `on-publish` app's publishers gain the `publish` tool. A live app's hosted page carries 2.0 KB more up front (1.8 KB handed a compiled app), the pages face and a visitor's form about as much — the declaration's `publishing`, who is a reader, the store's hooks for `publish`, the provider's fetch for an app that publishes, and the two fixes above. How a reader is served is its own entry, `@graview/core/publishing`: a server, `@graview/pages/static`, `@graview/core/linked`, the command line and the agent tools import it, and a page fetches it with `@graview/core/past` before it draws an app that publishes; until it is loaded a reader of such an app is refused, never served the head, and the store offers no `publish`. Graview Cloud's worker imports 18.9 KB more (2_017_361 bytes against 1_997_994); its byte budget is 2_250_000.
- 5edb2e7: The Suggested tray offers Not now beside Accept and Decline. It hides a suggestion from the person who pressed it until tomorrow, and keeps that as a `Dismissal` in the host's store of the person's own state (`dismissals`: `{ list, dismiss, onChange? }`, a `DismissalStore`, on `GraviewProvider`, `mount` and `PagesApp`). Without one, the tray keeps it in this browser: localStorage where the page may use it, else the page's memory. The log says nothing of it, so the suggestion still waits for everyone else and the app's own actor is held to nothing; Decline still answers for everyone. What the button does is said to a screen reader, and when a row settles the keyboard moves to the next row, or to Activity, never to `<body>`. Given what the app's own actor did while the person was away (`whileAway`: `{ tidied, waiting? }`, which `whileAway()` from `@graview/tools/tidy` returns), the tray says "One suggestion waiting · tidied 12 things while you were away", and it still draws when nothing waits but something was done. A seat gets the same answer as a tool: `withNotNow(runtime, dismissals)` from `@graview/tools` lists `dismiss_suggestion` (with `until`, an ISO time, if the person names one) and leaves what the person dismissed out of `list_suggestions`. The chat and the agent seat wrap their runtimes with it. It wraps the runtime instead of living in it, so the names Graview Cloud's worker imports stay at 1_999_652 bytes of their 2 MB. Activity folds everything one run of the app's own actor applied (`Lineage.run`) into one row, wherever a person's change fell between its batches, and says a run made overnight that ended today as "Tidied 12 things overnight". Where an actor gave no run, consecutive batches fold as before. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front for it (105 bytes, 51 gzipped), and the scene face 480 more before it draws.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `dismissals` and `whileAway` are optional props, a host that passes neither gets the tray in this browser, and `dismiss_suggestion` is listed only by a runtime wrapped with `withNotNow`. The digest row a run makes is new: Activity rows for an actor that gives no `run` fold as before, and `ActivityRow`'s digest gains an optional `run`. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format and the tool schemas a bare `createToolRuntime` lists are unchanged.

## 0.1.49

### Patch Changes

- 1c1bfcd: A host's model is asked with one object and answers with one, so it can be handed a framing, pictures and a way to give up, and say what the call used, without breaking again. `Completion` was `(prompt: string) => Promise<string>`; it is `(request: { prompt, system?, images?, signal? }) => Promise<{ text, usage? }>`, each picture `{ mediaType, data }` in base64 and `usage` `{ inputTokens, outputTokens, costUsd? }`. `openAiCompatibleCompletion` and `xaiCompletion` send the framing as a system message and the pictures as image parts, give up on the caller's signal and read what the call used; `localCompletion` takes the framing; the inline AI hands the framing over apart from the ask rather than joined to it, and `completionDecide` reports the completion's usage. The dev server's door (`aiDevProxy`, `aiThroughDevServer`) takes `{ prompt, system?, images? }` and answers `{ text, usage? }`. The Fast slot's contract — `Decide`, `Decided`, `Answer`, `ChoiceAnswer`, `NoulAnswer`, `ScoreAnswer` and `Usage` — is in a module of its own rather than Jev's, by the same names on `@graview/tools`.
  
  Compatibility: breaking in the API a host's seat reaches, before 1.0 — every `HostAi.complete` and every caller of a `Completion` takes and returns the object (`async (prompt) => text` becomes `async ({ prompt }) => ({ text })`). Additive on the dev server's door: `system`, `images` and `usage` are optional fields. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format and tool schemas are unchanged.
- ec8c429: What a suggestion and a lineage keep is settled before 1.0. A suggestion may say when it stops waiting (`expiresAt` on `SuggestOptions` and on its op): from then on it leaves the tray, is neither accepted nor declined, and the same calls suggested again are a new suggestion, so an actor that still thinks so judges it again. "Not now" is not a decline: a `Dismissal` (`{ suggestion, at, until? }`) is the person's own state, which a host keeps where it keeps what a person chose and hands to `suggestions(store, principal, { dismissed })`; it hides the suggestion from that person alone, writes nothing to the log and holds the app's own actor to nothing, while a decline stays an undo in the log that answers it for everyone. A decline now refuses the same change by what it means as well as exactly as asked (`meaningKey`): the same link of a relation whichever act asks it, a record the actor would make again under a newly minted id, a merge of the same pair either way round, and a record archived by another spelling of the act — worked out from the calls, so a decline already in a log matches too. A lineage may say which judge made the call (`actor`), the run it was made in (`run`, one id for a night's work) and the records its basis rests on (`evidence`, at most 50 ids, which withhold the op from a seat that may not see one). A key a build does not know is kept as JSON keeps it, so a later client's lineage reaches every reader: none named `__proto__`, `constructor` or `prototype`, and the whole at most `LINEAGE_LIMIT` (4000) characters. A log written by 0.1.48 folds, waits and refuses as it did, held by a fixture of one. A hosted page carries 0.9 KB more up front for it (the pages face alone 1.1 KB), and the names Graview Cloud's worker imports come to 1_999_652 bytes of their 2 MB.
  
  Compatibility: additive for ops and the wire — `Operation.expiresAt` and `Lineage.actor`, `run` and `evidence` are optional, and an op without them reads and folds as before; a build before this one keeps `expiresAt` on an op it receives and reads the suggestion as waiting. Breaking, before 1.0, for a lineage's unknown keys: a store kept none before and keeps them now, and a lineage whose later key JSON cannot write, is named for a prototype, or runs past 4000 characters is refused with a `GraphError`. A decline refuses more than it did (the same change, however it is asked); nothing it refused before is let through. Stored formats, the document format and tool schemas are unchanged.
- 4ca00a7: The app's own actor can now find what to suggest, and a host can run it on a schedule. `@graview/tools/tidy` holds three judges that read the store as the actor sees it — `duplicates` (two records of a kind with one name, or one email or code, merged into the one with more links), `links` (a record whose text names another record, linked through a relation the app already declares and only where its cardinality allows; a name two records share links neither), and `archive` (a record its kind says is finished — its lifecycle, its main choice field read as the map reads it, or a done flag — unchanged for 30 days) — each giving its calls with a basis a person reads and a confidence. `dream(store, { now, limit })` runs them once as "Graview, tidying" and suggests each, surest first: never what a person declined, never twice what already waits, never about a record left alone, changed in the last day, or of a kind or relation the app says is never tidied. It applies nothing on its own unless the host passes `applyAtLeast`, since a declaration says only where the actor may never act; a model in the Fast slot's shape may weigh each candidate, and the run stands on the judges' own word when it does not answer. It returns what it did by judge. `whileAway(store, { seq })` is the tray's line: "2 suggestions waiting · tidied 12 things while you were away". A host that judges elsewhere suggests through the new `suggest_changes` tool, listed only for a seat that says `automated: true` and refused to any other in a sentence, and `graview dream <entry> --data ./data` runs it against a folder or a SQLite file. The tidying skill says how.
  
  Compatibility: additive — a new entry, `@graview/tools/tidy`, a new `graview dream` command, and a new tool, `suggest_changes`, listed only for an automated seat; every other seat's tool names and input schemas are unchanged. Ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged: a suggestion is the op `suggest` already writes.
- 1f80e4a: The app's own actor now leaves the judging to a System One model when the host lends one, and keeps code to finding, routing and making. `dream(store, { ai })` (or `{ model }`) puts forward far more than an exact match: two records of a kind that share a telling word, a key value or a near hit from the host's `near` ranker; a record whose text mentions another's name or one of its words; an empty choice field; an empty one-link relation. Each candidate is one request to the model, its records as named JSON as the actor may see them and every question about them together — a Score for whether two are the same thing with a Noul for the name and each key field, a Choice among the likeliest far records with "none of these" for each declared relation, a Noul for "finished or no longer relevant" with how long it has been unchanged and how its kind says it is finished, and, for the new `categorize` judge, a Choice over a field's options with "none fits". Code maps each answer to a confidence (a Score's expected level, a Choice's probability and its margin, a Noul's probability) and routes it by how much the change would undo (`BANDS`: merge 0.9 to act and 0.5 to ask, archive 0.85 and 0.55, a link or a filled choice 0.8 and 0.45; a split choice never acts): made unasked only where the host's `applyAtLeast` allows, suggested with a basis a person reads ("Jev: 0.93 that “Acme Corp” and “ACME Corporation” are the same vendor") and the model named in the lineage, or dropped. It asks six at a time within a run's budget of 60 requests and 200,000 input tokens, keeps each answer by a hash of what was asked (`memoryDreamCache`, or the host's own `DreamCache`) so an unchanged record is not asked again, and reports what it spent. Without a model, or where it failed or the budget ran out, the deterministic judges stand as the floor. `graview dream` takes `--model jev-latest` (with TYPESAFE_API_KEY), `--max-requests` and `--cache <file>`.
  
  Compatibility: additive — `@graview/tools/tidy` gains `categorize`, `missingLinks`, `BANDS`, `routeOf`, `memoryDreamCache`, `systemOneOf` and their types, a judge may say its `severity`, a run's report its `usage` and `budget`, and an outcome may be `dropped`; `graview dream` gains three flags. Ops, stored formats, the wire and every seat's tools are unchanged: a judgment is the suggestion or the lineage-carrying change `suggest` and `applyAll` already keep.
- 5f1603f: `graview view check --audit --browser` runs every accessibility rule, in a browser. The ring the keyboard shows, text contrast and a keyboard trap — the three rules of `auditView` that need a page laid out or Tab pressed — ran only inside the framework's own harness, so a view's author could check the structure of a view and nothing a person would see. With `--browser` the command lays what the view drew out in Chromium — its markup with the host's marks, its stylesheets as the host keeps them, the app's tokens, the host's region rules — between a control before it and one after, runs the audit there with what the view hears a click on, and presses Tab from before the view until the keyboard is past it, and Shift+Tab back; every request the page makes is refused, and the view's script does not run in it (a view hears no Tab and no focus, so the order Tab walks is the order its drawing gives). It says every rule it ran ("— every rule") and fails on a problem the view must fix, as `--audit` does; `--browser` implies `--audit`. It uses the project's own Playwright, now an optional peer of `@graview/guest` asked for only by this flag, and where there is none — or no Chromium — it exits 1 saying what to install. The audit reaches the page as one classic script built from its source (`pnpm guest:runtime`), which no app's page loads. `graview-view-links` says to add `--browser` for contrast, the ring and a trap. Guest's tests find the drawing handed to the page escaped and marked, the page's tokens and its controls around the view, the script current with its source and loading nothing, and the command saying what to install without Playwright while `--audit` alone still runs; `pnpm verify a11y` runs the command on a view with faint words and a `<div>` heard only by a click and finds every rule ran, the ring and Tab measured on its two buttons, and both problems found ("graviewViewCheckBrowserRunsEveryRule").
  
  Compatibility: new on the command line: `graview view check --browser`. `@graview/guest` gains `playwright` (>=1.40) as an optional peer dependency; nothing installs it, and nothing else asks for it. `runWorkerViewHeadless` takes `drawing: true` beside `audit: true` and answers with `drawing` (the tree it drew, with the host's marks, and the elements it hears a click on); a worker that imports `@graview/guest/headless` carries nothing more for it. The declaration, the wire, the stored formats, check findings and derived tools are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — its audit runs on its own pages.

## 0.1.48

### Patch Changes

- 29f5e0d: A declared row names its record. Threat Watch's threat row was set to `{severity} · {status} · …` alone, and its list drew lines of facts with no threat named in any of them — a list of links nobody could tell apart — while describe_place said each record's title above them: the words and the picture disagreed. A row is a link to its record and needs a name, for a reader and for a person: one whose blocks show no `title`, no `headline` and no words holding the record's whole name is now drawn with the record's name first, as a title is; one that names the record is drawn as declared (`blocksName`, on `@graview/core/blocks`). describe_place says the name that leads the row, then what its blocks say — a row led by a headline is said with the headline as its title, once, where it was said as the record's title and then as a heading. `graview-view-specs` says a row is always named, and how to choose how. Primitives' tests find a row of facts alone drawn with its record's name first and said with it, and a row led by a title, by a headline or by words that hold the name drawn as declared with the name once. A hosted page carries 0.9 KB more up front for this and the abbreviations of `a-creating-form-asks-its-name-first` (0.4 KB on the page handed a compiled app), the scene face 1.2 KB more before it draws, and the pages face alone 1.6 KB more (0.8 KB gzipped), under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-declared-row-names-its-record-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: the declaration, stored formats, the wire, check findings and derived tools are unchanged. A row spec that names its record nowhere now draws the record's name before its blocks; describe_place's item for a row led by a headline has the headline as its `title` and leaves it out of `parts`. New on `@graview/core/blocks`: `blocksName`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — its pages and describe_place agree once it takes this version.
- 5b30150: A lens kept off the lists is kept off a record's "Seen in" and a kind's "Also as" too, and `add-lens` refused a taken title without saying whose it was. Threat Watch, made in Graview Cloud, was given a board per choice field and a calendar per date it never asked for; its author hid them with `set-lens-hidden` and drew their own. A record's page still said "Seen in:" every hidden board, first, ahead of the pictures the app meant to show, and the kind's list said them under "Also as:". Both now pass over a hidden place, which is still a page at its address, reached by a link or `navigate`. And `add-lens` with the title "Remediations by audience" answered only "there is already a lens called…", for a lens nobody could see on the page: it now names the lens that holds the title — what draws it and over what, and that it is kept off the lists, which does not free its title or its address — and its fix says `"replace": "<that title>"` draws the new one in its place. A lens drawn in the place of a hidden one stays hidden, as it did; the sentence `add-lens` says now says so, and that `set-lens-hidden` with `"hidden": false` shows it. `describeApp` (`graview describe`) marks a hidden lens among the lenses that draw.
  
  `pages`' tests find a record's page saying "Seen in" the listed picture and not the hidden one, saying nothing when its only pictures are hidden, the kind's list leaving the hidden one out of "Also as", and the hidden picture still a page; `core`'s find the refusal naming the lens, its lens and kind, and its being hidden, the replace taking a hidden lens's title and saying it stays hidden until shown, and `describeApp` marking it. `graview-shipped-lens` says so.
  
  Compatibility: a behavior change on the routed face only: a record's "Seen in" and a kind's "Also as" leave out lenses marked hidden. `add-lens`'s refusal of a taken title and its sentence for a replaced hidden lens read differently; what it refuses and applies is unchanged. The API, the declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 29f5e0d: describe_place says the forms a place offers. An agent that declared "Add a threat" could not see what the form asks: which fields, in what order, which must be answered, a box or a choice and of what. A kind's list (and a lens's place over one kind, unless it says `create: false`) now carries `forms`: each act this seat may run that makes the kind's records, its title, its button and its fields in the order asked — each field's label in the form's own words, whether it must be answered, what it takes ("text", "long text", "date", "date and time", "number", "amount", "choice", "yes or no", "record", "picture", "list of …", "group"), a choice's choices as the form offers them, a number's range and a picker's kinds — less what the seat's sight keeps from it. A visitor's form has a place of its own, `forms/<name>` (or `/forms/<name>`): its title, its intro and what it asks the visitor (`drawnBy: "form"`), and a place not found lists the forms among the places. The text says each form under the place, a star on a field that must be answered. It is read from the walk every face's form is drawn from (`formFields`, `argumentWords`, `keptArguments`), so what is said is what is asked. `graview describe --place forms/<name>` says the same, the `describe_place` tool's description says forms and `forms/<name>`, and `graview-visitor-forms` says how to read one. Pressing a view's `data-act` headless was already there (`runWorkerViewHeadless(…, { press })`, `graview-view-forms`) and is unchanged. Core's tests find a list's creating form said field by field in order with labels, stars, inputs, choices and a range, no form offered to a seat that may not run it, a visitor form said at its own address, and the forms named among the places.
  
  Compatibility: additive — `PlaceDescription.forms`, `DescribedForm` and `DescribedFormField` on `@graview/core/describe`, `drawnBy: "form"`, and `forms/<name>` as a place. Places described before are said as they were, with their forms after them. Stored formats, the wire, the declaration and check findings are unchanged. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — its describe_place carries the forms once it takes this version.

## 0.1.47

### Patch Changes

- 6d84721: A view's check knows the place of a titled view the host keeps, and `describePlace` describes that place (Roadmap f-check-view-places). Threat Watch, on Graview Cloud, keeps a view of many threats titled "Live threats", drawn at `/places/live-threats`; its home linked there with `<a data-place="live-threats">`, the link worked on the page, and `propose_view`'s check said "It names the place "live-threats", which this app does not have" — a false warning that teaches a chat to stop writing `data-place`. The check was handed the document's places and never the host's views, and `describe_place` said the place was not one the app has. Now `runWorkerViewHeadless` takes `views`, the manifests of the views the host keeps — the list `placesOf` and `describePlace` already take — and each titled view of many is a place at the slug the router gives it, through `viewPlaceOf` on `@graview/core/seat`, the function `placesOf` itself reads, so a `data-place`, a `graview.navigate` or a press on a link naming one is no problem; the view being run is a place of its own when it is titled. A name none of the places is still goes nowhere and says so. A run that is not handed `views` cannot know the host's views' places, so it no longer says such a name for certain: "It names the place "live-threats", which is none of the places this run was told of. … This run was not handed the views the host keeps, so a titled view's place is not among these: if it names one, the link goes there." `graview view check` hands `views: []`, since the app's registered views are its places already, and says what it said before. `describePlace` handed `views` finds a titled view's place by its slug, its title or its address, and says it as it says a lens's: its title, slug and address, drawn by `view:<name>`, one `{ t: "view" }` part with the view's title, whether it flows with the page or keeps a frame of its own (`flows`, only where the host said), and the parts a headless run drew; its text says `[the view "Live threats", flowing with the page]` and what the view drew under it. `graview-view-links` says a titled view's place is a place a link may name, and `graview-host-page` that `describePlace` and `runWorkerViewHeadless` take the views. `core`'s tests describe a titled view's place by slug, title and address, flowing, framed and unsaid, and none where the host names no such view; `guest`'s find a `data-place` and a navigate naming a titled view's place not flagged and a press on it opening it, a slug naming nothing still flagged for certain, a run without the views saying it as a doubt, and the place a run drew described.
  
  Compatibility: additive in the API — `runWorkerViewHeadless`'s `views`, `viewPlaceOf` on `@graview/core/seat`, `DescribedHostView.flows` and `hidden`, and `flows` on a `{ t: "view" }` part. A behavior change in a headless run that is not handed `views`: a `data-place`, a `data-record` or a navigate naming none of its places is said as a doubt rather than as no place of the app, and a titled view's own place is among its places; `describePlace` handed `views` describes a titled view's place it refused before. The manifest, the declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 7478654: A drawn view's accessibility is audited (Roadmap f-view-a11y-audit). `auditView(root)` from `@graview/guest/audit`, a new entry no page loads, holds what a worker view drew — its shadow root on a page, or a headless run's tree — to what a person on a keyboard or with a screen reader meets, and says each problem as a repair: the rule, its WCAG 2.2 criterion, how bad, where (a selector from the nearest `data-key`), a one-line fix, and `automatic` when the framework is the one to fix it rather than the view. It judges the drawing as the host draws it, so a link, a press and a control the view drew itself, which the host gives the keyboard, are no problem, and a `<div>` the view only hears a click on is. Eight rules run wherever the drawing is: `keyboard` (2.1.1), `role`, `name`, `toggle-state` and `nested-interactive` (4.1.2), `img-alt` and `svg-name` (1.1.1), and `scroll-region` (2.1.1). The ring the keyboard shows (`focus-visible`, 2.4.7 and 1.4.11) and text `contrast` (1.4.3) run only in a browser that lays the page out, and say how many elements they measured; anywhere else they are listed as skipped with why, never as passed, as is a keyboard trap (2.1.2), which needs keys pressed. `runWorkerViewHeadless(…, { audit: true })` audits what a run drew, with the elements its view listens on for a click — the view's runtime now says which those are — and `graview view check … --audit` prints it and fails on a problem the view must fix.
  
  A place's frame that scrolls its picture inside it (a lens, or a view with `flows: false`), and a draft view's frame, now take the keyboard and are named for the place: `role="group"` with the place's title, so the arrows scroll it and a screen reader says what it holds. A group rather than a region, because the view drawn in it is a region of that name already, and two landmarks of one name fail axe's `landmark-unique`.
  
  `guest`'s tests flag a `<div>` with only a click handler and pass one that says `role="button"`, pass an `<a data-record>` and a card bound to a record, flag a button with no name, a field with no label, a toggle with no state, a control inside another and a card that holds a button, an image with no alt and an SVG that is `role="img"` with no name, say which rules ran and which did not, audit Threat Watch headless with the clicks its view hears, and run `graview view check --audit` to its exit code. `pnpm verify a11y` draws Threat Watch's home and the guest sandbox's offers views on the pages face and audits each in the browser, light and dark: the host's ring and the views' words measure (15 rings and 23 words on Threat Watch), faint words and a ring taken away laid on the same page are found, the audit on the page says what the headless run says, the framework's markup around each view passes the audit, a framed place is a box named for it that Tab reaches, Tab goes into each view and out again, and what axe finds inside a view the audit finds too. `graview-view-links` says how to run it. The view's runtime is 162 bytes larger (48 gzipped), under raises of its budget and of the guest host's that inlines it (`scripts/budget-raises/a-drawn-views-accessibility-is-audited-runtime.json`, `…-open-kit.json`); nothing a hosted page loads up front grew.
  
  Compatibility: additive for a host — `@graview/guest/audit` is a new entry, `runWorkerViewHeadless`'s `audit` and `graview view check`'s `--audit` are optional, and a headless run's transcript may carry `clicks`; a framed place's scrolling box is now a tab stop named for the place. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.
- f85abad: A worker view of one record is a card on the Scene, as a view of many already was, and a host with no Pages expands the card in place. The record in focus kept to its box and scrolled inside it (FR-141) — a page inside a picture, with the record's own fields under the view at the foot of a scroller. `registerWorkerView` now marks a view of one record previewed too, and the Scene draws it as the same card (`ScenePreview`): the record's label, as much of the view and the fields under it as the box holds — it measures all it holds, not only what the view drew — faded at the foot where there is more, and Open, which goes to the record's page on Pages (`/tasks/t1`, under the page's base where it has one). FR-141's rule changes with it: the record in focus is still held to its box and scrolls inside it when the framework's record, a declared page or a view the host draws in React stands there, but no longer a worker view's card, which the stylesheet's rule passes over (`[data-graview-record-focus] > :not(.graview-kind-tag):not([data-graview-preview])`); a panel inside the card is still drawn whole. Where there are no pages to open — a Shell drawn without the embed's page door — the card offered nothing; now it offers Expand, which grows it in place from its top to the height of all it holds, with nothing in it scrolling, laid over the cards below it until Collapse or Escape takes it back.
  
  `react`'s tests find Expand grow the card past its box and its `scene.maxHeight`, unfaded and with nothing scrolling, Collapse and Escape take it back, no control where the card holds it all, and a view of one record drawn through `ResolvedView` as a card named for the record with Open to its page; `guest`'s find a view of one record marked previewed (it was asserted unmarked under FR-141, and that test now says the opposite on purpose); `primitives`' find the record's scroll passing over the card and a panel inside it still drawn whole. `verify-address`, at 1440 and 390, opens a view of one task eighty notes long on the Scene and finds a card no taller than its box and inside the window, faded, with nothing scrolling and Open landing on the task's page (aTallRecordViewOnTheSceneIsACardWhoseOpenLandsOnTheRecordsPage), and in the whole-page Shell finds Expand growing it from its top to all it holds and Collapse taking it back (withNoPageDoorExpandGrowsTheRecordsCardInPlace). `graview-worker-view` says so. The scene face carries 1.3 KB more before it draws (1_376 bytes), under the before-it-draws limit raised to 974 KB.
  
  Compatibility: on the Scene a worker view of one record is drawn as a card held to its box with Open to the record's page, where it was a box that scrolled, and a Scene card where there are no pages offers Expand. The API is unchanged. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.
- ed05ba4: A worker view is as tall as it draws on every face, and on the Scene a tall one is a card with Open rather than a column three screens tall. The host measures the drawn root (`[data-graview-view-root]`, now a block of its own so its height holds its children's margins) with a `ResizeObserver` and keeps that as the view's content height — on the region as `data-graview-content-height`, on the handle as `contentHeight`, and told to the face through `mountWorkerView`'s `onContentHeight`; a view never says its own height. On Pages a titled view's place, the home and a record's page already flowed with the page at that height (0.1.46), and still do. On the Scene a view of a kind's members was drawn as the whole of itself in its place's box — sixty rows spilling up under the bar and down past every card around it, with nothing behind them. `registerWorkerView` now marks such a view previewed (`markPreviews`, read by `previewOf`), and the Scene draws it as a card like its other pictures: its title, as much of the view as the box holds — at most its measured content height — the foot fading into the card's ground where there is more, and Open, which goes to its place on Pages (`PageDoorContext`, which the embed provides with the address of the page). Nothing in the card scrolls. Two manifest hints say otherwise: `flows: false`, for a fixed canvas such as a map or a chart, keeps its place's frame on Pages; `scene: { maxHeight }` holds its Scene card lower, 80 to 4000 pixels. `checkManifest` judges both in sentences and refuses either where it means nothing (a frame for a view that is no place, a Scene card for a view of one record). `graview-worker-view` says how a view is sized on each surface and the two hints. `guest`'s tests find the hints taken and refused, a view of the members marked previewed with its `maxHeight`, and a view that says `flows: false` drawn in its place's frame; `react`'s find a card held to its box at the height its host measured, faded and with Open where there is more, Open going to the place without choosing the card, no Open where there are no pages, and no fade where the card holds it all. `verify-address` opens a view eighty entries long on the Scene at 1440 and 390 and finds it a card no taller than its box and inside the window, at its measured height, faded, with nothing in it scrolling, and Open landing on its place, flowing whole (aTallViewOnTheSceneIsDrawnBoundedWithOpenAndOpenLandsOnItsPlace), and a view that says `flows: false` and `scene.maxHeight` held to both (aViewThatSaysFlowsFalseKeepsItsFrameAndItsScenePreviewKeepsToItsMaxHeight). A hosted page carries 0.5 KB more up front (469 bytes, 75 gzipped; 127 on the page handed a compiled app), the scene face 3_020 bytes more before it draws, the guest host registering a worker view 1_411 (352) and drawing one on the open kit 1_570 (420), under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-view-is-as-tall-as-it-draws-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the API — the manifest's `flows` and `scene`, `WorkerView.contentHeight`, `MountWorkerViewOptions.onContentHeight`, and `@graview/react`'s `markPreviews`, `previewOf`, `PREVIEWS`, `ViewContentHeight` and `PageDoorContext`; a manifest without the hints is judged and drawn as before on Pages. On the Scene a worker view of a kind's members is now drawn as a card held to its box with Open. The wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 6f9bf30: A worker view's `<button>` and its links are plain by default, so the semantic element is the easy one. A view that drew a real `<button>` had the browser's gray box, border and font to undo, so views drew a `<div>` with a click handler instead — reached by no keyboard and nothing to a screen reader. The region now resets every `<button>` to the words around it (`font` and `color` inherited, no background, border, padding or margin, `appearance: none`, a pointer) and a link the host made to its words' color with no underline, both under `:where`, at no specificity, so a view's own class or element rule always wins. A `<button data-record>` goes where it says as an `<a data-record>` does, and on an element bound to an act `data-record` is always that act's record and never a link: `<button data-act="acknowledge" data-record="threat:7">` acknowledges the threat and goes nowhere, which the tests now hold. The guides say it plainly — go somewhere with `<a data-record>` or `<a data-place>`, do something with a `<button>` or `<button data-act>`, style either freely, and draw no `<div>` or `<span>` with only a click handler; a list of records is a list of `<a data-record>`, never `graview.on("click", "[data-id]", … navigate)` — in `graview-worker-view`, `graview-view-links` and `graview-view-forms`, and in `@graview/guest`'s README, whose example heard a button's click to navigate. No view audit exists yet to flag a clickable `<div>`: that is the coming view accessibility audit's.
  
  `guest`'s tests read the region's two zero-specificity rules, follow a `<button data-record>` keeping its own role and tab stop, and press a button bound to an act with a `data-record` of its own and one inside a card bound to another record, each carrying its record and going nowhere. In the taps transport, in three engines, an `<a data-record>` is drawn in the words' own color and not underlined.
  
  Compatibility: a view's `<button>` drawn with no style of its own now looks like the words around it rather than the browser's button; a view that wants the browser's look sets it on its own class. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 6f9bf30: A worker view's controls are reached by the keyboard by default. On Graview Cloud's Threat Watch, Tab reached 6 of the home's 92 things a person could click, and none on most views: the host gave the keyboard to a card bound to a record and to an element bound to an act, and to nothing a view drew and heard itself. Now any control a view draws that does not press of itself — a `role` of button, checkbox, switch or tab, an `aria-pressed`, an `aria-expanded`, on a `<div>` or a `<span>` the view hears with `graview.on("click")` — is given the keyboard where it has none (`tabindex="0"`; a tab the view keeps at `-1` keeps it) and drawn as something to press, and Enter or Space on it is told to the view as the key and then a `click`, as a button's is, with the page kept from scrolling under Space; an element bound to an act is told the same `click` after its key. A table's row bound to a record keeps its role and gives the keyboard to its first cell, where Enter opens the row. Whatever has the keyboard shows a ring: a whole outline at least 2px wide, set 2px off, in the app's accent, `!important` so a view's `outline: none` does not take it away — a view restyles it with `--graview-focus-color`, `--graview-focus-width` (never under 2px) and `--graview-focus-offset`. An `<svg>` with no `role`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `<title>`, and nothing inside it that takes the keyboard, is hidden from assistive technology (`aria-hidden="true"`). `.graview-sr` is a class the region draws as nothing on the page and says to a screen reader. And the keyboard stays where it was across a render: an element with a `data-key` the view moves among its siblings was drawn anew by the host, which took the keyboard and what the host knew of the element with it; the host now puts the same element back where the view moved it and gives it the keyboard again if it had it.
  
  `guest`'s tests draw a row, a `<div role="button">`, an `aria-pressed` and an `aria-expanded` span, a checkbox, a switch, two tabs, a button that says `aria-pressed` and four pictures, and find each control given the keyboard, pressed by Enter and Space as the key and a click with the default kept, the button left its own, the row opened from its first cell, the picture that says nothing hidden and the others left to say what they say; and a view's own runtime drawing a keyed list again in place, re-sorted twice and with the focused row gone, the keyboard on the same element each time and handed to nothing when it went. `node scripts/guest-sandbox.mjs --transport=taps` holds it in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: Tab from the top of the page reaches all fifteen links and presses Threat Watch's home draws (Option-Tab in Safari, which passes over any link with an address without it), Space on a `<div role="button" aria-pressed>` the view hears itself presses it without scrolling the page and the keyboard stays on it as the view draws again, and the ring is solid, 2px, set off and at 3:1 or more against the ground behind it, light and dark. The worker views' guides say it: `graview-worker-view` (links and buttons by default) and `graview-view-forms`.
  
  Compatibility: additive for a view — a control it drew with a role, `aria-pressed` or `aria-expanded` and no `tabindex` is now given `tabindex="0"`, and its listener for `click` now also hears Enter and Space on it; a decorative `<svg>` now carries `aria-hidden="true"`; a view's `outline` on `:focus-visible` gives way to the host's ring unless it sets the `--graview-focus-*` properties. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.
- f85abad: A worker view's link in an embed carries the host's own address, or none, and a focus ring at the edge of a view's region is drawn whole. In an embed whose host keeps its own addresses — memory routing, a chat's widget, an article — an `<a data-record>` carried the routed face's base and path as its `href` (`/tasks/t1`), so a Cmd-click opened a page of the host's site that may not exist. The embed now takes `hrefOf(path)`, the host's address for a page of the app, and a view's links, the bar's places and the Scene's Open carry what it returns; under memory routing with none given they carry no `href`, and a view's link is a link by its role and a tab stop, as on the scene, followed in the app on a click, a tap and Enter. Under address routing they carry the face's own address unless the host says otherwise. `PagesApp` takes the same `hrefOf`, or `false` for no address; `PageDoor.href` may answer none, and the Scene's Open is then a link by its role. And a worker view's region clips what the view draws to its box, which cut the ring of a control flush with its edge in half: while something in it has the keyboard the region lets the ring's reach — its 2px and its 2px offset — paint past its edge (`overflow-clip-margin`, in Chromium and Firefox) and nothing else, still contained, isolated and clipped; in WebKit, which has no `overflow-clip-margin`, a control found within that reach of the edge as it takes the keyboard draws its ring inside its own edge. A Scene card's body clips only its top and foot, and lets the ring past them while the keyboard is in it.
  
  `embed`'s tests find a view's link given no address under memory routing, the host's under `hrefOf`, and the face's own under address routing; `guest`'s find the region letting the ring past while the keyboard is in it and, in an engine without `overflow-clip-margin`, a control at the edge marked to draw its ring inside. In the taps transport, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, Threat Watch is mounted with the host's addresses (`/?at=<path>`): its `<a data-record>` carries the host's address, a Cmd- or Ctrl-click opens the host's page there on the threat's page, and a plain click opens it in the app; mounted with none, the link carries no `href`, is a link by its role and a tab stop, and Enter opens the threat's page; and a link flush with the region's left edge, given the keyboard, shows all four sides of its ring. `graview-embed` and `graview-view-links` say so. A hosted page carries 44 bytes more up front (0.0 KB more up front, 47 gzipped; 44 / 60 on the page handed a compiled app), and the guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit 647 bytes more (235 gzipped), under raises of those budgets (`scripts/budget-raises/a-views-link-carries-the-hosts-address-*.json`, `a-views-ring-is-whole-at-its-regions-edge-open-kit.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive for a host — the embed's and `PagesApp`'s `hrefOf` are optional; under memory routing an embed's view links and the Scene's Open carry no `href` unless the host gives `hrefOf`, where they carried the face's own path; under address routing nothing changes. `PageDoor.href` may return `undefined`. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 6f9bf30: A worker view's links are real links. An `<a data-record>` or `<a data-place>` was followed on a press but was no link to the browser: no address on hover, nothing in a new tab on Cmd- or Ctrl-click, nothing to copy — the host gave it a `role="link"` and a `tabindex` in place of an `href`. Now on the routed pages the host gives each the address the face goes to as its `href`, with the face's base, filter and section (`/a/<app>/threats/threat%3A7`, `/places/by-day?filter=day%3Afri`), opened in this tab: a plain click, a tap and Enter are followed in the app as before, and a click with Cmd, Ctrl, Shift or Alt, or a middle click, is the browser's — a new tab, a new window — and never also taken by the face around the view. With an address, the link needs no role or tab stop of the host's, and they are taken back; on the scene, which has no addresses, a link keeps them. The view still never writes an app's address itself: an `href` it gives is a link out, judged as before, and an `<a>` with one is left as the open kit drew it. A link out alone says it opens a new tab. `GoTo` (`@graview/react/provider`) takes an optional `href(to, also)`, the address going there arrives at, and the routed face's gives it (`/a/<app>` joined on, as a listed record's link does); a host that mounts a worker view itself gives `hrefOf` on `mountWorkerView`, or none.
  
  `guest`'s tests give a record link and a place link their addresses with no role, tab stop or target, follow a plain click and leave Cmd, Ctrl, Shift and middle clicks unprevented, unfollowed and unheard by the face, give no address to a link that goes nowhere or on a face with none, and put the open kit's target back when a link stops naming a record. `pages`' tests read the routed face's `href` for a record, a list narrowed and to a section, and a lens under `basename="/a/hall"`, the address the face then arrives at, and none for what it does not have. In the taps transport, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, Threat Watch's `<a data-record>` carries `/threats/threat%3Aphishing`, a Cmd- or Ctrl-click opens a new tab there and leaves the app on its home, and a plain click opens the threat's page at that address. `graview-view-links` says so. The guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit carries 4.1 KB more for the three changes together (4_094 bytes, 1_381 gzipped), and the guest host before a worker is drawn 207 bytes more (36 gzipped), under raises of those budgets (`scripts/budget-raises/a-views-keyboard-and-real-links-open-kit.json`, `…-guest-host.json`); nothing a hosted page loads up front grew.
  
  Compatibility: additive for a host — `GoTo.href` and `mountWorkerView`'s `hrefOf` are optional, and a host that gives neither draws links as before; on the routed pages a view's `<a data-record>` and `<a data-place>` now carry an `href` and no `role` or `tabindex`. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.46

### Patch Changes

- 15f234f: A titled worker view's place flows with the page, and a brand may keep the line under the app's name off the home without blanking the description. Every view opened at `/places/<slug>` was drawn in a frame `min(72vh, 760px)` tall with a border that scrolled inside itself, under the page's own scroll — on a phone, a box that traps the thumb — while the home's view and a place of blocks flowed. `registerWorkerView` now marks the view it registers `flows`, as a place of blocks is, and its place is drawn in the page's column as long as the view draws; a calendar, a board, the columns and the other pictures keep their frame. `graview-worker-view` says a view's place flows with the page and needs no height to fill one. And with a home view the routed face drew the document's `description` above it, so a view with its own hero said everything twice, and the only way to stop it was to delete the description, which took it out of the app's listings too. A document's `brand.subtitle: false` now keeps the line off the home — over a home view, under a home of blocks and on the derived home alike — and the description stays the app's: the brand's `subtitle`, the name's hover on the bar, its listings. `set-brand` writes it (`subtitle: false`; `true` or `null` draws the line again) and says so in words, the diff of a change says it the same way, the compiled brand carries `subtitleDrawn: false`, and `describePlace` leaves the line out of the home's masthead. Left out, the line is drawn as it always was. `graview-brand` documents the key. `guest`'s tests find a titled view's place marked to flow and drawn with no height and nothing scrolling inside it, and `pages`' find a calendar, a board and the columns keeping their frame; `verify-address` opens a kept view's place and the board at 1440 and 390 in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, and finds no box between the view and the page that scrolls while the board's frame does (aTitledViewsPlaceFlowsWithThePageWhileABoardKeepsItsFrame). `core`'s tests find the key validated as false and refused as anything else, written by `set-brand` and taken back by `true` and `null` to the very document, and the masthead without the line; `pages`' find the home drawing no line with it and the line without it, the description still the name's hover. A hosted page carries 0.0 KB more up front (3 bytes, 1 gzipped) and the guest host registering a worker view 6 (1), under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-views-place-flows-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the declaration — `brand.subtitle`, optional and only `false`; a document without it draws as it did, and a build that predates it refuses a document that carries it. Additive in the API: `Brand.subtitleDrawn` and the `set-brand` edit's `subtitle`. A titled worker view's place no longer scrolls inside itself; the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 99d25eb: Built-in views look designed: one type scale, one left edge and one heading per page on the place header, blocks lenses, grouped row and card lists, kind lists and record pages (Roadmap f-auto-view-type-polish). Threat Watch's By type page stacked two headings — the place's title and count, then the lens's own larger headline saying the count again — inset its rows 16 pixels past the title's edge, ruled each group label with a one-sided accent, drew 69 row titles in the display face, said "high · resolved · supply-chain · disclosed May 18, 2026" as one string, and pressed its arrow against "All threats as a list→". Now a blocks lens that opens with a headline hands it to its page, which says it as the line under the title in place of the count and draws it once; on its page the lens stands in the page's own column, with no inset of its own; a group's heading is its label and how many it holds, in the text face, over the rows' own hairline; a record's name in a row or on a card is the text face at medium or semibold weight and normal tracking, never the display face; a card's or a row's line of facts with a choice field named bare in it draws each choice as a small chip and the rest as quiet words after them, in the order written, and a kind's list draws its glance's choices the same way; an arrow after a link (→, ↗) stands a space from its words and is not underlined; a place's header, its first block, its blocks and its groups are spaced on a 4px scale, given as tokens (`--graview-space-1` … `--graview-space-7`: 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48); a page's title steps one step down the scale on a phone; and on a desk wide enough for a margin, a kind's list hangs its choose boxes in that margin so its names start on the title's edge. A hosted page carries 0.3 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and their budgets are raised by that; the pages face alone by what it measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; the declaration is unchanged and no check finding is added. New: a resolved text block on a card or a row carries `runs` (each fact, a choice marked by its `field`) where one of its facts is a choice field named bare; describe_place says such a line as it is drawn, a chip a space from what is beside it ("High Contained Supply chain disclosed May 18, 2026") and a grouped list's group carries its `count`, said in the text as "Supply-chain compromises (6):"; the theme writes `--graview-space-1` … `--graview-space-7`; `PlacePageHead` on `@graview/primitives/pages`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing to keep Threat Watch's v19 workaround — a lens may open with a headline again, and its page says it once, under the title.

## 0.1.45

### Patch Changes

- a811afc: A card a worker view binds to a record opens on a click, a phone's tap and Enter, not only an `<a>`. Graview Cloud's Threat Watch drew its home as cards — `<div class="card" data-record="threat:…">` with a heading, badges and a line inside — and `describe_place` listed them as record cards, yet the host made a link only of an `<a data-record>`, so a press on a card opened nothing, and on a phone a tap on its heading did nothing at all; its authors worked around it with `data-open` and `graview.on("click", "[data-open]", e => graview.navigate(…))`, `pointer-events: none` on the children and `touch-action: manipulation`. Now any element with `data-record` or `data-place` is a link the host follows, from wherever inside it the press lands: the host walks from the element pressed up to the nearest link, so a tap on a card's heading or a badge opens the card, and a press that lands on something inside with a press of its own — a button, a field, a `data-act`, a lightbox, a drop target, a control's `role`, another link — is that thing's alone, so a "More" toggle the view binds with `graview.on("click")` toggles and opens nothing. An element is no link when it is a press scope: a `fieldset` or `role="group"`, an element with `data-act`, `data-lightbox`, `data-drop` or `data-field`, a field, or one that holds a field to type into or pick from (a checkbox leaves its row a link). The host gives a link the keyboard (`tabindex="0"`) and makes it a link to assistive technology unless it is a list's item, a table's row or says a role of its own, and the region draws every link with `cursor: pointer`, which is what iOS makes a tap a click on, `touch-action: manipulation`, so a tap is not held for a double tap's zoom, and a faint tap highlight. Each element is looked at again after every batch the view draws, since what a card holds is known only once it is drawn whole. How a tap reaches a view, now said in the guide: a phone makes a tap a `click`, the host hears it on the view's shadow root and follows a link first, then tells the view, whose `graview.on("click", selector, fn)` is handed `event.target`, what the finger landed on, and `event.element`, the element the selector matched at or above it.
  
  `guest`'s tests draw Threat Watch's card, a row, a place's section, a fieldset and a card holding a field, and find the card and the row links the host made, followed from the heading, a badge, a line nested deeper and by Enter, the face around the view never hearing the press, a button and a checkbox inside left their own, and the press scopes no links. `node scripts/guest-sandbox.mjs --transport=taps`, a new transport, mounts Threat Watch's home as a host does: in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox the card is a link drawn with a pointer and no double-tap zoom, and a click on its heading and Enter on it open the threat's page; and on a phone (Chromium's and WebKit's mobile emulation; Firefox has none) a tap on its heading and on a badge inside it open the threat's page, and a tap on the view's own toggle toggles it and opens nothing. Before the change a click, Enter and a tap each left the reader on the home. Links in a worker view are a skill of their own, `graview-view-links` — a link, a card that opens, how a tap reaches the view, narrowed and to a section — and `graview-worker-view`, which was near its limit, points at it. The guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit carries 1.7 KB more (1_736 bytes, 738 gzipped), under a raise of that budget (`scripts/budget-raises/a-card-bound-to-a-record-opens-on-a-tap-open-kit.json`); nothing a hosted page loads up front grew.
  
  Compatibility: additive for a view — an element other than an `<a>` with `data-record` or `data-place`, which opened nothing, now opens its record or place on a press and is given `tabindex="0"` and, where it has no meaning of its own, `role="link"`; a view that bound such an element to do something else on a click binds it by `data-key` instead. The wire, the stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 7069188: A form for a visitor: one act of an app opened to people who never join the workspace — an RSVP, a sign-up — drawn alone, run as a visitor the host mints per answer, its answer landing as a record and nothing else of the app shown. Roadmap f-public-forms, filed by Graview Cloud: "The pieces are in place (people records, link roles, the files route); what is left is a form page per kind for a visitor with no account, a minted author per submission ('form:<token>'), abuse limits, and the plan gate." The framework's half is here; abuse limits, the plan gate and minting the token stay the host's.
  
  The declaration says the form: `forms.<name>` in a document, `forms` on `defineApp` — `act` (one that makes a record or acts on none), `title`, `intro`, `fields` (the arguments asked, in order; unsaid, every one a visitor could fill), `say` (what the visitor reads under "Received — thank you."), `submit` and `role` (`visitor`, unsaid). It is not a second permission system: a visitor is a principal holding that role, so the policy says what they may do and see as it says it for anybody — a grant lets the role run the act, and a sight that never names it keeps every record from it. `formVisitor(app, "rsvp", "form:<token>")` (`@graview/core/forms`) mints `{ kind: "human", id, name: "a visitor via the RSVP form", roles: ["visitor"] }`, so Activity names each answer for the form and never by its token (`nameOfAuthor` says "a visitor via a form" for a `form:` id that carries no name); `visitorForm(app, name)` resolves the act, the fields and the words; `formReceived` is what is said after. `graview check` holds a form to what a visitor can do: `form-unknown-act`, `form-act-on-a-record`, `form-without-sight` (no policy, or one that says nothing of who sees what, so the visitor would be served every record), `form-act-not-granted`, `form-field-unknown`, `form-omits-required` and `form-field-unfillable` (a required picker over records the visitor is not served, or a field their sight keeps from them) are errors; `form-visitor-sees` (a sight serves the visitor a kind — to everybody, or by name where no picker asks for it) and `form-visitor-may-more` (the role may run acts no form opens) are warnings.
  
  `@graview/embed/form`'s `mount(el, { app, form: "rsvp", principal: "form:<token>", remote })` draws the form alone — no bar, no lists, no record, no seat — over the store as the visitor is served it, runs the act as them, says it was received in the declaration's words with the keyboard on what it said, and tells `onSubmitted`; `principal` is the minted id or the visitor whole, and a form the declaration has not got is refused by its name. `@graview/pages/visitor-form`'s `VisitorFormPage` is the same for a React host. The derived form asks the arguments `only` names in that order. `graview preview` serves each form at `/forms/<name>`, a visitor minted per page and handed only what that visitor is served, and the page's head no longer preloads the preview seat's document there. The store, the act and the served seat needed nothing new: the visitor's seat is served nothing, its log withholds every record, and an id it guesses is refused as missing.
  
  `core`'s tests read the form from a document and give it back, resolve its fields and words, mint the visitor, run the act as them and find the record on the whole store and its author named for the form, refuse another act, serve the seat nothing before and after its answer (its answer and its log name no other record, and a guessed id is told the same as an absent one), and say every finding; `pages`' draw the RSVP alone, submit as the visitor and say it was received; `embed`'s mount it and answer it. `pnpm verify preview` opens `/forms/rsvp` over a seeded RSVP at 390 and 1440 in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: the title and its four fields, no bar and no link, nothing sideways, the guest on the store as "a visitor via the RSVP form" with a visitor minted per page, the keyboard on "Received", and none of the records already there drawn on the page or in anything the server sent it. The `graview-visitor-forms` skill says the declaration, the grant and the mount, and `graview-permissions` points at it. A visitor's form alone is a new bundle budget, set at 373_310 / 143_118 (380_000 / 146_000), with none of the bar, the notices or the first look (the frame's store and host scheme are a module of their own, `frame-light.ts`). A hosted page carries 0.2 KB more up front (232 bytes, 87 gzipped: the document's `forms` shape and "a visitor via a form"), and the compiled page 0.1 KB (88 bytes, 45 gzipped), under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-form-for-a-visitor-a-hosted-page*.json`); the pages face alone 93 bytes over its budget minified, none gzipped, under a raise (`a-form-for-a-visitor-pages-face.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive for the declaration and the document format — `forms` is a new optional key, which a build before this refuses in a document as a key it does not know; the `form-*` check codes are new. Additive in the API: `@graview/embed/form` (`mount`, `FormEmbed`, `FormEmbedOptions`, `FormEmbedHandle`), `@graview/pages/visitor-form` (`VisitorFormPage`), `formVisitor`, `visitorForm`, `formReceived`, `visitorSeesKind`, `pickersIn`, `VISITOR_ROLE`, `VisitorForm` on `@graview/core/forms`, `VisitorFormDeclaration` and `GraviewApp.forms`, `themeBaseCss` on `@graview/primitives/pages`. The wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged: a visitor is a seat like any other, said in the seat headers a host already believes.
- 7814c9f: A record page with a page slot and a view of its own says where each is drawn. Threat Watch, on Graview Cloud, gave a kind page blocks ("Read the sources", set with `set-view slot: "page"`) and a record view, and `describe_place` said neither the slot nor any sign of it, while on a kind with no view the slot headed the page. On the routed face the order is one and is now written down: the view, then the slot's blocks, then the facts they did not say — and the facts did say again what the slot said, since the view carried none of the slot's `says`; `registerWorkerView` now hands it on (FR-141), so the record page leaves those out under a view as it does without one. A view whose manifest says `replaces: "page"` is the whole page, as before, and the slot is never drawn. `describePlace` takes the host's views (`DescribePlaceOptions.views`, each a `DescribedHostView`: its manifest's `name`, `title`, `attach`, `cardinality` and `replaces`, and the `parts` a headless run drew), and a record's page whose kind has a view of one says a new part, `{ t: "view" }`, at its head, then the slot, then the facts; where the view replaces the page it says the view alone, is drawn by `view:<name>`, and says a problem at `views.<kind>.page`: the slot is never drawn. `shadowedPageSlots(app, views)` on `@graview/core/describe` says the same problems for a host's list beside `graview check`'s, which does not see the host's views. `graview-view-specs` says where a page slot draws beside a view and what `replaces` does to it, and `graview-worker-view` says the same in a line. `core`'s tests describe a record with a view that sits above the page, with none, and with one that replaces it; `guest`'s draw each on the pages face and find the slot under the view and over the facts, a fact the slot said not said again, and no slot under a view that replaces the page; `verify-address` draws both at 390 and 1440 (aRecordPagesSlotIsDrawnUnderItsViewAndAboveTheFacts, aViewThatReplacesThePageDrawsAloneAndItsSlotIsNot).
