The guest host registering a worker view carries 71 bytes more (23 gzipped), under a raise of its budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-records-page-slot-is-drawn-under-its-view-guest-host.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the API — `DescribePlaceOptions.views`, `DescribedHostView` and `shadowedPageSlots`; a `DescribedPart` may now be `{ t: "view" }`, said only when the host passes its views, so a description asked for as before reads as before. A behavior change on the record page under a view that sits above the page: a fact the kind's page blocks say is not listed again among the facts. The manifest, the declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- 22e7c65: A worker view links out to an address it was handed: a record's own `url`, its contact's `mailto:`, its `tel:`. Roadmap `feature:views-can-t-draw-an-external-link-even-to-a-record-s-own-url-field`, filed from Threat Watch by Graview Cloud: `<a href="https://…">` was refused ("<a> does not take href") and a record's URL drew as text. An `<a href>` is drawn now where its address — `https:`, `mailto:` or `tel:`, as the URL parser says it — is a value of a record the view was shown, and, where the host lists origins for links (`links: { origins: ["https://nvd.nist.gov"] }` on `mountWorkerView`, a registered view's definition and `runWorkerViewHeadless`), any `https:` address on one of them. The host draws every `<a>` to open in a new tab, `rel="noopener noreferrer"` and `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"`, set before its address and never the view's to change, so the page is never navigated, the tab it opens has no hold on it and is told nothing of it; and it marks a link out with an arrow after it whose alternative text, " (opens in a new tab)", is in the link's accessible name. A press on one is not also taken by the face around the view for a press on the card. Anything else — an address the view built, `javascript:`, `data:`, `http:`, a password in it — is drawn with no `href` and said once, in the view's console and to the host's problems: "The link "…" was not drawn: it is no address this view was handed. A view links out only to an address a record it was shown holds (a url field), as the record holds it; a record or a place of this app is <a data-record> or <a data-place>.", or that a view links only to an https:, mailto: or tel: address. A headless run judges a link by the same code over the records it showed the view, and says the same sentence among its problems.
  
  Why handed and not any `https:` address: a view reads what the seat sees and can write any string, so a link it made is a way out of the app — `https://elsewhere.example/?d=` and whatever it read, one press away, however the link is dressed. An address the view was handed carries nothing it chose but which one it links. A list of origins is the host's choice for its place, as `images.origins` is; a manifest cannot widen it, since the manifest is the view author's own word.
  
  `guest`'s tests draw, on a page, a record's `https:` url, `mailto:` and `tel:` as links opening in a new tab with no opener and no referrer; refuse the handed url with what the view read added, an address elsewhere, an unlisted origin, `javascript:`, `data:` and `http:`, each said in its sentence; draw any `https:` address on a listed origin and no other; and find a headless run saying exactly the page's sentences. `guest-sandbox --transport=open` draws a view's links to the car's own page and its dealer's address in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, and inside Claude's widget: drawn to open in a new tab with no opener and no referrer and an arrow after them, each a link in the accessibility tree whose name says it opens in a new tab; the car's page with what the view read added and a `javascript:` link drawn with no `href` and written down as refused; a press opening the address in a new tab whose `window.opener` is null and whose `document.referrer` is empty, the page staying where it was; and the same view run headless refusing the same two links in the same words and nothing else. The `graview-view-drawing` skill says what a link out may be and why, `graview-worker-view` points at it, and the guest README says it. The guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit carries 2.4 KB more (946 bytes gzipped) and registering one 30 bytes (11 gzipped), each under a raise that says so (`scripts/budget-raises/a-view-links-out-to-what-it-was-handed-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive. `links` is a new optional key on `mountWorkerView`'s options, a worker view's definition and `runWorkerViewHeadless`'s options, `HeadlessInput.linkOrigins` a new optional field, and `<a href>` a new attribute the open kit draws where it was refused; a view that drew an `href` before now draws it where it was handed it. The wire and the stored formats are unchanged.
- 0ffa768: A worker view may be drawn edge to edge on a phone. Threat Watch, on Graview Cloud, put a hero at the head of its home and of each record page, and at 390 pixels the page's column stood 20px of gutter either side of it: 40 pixels of 390 the view could not reach, and its `margin: 0 -14px` was clipped at its box's edge, cutting the hero's rounded corners rather than widening it. A manifest now says `bleed: true` (`WorkerViewManifest.bleed`, which `checkManifest` takes as true or left out), and where the pages face draws that view — the home's body, a record's page — on a face 680 pixels wide or less, its box takes the gutter back, less the device's safe areas (`env(safe-area-inset-left)` and `-right`), so it is the phone's width; at a desk it is the column's width, as every view is. Nothing else moves: a view without it is drawn inside the gutter as before, and what any view draws past its box is still clipped there. `registerWorkerView` marks the view (`markBleeds`, `bleeds` and `BLEEDS` beside `markReplacesPage` on `@graview/react` and `/provider`), the pages draw it in `Bleed`, the column's sides are `var(--graview-page-gutter, 20px)` so the gutter and what a view takes back are one number, and the column says `data-graview-page-main`. `graview-view-drawing` says where a view's box is and what `bleed` changes, and `graview-worker-view` names the key. `guest`'s tests find a view that bleeds drawn in a box that takes the gutter back on a phone and any other view as it was, and the key refused as anything but true; `verify-address` mounts the tasks as Cloud does with a home view and a record view that bleed, and at 390 each spans the viewport less its safe areas with nothing scrolling sideways, at 1440 the column's width (aViewThatBleedsSpansAPhoneLessItsSafeAreasAndTheColumnAtADesk). The pages face carries 722 bytes more (191 gzipped), a hosted page 0.2 KB more up front (186 bytes, 49 and 37 gzipped, handed a compiled app or not), the guest host registering a worker view 288 (70) and drawing one on the open kit 122 (22), under raises of those budgets by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-view-that-bleeds-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: additive in the manifest — `bleed`, optional; a manifest without it draws as it did, and a build that predates it passes it over and draws the view inside the gutter. Additive in the API: `markBleeds`, `bleeds`, `BLEEDS`, `Bleed`'s attribute `data-graview-bleed` and the column's `data-graview-page-main`. The declaration, the wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.
- fccd1d4: A worker view's every stylesheet is drawn, in the order it gave them. Roadmap `feature:views-a-second-graview-style-call-silently-drops-the-first-stylesheet`, filed from Threat Watch by Graview Cloud: a view that called `graview.style` twice — the app's shared design-system sheet, then its own — drew with only the second, and nothing said the first was gone. Each call now adds its sheet after the ones before; the runtime sends the host the whole list (`{ type: "style", sheets }`), and the host draws every sheet, judged each on its own as one sheet always was, so a sheet left open swallows nothing after it, and all of them held to the one 64 000-character budget between them, a sheet past it refused for its size with the ones before it drawn. A sheet given again is the sheet already there, so a view that styles on every push adds nothing and sends nothing. A headless run draws the same sheets the page does: its transcript holds `sheets`, and `drawTranscript` judges them as the page does.
  
  `guest`'s tests send two sheets through the view's runtime and find both in the message, a sheet given again sent once, the page drawing the first sheet's rules ahead of the second's, a sheet left open not swallowing the next, a sheet past the shared budget refused for its size, and a headless run's sheets drawn to exactly what the page draws. `guest-sandbox --transport=open` draws its showcase from two sheets in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox and finds both drawn, the second after the first. The `graview-view-drawing` and `graview-worker-view` skills and the guest README say it. The guest host registering a worker view carries 43 bytes more (5 gzipped) and drawing one on the open kit 284 (80 gzipped), each under a raise that says so (`scripts/budget-raises/a-views-every-stylesheet-is-drawn-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: the wire changes — the `style` message a view's runtime sends is `{ type: "style", nonce, sheets: string[] }` where it was `{ css: string }`, and a host from before this drops a runtime's `style` from after it unread (the runtime and the host ship together in `@graview/guest`, so a page that loads both from one version is whole). In the API, `HeadlessTranscript.css` is `sheets`, `drawTranscript`'s second argument is the list of sheets, and a guest host's `onStyle` takes the list. A view's source is unchanged: one call draws as it did.
- d151e35: A worker view's own accessible controls are held to drawing alike on the page and in a headless run, states and all. Roadmap `feature:views-aria-checked-on-a-span-makes-the-browser-fall-back-to-the-plain-list-while-the-headless-run-passes`, filed from Threat Watch by Graview Cloud: a `<span class="box" role="checkbox" aria-checked="true">` ran headless ("It runs") and the page drew the kind's own list in its place until `aria-checked` came out. The open kit has taken every `aria-*` attribute and every role but the app's chrome's and notices' since it was made, and the page and the run draw through the one renderer and judge (`host/open-render.ts`, `host/open-judge.ts`), the page into a document and the run into a tree of plain objects; what is new is that this is held, and in a real browser. On this version the filed markup draws, ticks and is spoken in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, so the stop Threat Watch saw is not in the drawing: a kept view that fails is replaced by the kind's own face and says why only through the definition's `onFailure (reason, detail)`, which is where the host learns it.
  
  `guest`'s tests send a view's records for a checkbox, a mixed one, a switch, a pressed toggle, a disclosure with `aria-controls`, a tab list and its panel, a radio group, a slider, a progress bar, a current link, a listbox with an active descendant, a tree item, a menu item checkbox, a sorted grid, a field with an error message and a polite live text through the view's own runtime, and find every state and property and every role kept on the page; the run's tree the same element for element and attribute for attribute; the app's notices and chrome (`role="alert"`, `role="status"`, `aria-live="assertive"`, a named section) refused alike on both sides and said as problems; and the view run headless with none. `guest-sandbox --transport=open` draws Threat Watch's checkbox beside a switch, a toggle, a disclosure, tabs, a radio group and a slider in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: drawn as the view drew it, never stopped or replaced, its `[aria-checked=true]` rule styling it; each control said with its state in the engine's accessibility tree; a press ticking it again; and the same view run headless drawing attribute for attribute what the page drew. The `graview-view-drawing` skill and the guest README say how to build such a control.
  
  Compatibility: no change to the wire, the stored formats or the API; what the open kit draws is as it was.
- bbde29f: Any element a worker view binds to an act is pressed as a button is, and a headless run presses one and says what it would send. The guide said "a data-act button", and Graview Cloud's Threat Watch drew `<span role="button" data-act>` inside a fieldset: whether the host honored it, by a tap or by the keyboard, was found out only on the page, and `propose_view` could not press anything. Now it is decided and said. Any element with `data-act` is a press — a `<span role="button">`, a `<div>`, a badge, an SVG `<g>` — pressed by a click on it or on anything inside it, and a phone's tap is a click. One that does not press of itself is given the keyboard (`tabindex="0"`), is a button to assistive technology unless it says another role, is drawn with a pointer and `touch-action: manipulation` (`data-graview-press`, looked at again after every batch the view draws), and Enter or Space on it presses it, Space without scrolling the page; a button's own Enter, which clicks it already, is not a second press. Three things are never pressed: an `<option>`, a `<label>` (a press on it presses its field too, and would ask twice), and a field to type into or pick from that does not save (a click into it to type was taken for a press); a headless run says each among its problems, in a sentence naming what to do instead.
  
  `runWorkerViewHeadless(…, { press: "<selector>" })`, or `{ press: { selector, typed: { note: "…" } } }`, presses the first element the selector matches once the view has drawn, as `graview.on` matches one, and answers `pressed` beside the description, judged in the host's context by the live host's own rules over what the view drew before the press: the link it lands in and where that goes, or why it goes nowhere; the act a bound press asks for with the arguments it would carry — the bound record, the manifest's constants, the fields in its fieldset, typed only where `typed` says and otherwise as the view left them or as the host would prefill them — and whether this seat's store would apply it, rehearsed with `previewAll` and never applied, with its sentence, or the refusal in the words the view would hear (`refused`, `untyped`, `undeclared` …); and what the view's own code did on hearing the click: the acts it asked for, where it asked to go, and whether it drew again; with all of it said in `pressed.says`. A selector that matches nothing, or a link that goes nowhere, is a problem of the description. `graview view check view.js … --press "<selector>" [--type name=value]…` prints the same. `describe_place` and `propose_view` can check a press before a view ships.
  
  `guest`'s tests drive the host's press wiring over Threat Watch: a `<span role="button">` in a fieldset, a `<div>` and an SVG `<g>` bound to acts are given the keyboard and a button's role, and pressed by a click inside them carrying the words typed beside them, by Enter and by Space; a button's Enter is not pressed twice; a select and a label are never pressed. A headless run presses a card's "Acknowledge" span and says the act and the record it would carry, applied for the analyst and refused in the policy's words for a stranger, nothing applied; a press on a card's heading opens the card's record; the note typed is carried and an untyped one is not; a toggle's press is the view's own and it drew again, the description being of what it drew before; a selector that matches nothing is a problem; and the describer says a label, an option and a field that does not save are never pressed. `node scripts/guest-sandbox.mjs --transport=taps` holds, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, that the span is a press the host made, that Space on a card's "Acknowledge" acknowledges the threat, the view hearing it and the card not opening, and that Enter on a fieldset's "Save the note" saves the note typed beside it; and on a phone in Chromium and WebKit, that a tap on "Acknowledge" acknowledges it and opens nothing and a tap on "Save the note" saves it. `graview-view-forms` says what can be pressed and how to press it headless. The guest host drawing a worker view on the open kit carries 1.7 KB more (1_693 bytes, 784 gzipped), under a raise of that budget (`scripts/budget-raises/an-element-bound-to-an-act-is-pressed-as-a-button-is-open-kit.json`); a worker view's runtime 82 bytes, within its budget; nothing a hosted page loads up front grew.
  
  Compatibility: the wire between a view and its host is unchanged — a press is still the person's click, or now their Enter or Space, judged by the host. A view's elements change: an element other than a button bound to an act is given `tabindex="0"` and, where it has no meaning of its own, `role="button"`; a `select`, a text field or a `textarea` with `data-act` and no `data-save`, a `label` and an `option` with `data-act` are no longer pressed by a click (give a field `data-save`, or bind a button). Additive in the API: `press` on `runWorkerViewHeadless`'s options, `pressed` (`HeadlessPress`) on its answer, `judgeHeadlessPress`, `press` on `HeadlessInput` and `pressed` on `HeadlessTranscript` (a host that runs its own isolate passes the input through as ever), `find` on the view runtime, and `graview view check --press` and `--type`. The stored formats, the declaration and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.44

### Patch Changes

- 10ea092: A view the host keeps is a place in the nav, and a kind kept off the places leaves the Pages drop-down. Roadmap q-custom-views-in-nav, filed from the hosted En Dash Social Media Playbook: its "To do" is a script view a chat drew and the host keeps by its manifest, and it showed only as an "Also as" link on the tasks list — `arrange-pages` refused it in `order` ('"to-do" is not a kind this app has') and in `first` ('"To do" is not a place, a kind or "home"'), since the declaration cannot know a host's views. The declaration now names one as `view:<its manifest name>` in `pages.order`, `pages.first` and `pages.primary`. Where the host hands its views' manifests to the edit (`EditOptions.views`, `HostView`: `name`, `title`, `attach`), `arrange-pages` takes the view's title or name and writes `view:<name>` (`order: ["To do"]` is `["view:to-do"]`), and refuses a name the host does not keep, naming the ones it does; unsaid, it takes `view:<name>` on the host's word and says "(once the host registers it)". `graview check` and the checked `compileDocument` (`CheckOptions.views`, `CompileOptions.views`) say a `view:` name no view registered here has is a note, `pages-view-not-drawn-here` — not drawn here yet, it stands where it is named once the host registers it — and, told the host's views, a name none of them is a warning, `pages-view-unknown`. On the page, the bar's places list a registered view `pages.order` names among the Lists at its place in the order (`arrangedEntries`), standing on a wide bar unless `pages.primary` says others; `pages.first` opens on it (`openingOf` reads `view:<name>` by the registration's name); the home's gallery puts it first. `placesOf(app, { views })` lists the host's titled pictures of a kind, each with `view: <name>`, where the order names them and `first` where `pages.first` does — what a host lists when an app is opened, and the places a drawn view's `data-place` is judged against.
  
  And a kind `pages.hide` names really leaves the Pages drop-down: it was dropped from the home only and folded into More, so the playbook's eleven reference lists made the drop-down "insanely long". It is listed only while it is the place you are on, so the bar still says where you are, and it still opens at its address, in Find and from wherever it is linked. `set-lens-hidden` on a kind's name or plural (`title: "source"`) writes `pages.hide` where no lens has that title, refused where `pages.primary` stands the kind; the place menu's Hide on a kind's list, for whoever may change the declaration, hands the host's `keptViews.lens` that edit, and the drop-down's Hidden lists each kept-off list with Show. `arrange-pages` and the preview of a change say "the places and the front page leave off …". `core`'s tests name a view by title and by `view:<name>`, with and without the host's views, refuse the rest in words, check the note and the warning, list and open the view, and hide and show a kind's list; `pages`' render the drop-down with the view first among the lists and without the hidden kinds, and the hidden list at its address; `embed`'s hide a list from the place menu and show one from Hidden. `pnpm verify address` mounts the tasks as Cloud's shell does with a script view registered as `to-do` and `pages: { order: ["view:to-do", "task"], first: "view:to-do", hide: ["person"] }`: the bare address opens on it, it stands first after Home, and the people are not listed and still open at `/people`. A hosted page carries 0.9 KB more up front (903 bytes, about 340 gzipped; the compiled page the same), the bar's arranged list and the hidden kinds' Show, under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/a-kept-view-is-a-place-in-the-nav-*.json`); the pages face alone 1.4 KB more (1_479, 572 gzipped), and the scene face's before-draw line is 964 KB.
  
  Compatibility: additive for the declaration — `pages.order`, `pages.first` and `pages.primary` may name `view:<name>`, which a build before 0.1.44 reads as a kind or place it does not have and passes over (`graview check` warned of it), and `arrange-pages` takes a title or `view:<name>` in `order` where it took only kinds. A behavior change for `pages.hide`: a kind it names is kept off the bar's places as well as the home. `set-lens-hidden` takes a kind where no lens has the title, where it refused. Additive in the API: `HostView`, `arrangedEntries`, `viewReference`, `ArrangedEntry`, `EditOptions.views`, `CompileOptions.views`, `CheckOptions` and `checkApp`'s second argument, `placesOf`'s `views`, `AppPlace.view`, `BarPlace.hidden`; `arrangementFindings` takes `{ views }` where it took a list of places. The wire, the stored formats and the derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.43

### Patch Changes

- 3c6b0fa: An op carries its value once less: op format 2 keeps no inverse. Roadmap f-ops-without-inverse-on-the-wire: an op wrote what it changed in the call's arguments, in its primitives' `before` and `after`, and again in an `inverse` that was nothing but those primitives turned round — so a 19 KB picture changed in a text field was a 95 KB op, and most of why a heavy hosted app's log ran to 4.9 MB. The inverse is now worked out from the primitives where it is read, `inverseOf(op)`, remembered per op, and read by undo, a selective undo, a rebase's rollback, playback, verifying a fold and the seat's view of a withheld op alike; every primitive's inverse is another primitive, so nothing it needs was missing. `FORMATS.op` is 2. On the Beatles app with a 19 KB cover on each of its 18 releases, each cover changed once and 200 plain edits (`node scripts/op-bytes.mjs`), a cover's change is 57.4 KB where it was 95.5 KB, an added cover 38.4 KB where it was 57.5 KB, an undo of a change 38.4 KB where it was 76.5 KB, and the whole log 1.96 MB where it was 3.17 MB (38% less); with 479 covers, 72.6 MB comes to 45.5 MB. What is left twice is the value in the call and in the patch, which a rebase plays again and a sync sends on, and the value it replaced, which undo puts back.
  
  A format-1 log opens on this build as it did, and nothing on disk is rewritten to open it: a store drops an inverse that is its primitives' own from every op it opens on, receives or rebases onto (`bareOp`, the `upgradeOp` step from 1), keeps one that is not (a host could once hand `append` its own), and its file adapter's compaction writes what it keeps in format 2. An export is stamped `op: 2` and its ops carry no inverse; a bundle from before imports and undoes the same. The live wire carries format 2 as an addition to protocol 1: `openRemote` says it reads it — `opFormat` in its hello, `?opFormat=2` on `/graview/state` and `/graview/since`, `opFormat` in the body it posts — and is sent ops bare; a client that says nothing, as every one before 0.1.43, is sent each op with its inverse written out (`opsFor`), so an open tab on the last build goes on undoing and playing back against this server. A host that routes its own requests passes `WireAsked.opFormat` (`opFormatOf(url)` reads the query). `core`'s tests fold, undo one batch at a time, take back an early batch with what depends on it and redo it, play back at every seq, verify a fold and find where it parts, rebase and receive with the same log in both formats and get the same graph each time, and open 0.1.0's format-1 fixture and go on; `ship`'s open a 0.1.42 store on disk, undo, append and reopen it, and serve both readers on every route and the socket. A hosted page carries 0.5 KB more up front (515 bytes, about 450 gzipped; the compiled page the same), the derived inverse, the format-1 inverse dropped and the format said on the wire, under a raise of each budget by that (`scripts/budget-raises/ops-carry-their-value-once-*.json`), and the scene face's before-draw line is 962 KB.
  
  Compatibility: breaking for ops and stored formats, before 1.0 — `Operation.inverse` is optional and this build writes none (read it with `inverseOf`), and `AppendOp.inverse` is gone; op format 2, so a build before 0.1.43 opening a store this one has opened, or importing a bundle it exported, refuses it with `NewerFormatError` ("Upgrade the framework to read it") rather than undo an op it finds no inverse on, and a host rolling a store back writes its log out with `withInverse` under `op: 1`. Additive on the wire: `hello.opFormat`, `?opFormat=` and a post's `opFormat` are fields a server before 0.1.43 ignores, sending format 1, which this build reads; a client that says none is sent format 1 as before. `WIRE_PROTOCOL` and the subprotocol do not move, and the declaration and derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.42

### Patch Changes

- a5bbda0: A brand's logo has one for each scheme. Nick, building the TrueNorth recon app: the brand took an emoji or one mark, a client's real logo (a JPG on white) had nowhere else to go, and on a dark theme it sat as a white slab — the app drew it under `filter: invert(1) hue-rotate(180deg)` and `mix-blend-mode: screen` of its own. `brand.logo` is still a string, or now `{ light, dark? }`: the light one in the light scheme, the dark one in the dark, the light one there too where there is none. Whether a light logo is opaque on white cannot be known from it, so inverting it is asked for rather than guessed: `{ light, darkFallback: "invert" }` draws it inverted and screened onto the ground in the dark scheme alone, and beside a `dark` one it is never used, which `graview check` says (`brand-logo`, a warning at `brand.logo.darkFallback`). In a document the same object takes `alt` (`{ "src", "alt" }` and a string still mean one logo), each mark is judged as the one was (`brand-mark` at `brand.logo.light` or `brand.logo.dark`), and `set-brand` takes `logo: { light, dark }` and says "The logo gets one of its own for the dark scheme." (or "The logo is drawn inverted in the dark scheme.", "The logo is the same in both schemes."), as the preview of a change does. A declared app's logo is checked too now: a pair with no light one, a fallback other than `"invert"` (`brand-logo`), and a mark that could act (`brand-mark`). Contrast checks are on the colors, as before.
  
  The default shell's bar and a design's own draw it with `AppMark` (`@graview/primitives`, handed the brand on `context.brand`): both marks are in the page, each `data-graview-scheme-only="light"` or `"dark"`, and the theme's sheet for the scheme the page is in hides the other's — so the logo switches with the scheme however the page chose it, with no hook — and a light one the brand asked to invert carries `data-graview-logo-fallback="invert"`, which only the dark sheet styles. `logoOf(brand)` and `logoFor(brand, scheme)` (`{ src, invert }`) on `@graview/core` are for a design that draws its own image. The static page's masthead does the same; a worker view is handed the logo its scheme draws. `graview-logo`, a skill of its own, says all of it: `graview-brand` gives its marks to it, split along that seam rather than shaved. A hosted page carries 1.5 KB more up front for it (1_541 bytes, 509 gzipped; the compiled page 1.4 KB, 1_461 / 482), under a raise of each budget by that; the scene face before it draws 1_541 bytes more, the pages face alone 982 / 337, the embed without the studio 987 / 318, every face 4_241 / 1_439, the embed with the studio 4_161 / 1_421, and the guest host 80 bytes before a worker is drawn and on the open kit, each under a raise of its budget by what it measured.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged. The declaration: `brand.logo` gains a third form, `{ light, dark?, alt?, darkFallback?: "invert" }`, and every document that compiled still compiles the same; the TypeScript `Brand.logo` is `string | BrandLogo`, so code that read it as a string reads `logoOf(brand)?.light`. Check finding codes: new `brand-logo` (an error for a declared app's pair with no light one or a fallback it does not know, a warning for a fallback beside a dark one), and `brand-mark` now also at `brand.logo.light` and `brand.logo.dark`, and for a declared app's logo. New on `@graview/core`: `logoOf`, `logoFor` and the type `BrandLogo`. The `set-brand` edit takes `logo: { light, dark?, alt?, darkFallback? }`. What Graview Cloud does: `add_image` each file as now, then `{"op": "set-brand", "logo": {"light": "/graview/assets/<sha>.jpg", "dark": "/graview/assets/<sha>.png"}}` (or `"darkFallback": "invert"` with a light one alone, when the person says the logo is a picture on white); a page that draws its own masthead uses `AppMark` or `logoFor`, and a stylesheet it writes itself for a scheme hides `[data-graview-scheme-only]` of the other.
- 4fe0969: A page says where you are. Nick, from building a product face with its own shell over the pages: "people got caught in the derived pages and didn't know where they were". On a step's page nothing said "Plan › Steps › Consolidate", no tab lit, and the app rebuilt a trail by matching `pluralSlug` against the path and reading the record's label. Each derived list, record and picture now opens with a trail: a `nav` called "You are here", the section the kind's pages are in, the kind's list as a link, and the page itself last with `aria-current="page"`, said to a screen reader and kept out of sight because the heading under it says it. It stands on one line at a phone's width, a long step cut short rather than wrapped, and its hrefs carry the face's base. It takes the place of the record page's kind link above the name; a list with no section has nothing before its heading and draws none. `registry.section(kinds, label, { path })` names the section ("Plan" over change plans and their steps; without a `path` the trail says it and links nowhere) and `registry.sectionOf(kind)` reads it back. A design that draws its own shell asks `usePlaceHere()`: `{ as: "home" | "list" | "record" | "place" | "page", kind, id, label, section, trail: [{ label, href, path, current }] }`, to light its own tab and draw its own title. A bar outside the face's router, told the path by `steering` or `onNavigate`, reads `placeHere(context, path, { registry, base })`. A shell that places `<PageTrail />` holds THE trail, and the derived pages leave theirs to it; `surface("shell", Shell, { without: ["trail"] })` goes without. Seedbed's garden puts plots and plantings in "The garden", and its own plot page places `<PageTrail />` where its eyebrow was. The product-design rung of `graview-pages` is a skill of its own now, `graview-pages-design`, which teaches the section, the hook and the trail. A hosted page carries nothing more up front; the pages face before it draws is about 3 KB more, and the pages face alone, every face and the embed with the studio handed in are raised by what they measured (3_330 / 1_219, 3_288 / 1_195 and 3_299 / 1_207). `verify-pages` holds that a record's page says where it is, its trail's link goes to the kind's list under `/pages`, and a planting's page says "The garden › Plantings", at 390 and 1440, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. Additive on the pages' API (`PageTrail`, `usePlaceHere`, `placeHere`, `PlaceHere`, `PlaceStep`, `PageSection`, `registry.section`, `registry.sectionOf`) and on `FaceControl` (`"trail"`). On the markup, the record page's kind link above its name is now the list step of `nav[aria-label="You are here"]` (`[data-graview-trail]`), and a list or a picture in a named section opens with one.
- 5a68fac: A worker view hears a wheel, a drag and a pinch. TrueNorth's recon app ran its architecture map as a worker view, and it could select and open a zone but never pan or zoom: only clicks, typing, choices and a drop crossed into the worker. A view whose manifest says `gestures: true` now hears `graview.on("wheel" | "drag" | "pinch", selector, (e) => …)` over what the selector matches. Every gesture carries `x` and `y`, where the pointer is in CSS pixels from the top left of the matched element, and that element's `width` and `height`, since a worker has no layout to ask. A `wheel` carries `deltaX`, `deltaY`, `deltaMode`, `ctrlKey` and `metaKey`. A `drag`, from `pointerdown`, `pointermove` and `pointerup` with the pointer captured, carries `phase` (`start`, `move`, `end`), `pointerId`, `button`, `pressure`, and `dx` and `dy` since the view last heard of that pointer. A `pinch` carries `phase`, `scale` (the factor since the view last heard) and `rotation`; it comes from Safari's `gesturestart`, `gesturechange` and `gestureend`, or from a trackpad's pinch, which is a wheel with Control held. The host sends at most one of each an animation frame with the deltas between summed, and a drag's start and end at once. It calls `preventDefault` on a wheel or a gesture only over an element a view that opted in listens on, so the page still scrolls and zooms everywhere else. A view that listens for a gesture without the flag is told in its console, and `graview view check` (`runWorkerViewHeadless`) says so too, as it does a flag with nothing to hear. `checkManifest` refuses a `gestures` that is not true or false. The graview-worker-view skill teaches it, with `examples/a-map-that-pans-and-zooms.js`, an SVG map whose viewBox a wheel and a pinch zoom around the pointer and a drag pans. `pnpm verify guest` now drives that map through `--transport=gestures` in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. The host's gestures are a chunk of their own, 5.9 KB (2.4 KB gzipped), fetched only for a view that opted in and listens for one. The guest host carries about 0.2 KB more up front, the session handing on where a view listens, and a worker view's runtime 0.3 KB more. A hosted page's first load is unchanged.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire: a worker view's runtime sends `{ type: "gestures", nonce, bound: [{ event, selector }, …] }` whenever where it listens for a wheel, a drag or a pinch moves, and a view whose manifest says `gestures: true` is sent `dom-event`s whose `event` is `wheel`, `drag` or `pinch`, carrying the fields above. A host of an earlier version drops `gestures` unread, and a view that does not listen hears nothing new. The manifest: `gestures` is new and optional. A manifest without it is read as before, and its view hears no gesture. What Graview Cloud does: accept `gestures: true` in a proposed view's manifest and teach it in its views guide; nothing changes in how it mounts a view.
- 3aff737: A worker view's code is fetched only when the view is drawn. Measured on Graview Cloud on 2026-10-20, on a phone, the Beatles demo fetched all seven of its custom views' sources before the page mounted, though its home drew one: `WorkerViewDefinition.worker` took the code itself, so a host had to have every view's code in hand to register any. It now also takes a function answering the code (`WorkerViewLoad`, `() => Promise<WorkerViewCode>`), and `mountWorkerView`'s `worker` likewise. The manifest is what registers the view — as a place, in the place menu and wherever a manifest is read — so registering asks nothing; the function is asked when the view is first drawn, beside the worker's host rather than after it, once however often the view is drawn, and again after it rejected. What arrives is judged as handed code is (too long, or loading something, it is refused `source`); code that never arrives is refused `source` in words, "Its code did not arrive: 404 Not Found.", and the kind's own face is drawn in its place, so `data-graview-drawn` still comes. The guest README and `graview-worker-view` say how a host hands it. `guest`'s test registers two views with no code asked, draws one and finds only its code asked, asks once for two drawings, refuses code too long once it arrives and code that never does, and asks again after a refusal. A hosted page carries nothing more up front; the guest host registering a view is 185 / 84 bytes more and drawing one on the open kit 311 / 152, minified and gzipped (`scripts/budget-raises/heavy-apps-open-fast-*.json`).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. Additive on `@graview/guest/host/views` and `@graview/guest/host/worker`: `WorkerViewDefinition.worker` and `MountWorkerViewOptions.worker` take `WorkerViewCode | WorkerViewLoad`, and `WorkerViewLoad` is exported.
- 6809e5a: Undo and Find stand at a design bar's own height. Nick, building a product face with its own bar (the TrueNorth recon app): placed in it, `<PageUndo>` said "Take back “…the whole act's words…”" at whatever width that took and pushed Find to a sliver, and both carried inline styles the design could beat only with `!important` — the app replaced Undo's words with a CSS `::after`. `<PageUndo compact />` is the arrow and "Undo", with what it takes back as its accessible name and in its title; uncompact, its sentence is cut at 18rem (`--graview-undo-widest`) and still whole in its title. Both take `className` (on Undo's button, on Find's form; its box is `.graview-find-box`) and `size` — `"sm"` (30 px, the bar's tools), `"md"` (36 px), or a height as a number of pixels or a CSS length, said as `--graview-control-height` — and what they look like is now rules behind `:where` a design's one class outweighs, not a style attribute. Placed nowhere, each stands as before: Find at 30 px, the way back at 36. `graview-pages-design` says so: graview-pages had no room left under its limit, so its last rung — a product design over every surface — is a skill of its own, split along its seam, with Find and the way back in the design's bar beside it; 27 skills now. The pages face alone carries 0.5 KB more up front (482 bytes, 192 gzipped) and the embed with the studio the same (482 / 187), every face 1.4 KB in all (1_390 / 576), each under a raise of its budget by that; a hosted page is unchanged.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. New on `@graview/pages`: `compact`, `className` and `size` on `PageUndo`, `className` and `size` on `PageFind`, and the types `ControlSize` and `UndoLook`. The placed way back's markup is additive: `.graview-undo` on its button, `.graview-undo-place` on its box, `data-compact` and `data-size` where said; Find's form is `.graview-find` and its box `.graview-find-box`, with no style attribute on either. A shell that placed `PageUndo` sees a long sentence cut at 18rem where it stood uncut.

## 0.1.41

## 0.1.40

### Patch Changes

- 749a684: A store too large for the browser's localStorage is kept, in IndexedDB. WebKit keeps 5 MB of localStorage an origin and counts each character twice, so the gauntlet's 2.8 MB seed did not fit in Safari: since the last release the store opened and reported the write that failed, and a reload had forgotten every edit. `createBrowserAdapter()` now keeps the snapshot, the log and the meta in IndexedDB (the `entries` store of the `graview` database, under the keys localStorage used), and the log in pieces, one per write, so an edit appends what it did rather than writing the whole log again; a log read in more than 32 pieces is put back together into one. It is the default rather than a place a full store spills into, so where a store is kept never depends on how large it once grew. The database opens on the first read, not when the adapter is made, and nothing of it is in a hosted page. The gauntlet in WebKit, Chromium and Firefox now reloads with an edit it was given (`aLargeStoreReloadsWithItsEdit`, in `pnpm engines`). The `graview-ship` skill says where the page keeps its store.
  
  Compatibility: ops, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. Stored formats: a page's store moves from localStorage to IndexedDB, and an existing one is carried forward, not lost — the first time a scope is read, what localStorage holds for it is written into IndexedDB in one transaction, and only after that transaction commits is it removed from localStorage. When IndexedDB will not take it, it stays in localStorage and the adapter keeps it there; where IndexedDB will not open at all, the adapter is localStorage as before. A build from before this one, opened after it, finds localStorage empty and starts from the seed; when this build opens again, the store IndexedDB holds stands and what the older build wrote in localStorage is left there untouched. Changed: `BrowserAdapter`'s `loadMeta` and `saveMeta` answer promises (`openStore` awaits either kind), it gains `keptIn()`, and `createBrowserAdapter` takes `indexedDB`; passed `storage` alone, it is that storage only, as a test or a node rehearsal wants. What Graview Cloud does: nothing; a hosted page persists through its server, not this adapter.
- 9a8b622: `graview preview` and `graview serve` stop when they are told to, whoever still has them open. A product ran `graview preview app.json --seed seed.json --data data` behind a Vite dev server that proxied `/graview` to it, live socket and all, with a browser open: `kill <pid>` and `kill -INT` left it running, because closing the server waited on the proxied socket, which the proxy kept open. Only `kill -9` stopped it, and the data folder's `graview.lock` went on naming the dead process. Now SIGTERM, SIGINT or SIGHUP tells every live socket the store is closing (1001), cuts the keep-alive connections, gives a socket that keeps its side open 1.5 s before cutting it too, releases the data folder and exits; a close still waiting after 2.5 s, or a second signal, exits anyway. A hold whose process has gone is taken over as before, and the command now says so: `graview preview: took over ./data from a preview (pid 41234, since …), which is no longer running.` The test starts the built bin, holds a half-open live socket and a keep-alive connection, and sends each signal (`a-preview-stops-when-told.test.ts`). The `graview-ship` skill says how a preview stops.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire: messages are unchanged; `ServedStore.close()` takes `{ grace }` (1500 ms unless said) and now cuts a live socket that has not gone within it, where it used to wait for it forever. `StoreHold` gains `tookOverFrom`.
- 39106b6: `graview sync-seed`'s dry run refuses what `--apply` would refuse, and names every record up front. A product's seed had one chapter at `step: 5` where the declaration says `step` is an integer of at most 4: the dry run printed 146 clean steps and exited 0, and `--apply` then refused all 180 with "… does not match what chapter declares / Step: Too big: expected number to be <=4", one record at a time. The steps' primitives are now applied to a copy of the live graph as the store applies them, and when the batch would fail each step is tried in turn, so the dry run lists every refusal at once — the step (`chapter c3 is put`, `c1 is patched: step`), the record by its label, the field and the declaration's words — says which steps fit, writes nothing and exits 1. `--apply` goes by the same judgment and refuses before it writes, so it refuses nothing the dry run passed. `--json` carries them as `refused: [{ step, record, fields, said }]`. A host reaches the same judgment as `seedRefusals(schema, steps, live)` and its sentences as `sayRefusals`, from the main and runtime entries. The `graview-ship` skill says the dry run refuses.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. `graview sync-seed` exits 1, not 0, on a seed the declaration refuses, dry run or not.
- 04fe262: A change made just before the page navigates or closes is kept. Since the browser adapter moved a store to IndexedDB, a write landed a transaction after `store.apply` returned, so a person who edited and left at once, and the journeys harness that arranged a plot and went to the next page, opened a store without the change. `openStore` now hands every op to the adapter's journal inside the apply, and the browser adapter writes it to localStorage synchronously; the journal holds only the ops IndexedDB has not committed yet, and each is let go of once the log has it and a snapshot that reflects it has. The next open puts back what the journal holds, each op once: the ops the log does not have, by id, are appended to it, and the ops the stored snapshot does not reflect, by seq, are folded onto it. When localStorage refuses even the journal, `onPersistError` is told, and IndexedDB's own write goes on. `pnpm verify journeys` walks seedbed's "Getting from a problem to its repair" jobs again. The `graview-ship` skill says when a page's edit is kept.
  
  Compatibility: ops, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. Stored formats: the browser adapter adds two keys per scope, `<prefix>:<scope>:journal` in localStorage (the ops not yet committed, a JSON array, absent when there are none) and `<prefix>:<scope>:through` beside the snapshot in IndexedDB (the seq of the last op the snapshot reflects); a store kept without `through` reads as reflecting none of the journal, which an older build never wrote. `BrowserAdapter.keysFor` answers both, and the adapter gains `journal` and `loadJournal`, which `openStore` calls on any adapter that has them. What Graview Cloud does: nothing; a hosted page persists through its server, not this adapter.
- 56b88e1: A headline bound to a long field is warned of, and drawn a step smaller. `{"headline": "{call}"}`, where a call is three sentences, filled a focus card at display size: a headline is a few words, drawn large, and nothing said a long text did not belong there. Now `graview check` warns, `view-headline-long`, where a view's headline block binds a long field whole — a document's `text` field, Markdown among them, or a string declared in code longer than 500 characters, as `view-whole-text` reads them — "incident page heads it with call, a long text, drawn at a headline's size; a headline is a few words.", and the fix says it in a text block, `{"text": "{call}"}`, or heads it with `{call|excerpt: 60}`; an excerpt and a short field say nothing. And a headline past 90 characters says so (`data-long` on `.graview-spec-headline`) and is drawn at a heading's size, 1.125rem whatever its level, clamped to four lines, every word still in the page for a reader that does not see it. The `graview-view-specs` skill says so. A hosted page that compiles its document carries 0.4 KB more up front (0.1 KB gzipped), the check's own words, and its scene face before it draws 0.6 KB more; one handed a compiled app carries nothing more.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; every declaration that compiled still compiles. The check finding codes gain `view-headline-long`, a warning. `SPEC_VIEW_CSS` gains `.graview-spec-headline[data-long][data-level]`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing; a view a chat wrote with a long field as its headline is now drawn smaller, and the warning is there for its own check to show.
- 4aa429f: `graview check` warns when a `describe` written in code returns a template (carried from the twelfth walk, a film society). One sentence of the walk's app said `{startsAt | datetime}` to a person, braces and all: a template is filled in only in a document's strings, and a kind's `describe` or `label`, or an act's `describe`, written in code is shown as it returns. The watch caught it on the page and the checker said nothing. Now it warns, `describe-template-in-code` at `defineNode("<kind>").describe` (or `.label`, or `defineMutation("<act>").describe`): the text of the function's strings is read for `{name}` naming a field, relation or computed value of the kind or an argument of the act, and for any `{name | filter}`, and the fix says the value in code — `momentAsRead(node.startsAt)`, `dayAsRead(…)`, or `${…}` in a template literal. The `graview-view-specs` skill says so. The studio carries the checker, so the embed with the studio handed in measures 1.9 KB more (0.2 KB gzipped) and every face 2.1 KB, and their budgets are raised by that; nothing of it is in a hosted page.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; every declaration that compiled still compiles. The check finding codes gain `describe-template-in-code`, a warning about a describe or label in code whose words show braces to a person; a document's strings are not asked.
- 674748e: `graview shots` and `graview preview` picture an app declared in code, as they picture a document (W-245, the thirteenth walk, a camera-trap survey). Both said "a declaration document (a .json file) is required", so the survey, declared in code, could picture its contact sheet — a view proposed and not kept — only over a document written for the purpose, and a document written from the app (`toDocument`) leaves out every act, rule and label that is code. Now both take the app module `graview check` takes (`./dist/domain/app.js`, or any module whose default export or `app` is the app): the store runs the app as Node loaded it, and the page is handed its module bundled for a browser — with the project's esbuild, which a project built with vite has; without one, the command says how to add it — at `/graview/app.js`, in place of the document. The page sits as a person holding every role the app's policy declares, unless `--as` or `--roles` say who; a lens hidden from the place menu is not written back into code, so a code app's lenses are not removed from there. `servePreview` takes `code: { app, bundle }` in place of `document`, and `bundleAppForPage(entry)` makes the bundle. The `graview-host-page` skill says so, and `pnpm verify preview` photographs a code app's draft view by its title.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. `/graview/document` on a preview of an app in code answers `{ module: "/graview/app.js", principal, state, views, draftView? }`, and a document's preview answers as it did. New: `code` on `servePreview`'s options (`document` is now optional, one of the two); `bundleAppForPage`, `findBundler`, `NO_BUNDLER` and `PREVIEW_APP_PATH` on `@graview/ship`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing — its apps are documents.
- 380c236: `graview shots --places` takes a draft view's title, as it takes a kept view's (W-243, the thirteenth walk, a camera-trap survey). `graview shots app.json --view sheet.js --manifest sheet.json --places "Station sheet"` refused the title and listed "home, overview, stations" as the places there were, though the page it would photograph linked "Also as: Station sheet": the places were read from the document alone, and only the path `/places/station-sheet` reached the draft. Now a titled draft is a place of the run, by its title and its address word, listed among the places when a name is not found, and photographed among every place when none is named. `shotsOf` takes the draft's manifest as a fourth argument. The `graview-host-page` skill says so.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. `shotsOf(app, asked, faces, draft?)` takes a draft view's manifest. What Graview Cloud does: nothing more.
- 752da2b: `spatialHref(id, sceneHref = "/")` takes the scene's address, as `placeHref` already did. It answered `/#focus=<id>` whatever the host, so a host whose scene lives at `/scene/places/overview` could not link a page of its own to a record there. Now `spatialHref("plot:north-bed", "/scene/places/overview")` is `/scene/places/overview#focus=plot%3Anorth-bed`, and unsaid it is `/` as before. A project `graview create` writes passes its PagesApp context's `sceneHref` to it. The `@graview/pages` README and the `graview-pages` skill say to set `sceneHref` in the PagesApp context when the scene is not at `/`, and pass it on.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `spatialHref(id)` answers what it did.
- 6fc222b: The scene sits under a host's own bar. A product face with its own shell wanted the scene in it: `mount(stage, { face: "scene", bar: true })` worked, but its bar said the app's name a second time under the product's and offered a Scene | Pages switch whose Pages were Graview's derived pages, not the product's own, so the product hid both with CSS. `bar` now also takes what it leaves out (`EmbedBar`): `bar: { faces: false, name: false }` draws the bar without the switch or the app's mark and name, and keeps the scene's places, Find, the standing (Activity beside it once anything has happened) and the person; either may be said alone. `@graview/embed/pages` takes `bar: { name: false }` the same way. `AppBar` takes `named` (`true` by default), and a bar with no switch on the scene names the scene's places, as one with a switch did. A hosted page carries 0.1 KB more up front for it (143 bytes, 70 gzipped, the compiled page the same), under a raise of each budget by that; the pages face, the embed without the studio, every face and the embed with the studio are raised by what they measured. The embed's README and the graview-embed and graview-host-page skills say it.
  
  A scene at a route of the pages' own registry (`PagesApp` drawing the scene under the host's shell) is not here: the scene face is the embed's, under its `GraviewProvider`, its inspector, its map panel and its rules, and `@graview/pages` may not import the scene; it would take an embed-side page component that wraps the provider over the page context's store and draws the scene face, registered by the host with `registry.route(path, …)`, with the scene's stop kept apart from the router's address. Until then a product mounts the embed at that route with the bar above.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. Additive on the embed's options: `bar` takes `boolean | EmbedBar`, `EmbedBar` being `{ faces?: false; name?: false }`, on `mount` and `<Embed>` from `@graview/embed` and on `mount` and `<PagesEmbed>` from `@graview/embed/pages`; `EmbedBar` is a new type export of both. `bar: true` and `bar: false` draw what they drew. With `faces: false` the bar has no `[data-testid="app-faces"]`; with `name: false` no `app-home` and no `app-name`. What Graview Cloud does: a product face with its own shell mounts the scene with `bar: { faces: false, name: false }` and `heading: false`, and drops the CSS that hid the name and the switch.
- 6959652: `graview view check` runs a view of one record over a record, and a view's pictures are reckoned over its own records first (W-242, the thirteenth walk, a camera-trap survey). The survey's contact sheet, a pictures view of one station that reads the detections and the edge saying which camera took each, was run with no station at all and said "No station.", which judges nothing; and the picture budget the run reported was spent on every detection of the app in the order they were kept, so the station's own photographs were left out for the pictures of cameras it never draws — on the page too. Now a view of one record is run over the record `--record <id>` names, or the first of its kind the seat sees, and the run says which first: `over "North ridge" (station:north)`; a `--record` that is no record of the kind the seat sees is refused, exit 1. And what a guest reads across kinds (`readAcross`, a worker view's and a frame guest's alike) comes with the records linked to its own by an edge it reads first, so a budget spent in order — `pictures`, on the page and headless — is spent on what it draws for its own records before the rest. The `graview-worker-view` and `graview-view-drawing` skills say so. The guest host measures 0.2 to 0.3 KB more for it, and its budgets and every face's are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. A guest is handed the same records and edges as before; only their order in `nodes` moves, linked to its own records first. New: `--record <id>` on `graview view check`. What Graview Cloud does: nothing more — a headless check of a view of one passes `input: { node: { id } }` to `runWorkerViewHeadless` as it did.

## 0.1.39

### Patch Changes

- 6bff55b: A `pictures: true` view draws a full-size record photo, and a headless run says what the page will leave out (FR-192). Roadmap f-view-picture-src-large: Maryland Family Weekend's journey view drew its moments while they held 9 KB webps; once they held the phone's own JPEGs (187 KB to 1.5 MB), the page fetched every one, handed the view a `data:` image of it, and then refused to draw that very image — a picture is handed at up to 1,500,000 bytes, a view's image was cut off at 512,000 characters of `data:` URL — so the view fell back to the declared home with "The image "data:image/jpeg;base64,…" was not drawn". The page now draws whatever `src` its host handed a view for a record's picture, at any size, by the host's own address for it (`JudgeContext.picture`, which the worker view's drawing asks `GuestPictureHand.drawn`), and only as handed: a `src` the view changed is judged as any image it makes. On a page whose policy refuses `blob:` images, as Graview Cloud's does, the view is handed a short `blob:` address all the same and the page draws the `data:` image behind it, so the view never carries a megabyte of base64 and never has it refused. A JPEG with an EXIF APP1 segment after its JFIF one is drawn as any other. The cap stays on a `data:` image the view makes itself, and its refusal says so: "It is a data: image of 1,234,567 characters, more than the 512,000 a view's own data: image may be. A picture on a record is drawn at any size by the src the host hands the view (pictures in its manifest), used as it was handed." A headless run (`runWorkerViewHeadless`, behind `describe_place` and `propose_view`) hands a picture the page would leave out before fetching it — heavier than the host fetches, or past the view's budget as the page reckons it — no `src` and the page's own sentence in `left`, and says it among its problems at `pictures`; `HeadlessLimits` takes `pictureBytes` and `picturesBytes` as the page does. The guest-sandbox pictures harness draws a 1,200 × 900 JPEG with EXIF on a page that refuses `blob:` images, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. The graview-view-drawing skill says the cap and that a record's picture is drawn as handed. Drawing a worker view on the open kit carries 1.1 KB more (1,075 bytes, 454 gzipped), under a raise of its own. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-192.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-192. What Graview Cloud does: nothing for the live page — a view whose manifest says `pictures` draws its records' photos at any size on the next version; a headless run's `problems` may now carry `{ at: "pictures", says }` for a picture the page would leave out.
- c9c13d2: A view saves a field as the person types, and hears that it saved (FR-191). A view's code is never handed what a person types where sight is not total, so the Farm Bureau workshop's call sheet could take notes during a call only through a Save button the person had to remember. A field now carries its act and says when it saves: `<textarea name="notes" data-prefill="notes" data-act="save-notes" data-save="input" data-debounce="1500">` inside a `fieldset data-record` is saved by the host once the person pauses for `data-debounce` milliseconds (1 000 when it is left out, held to 250–10 000), at once when they leave the field, and as the view goes, whatever was still waiting; `data-save="change"` saves on the field's `change` alone. A save is a press in every way but the click: the act is the field's own `data-act`, its record and its fieldset's fields are read as a press reads them and held to the same rules (only what the person typed, a prefilled value only back where it came from, under their grants), and a click into the field is not a press. The view hears what became of every act a press or a save asked for as an `acted` event on the bound element — `graview.on("acted", "textarea", (event) => …)`, `event.pressed` holding `as`, `ok`, and `reason` and `message` when it was refused — so it can say "Saving…" on `input` and "Saved" on `acted`. A headless run (`graview view check`, `runWorkerViewHeadless`) says when a field's `data-save` would save nothing: no `data-act` or `name`, no fieldset bound to a record, a `data-save` other than `input` or `change`, or a `data-debounce` it does not read. The graview-worker-view skill teaches it, with `examples/a-call-sheet.js`. The guest host's open-kit drawing carries 1.4 KB more (0.5 KB gzipped), fetched with the press reader when a view first draws; a hosted page's first load is unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-191.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire: a worker view's runtime is sent a `dom-event` whose `event` is `acted`, carrying `pressed`, after every act a press or a field's save asked for; a view that does not listen for it hears nothing new, and `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-191. What Graview Cloud does: teach `data-save`, `data-debounce` and `acted` in its views guide in place of the Save button a call sheet needed; nothing changes in how it mounts a view.
- 817aaba: `add-lens` refuses an option the lens does not take (FR-190). Roadmap f-lens-option-refused: 0.1.38's notes said `add-lens` refuses an unknown option, but it applied an option a board does not take (Graview Cloud sent color in its British spelling), and `move: true` on a calendar, with only the checker's warning, and drew the lens without it, so a misspelled key applied and did nothing. Now `add-lens` refuses such a key at `edits.<i>.options.<key>` with the code `lens-option-unknown` and the checker's own words and fix — "The columns lens takes no option "shade", so "Work" ignores it." / "Use one of: sort, search, filters, move, collapsed, cap, hidden." — as it already refuses a column or a field the board does not have. A lens declared some other way, by a JSON patch (`applyPatch`) or a document sent whole, is judged by `lensOptionsRefused(stored, proposed)` on `@graview/core/document`: an error at `lenses.<i>.options.<key>`, in the same words, for each key a titled shipped lens brings that it does not take and the same lens (its lens, title and kind) did not already carry. A document already carrying such a key still compiles, with the `lens-option-unknown` warning. The checker, `add-lens` and `lensOptionsRefused` say it in one sentence and fix, written once. The graview-lens skill says `add-lens` refuses it. The embed with the studio, and every face, carry 251 bytes more (103 and 116 gzipped), under raises of their own; a hosted page carries no more up front. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-190.
  
  Compatibility: an `add-lens` edit that named an option its lens does not take, which applied with a warning, is refused; every declaration that compiled still compiles, with the same warning, and ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-190. What Graview Cloud does: on a change sent as a JSON patch or a whole document, refuse it when `lensOptionsRefused(stored, proposed)` answers any finding, as it refuses an edit's error; `propose_change` with `add-lens` needs nothing.
- a455281: Three more skills that had reached the 11,000 characters a skill is held under are split along their seams, and a fourth that was close is split where it held a job of its own. `graview-node-kind` had 1 character free and had shaved sentences to fit; it keeps declaring a kind — fields, edges, label, the ids a caller chooses, verbs, edits, `creates`, `fromTheOtherEnd`, `display.page`, `lifecycle`, a figure and a hue — and has them back: what a kind gets "without another line", the bootstrap ids never to hardcode (`"today"`, `"u-nick"`), the typecheck that sees an edge to an undeclared kind, the picker with nothing in it, the one way in on a blank installation, the brief `graview figure` prints and the chip's twenty pixels; `graview-view-specs` is new: a kind's card, row and page as data (`viewSpecs`, a document's `views` and `set-view`), headlines, figures and lists of related records, block keys, `computed` values and the template words for counts and times, with `view-*` and `computed-*` findings beside them. `graview-lists` had 2 characters free; it keeps filters, order and groups, cards at a glance, a kind's stop, several records at once, `renderPlace` and a date that repeats, and has back the right-click menu's whole paragraph (Shift+right-click, `data-graview-editable`), while `graview-tidying` is new: archiving and bringing back, merging a duplicate, and the app's own actor suggesting what it is not sure of and held to "Leave this one alone" and `tidying`. `graview-ship` had 18 characters free; it keeps opening a store, the seed, migrations, export, health, files, `serve`, a host that compiles once and `graview preview`, with its shaved sentences back (the readable adapter's stored version, the demo with no exit, the content steps, the tenant who cannot leave), and `graview-sync` is new: a snapshot projected from another system, `SyncEngine` and its conflict policies, an MCP remote behind `mcpDoor` (`hosts` included), and a Google calendar declared under `connections`, with the `connection-*` findings `graview check` says. The dev server's door to a coding agent (`localIntelligence`) moves from `graview-ship` to `graview-desk`, beside the door it is. `graview-brand` keeps the name, mark, typeface, palette, kit and capsules, and `graview-locale` is new: a currency, a locale and a time zone declared once, a field kept as money and the template words for each, with "3:30 PM", "en-GB keeps the day first" and "without one, sums are numbers" back. Each half points at the other in one line, and every one is under 9,100 characters. `graview skills install` installs twenty-six skills.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. A product's `.claude/skills` and `.agents/skills` gain `graview-view-specs`, `graview-tidying`, `graview-sync` and `graview-locale` on its next `graview skills install`, and the AGENTS.md list names them.
- 38ea99a: Three skills that had reached the 11,000 characters a skill is held under are split along their seams, and each half has one job. `graview-embed` had 4 characters free and could not teach 0.1.39's drawn signal or draft views; it keeps the embed on somebody else's page — the stop, the face, the name, the look, seats, many on one page, the views, a framed view of somebody's HTML — and `graview-host-page` is new: a host whose page is the app, with the address bar handed over (`routing: "address"`, `basePath`), a chat's widget sized from the embed, a hosted reader sitting only as who signed in, the host's own menu, news and first look inside the app, `setApp` keeping the reader's place and `preload`; when a place has drawn and settled (`data-graview-drawn`, `mount`'s `onDrawn(place)` told the `EmbedWhere`, FR-188); a view proposed and not kept (`draftView`, FR-187); and pictures of places with `graview preview` and `graview shots`, `--view <view.js> --manifest <manifest.json>` included. `graview-worker-view` had 2 characters free and had shaved sentences to fit FR-191; it keeps the manifest, the global, links, limits, registering and the headless run, and has them back — the `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with `hour` and `minute`, the section looked for on the scene, the "stag" filter's sentence, drawing summaries, the heartbeat's 5 s, the home full width on a desk, and the pictures' line — while `graview-view-forms` is new: a bound press, a record's own text filled to edit (`data-prefill`), a field that saves as it is typed (`data-save`, `data-debounce` held to 250–10 000) and the view hearing what became of each act (`acted`), with `examples/a-call-sheet.js` moved beside it. `graview-lens` had 2 characters free; it keeps building a lens that binds roles and proving its reuse, and `graview-shipped-lens` is new: declaring a calendar, a status board, a coverage, a timeline, a board, a plan or a blocks place as a titled place, a table of what each binds and every option it takes (held to `SHIPPED_LENSES` by a test), and what `graview check` and `add-lens` refuse — `lens-option-unknown`, `lens-cannot-draw`, `lens-title-taken` and the rest. Each half points at the other in one line, and every one is under 10,000 characters. `graview skills install` installs twenty-two skills.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. A product's `.claude/skills` and `.agents/skills` gain `graview-host-page`, `graview-view-forms` and `graview-shipped-lens` on its next `graview skills install`, and the AGENTS.md list names them.
- 328235d: A view takes files dropped on it (FR-193). Roadmap f-views-take-a-drop: Graview Cloud's family weekend wanted photographs and a phone's video dragged from Finder onto a day's card on its journey home view to become moments on that day; a worker view had no way to receive a file, and no act argument could carry one. A view now marks the element — `<section data-drop="moment" data-drop-connect="takenOn:day:saturday">` — and its manifest says which fields files become: `accepts: [{ kind: "moment", field: "photo" }, { kind: "moment", field: "clip" }]`, each a kind and its image, video or file field (`checkManifest` refuses a kind the app lacks, a field that holds no file, and a kind no act makes with that field). The host owns the rest. It offers the element as a drop target only where this seat may make that kind and link it, and the host's files take what its fields hold (`data-graview-drop`, said to assistive technology); while files are dragged over it the host draws it with its own outline and says what dropping there does, in a status of its own; on a coarse pointer the same element is a tap that opens the picker, asking for what the fields take (`image/*,video/*`), and Enter or Space on it does the same anywhere. Each file is held to the field its type fits (a picture to `photo`, a video to `clip`) and to the host's `kinds` and `maxBytes`, stored through the host's `files.upload`, and what was refused is said one sentence a file while the rest go on. A view that does not hear `drop` gets, per file, a record of the kind with its file on its field, linked as `data-drop-connect` says — by an act on the dropped-on record that makes one linked where the app has it, else the act that makes the kind and the derived "Set the day" — all in one change that one undo takes back, said "Added 4 moments to Saturday." A view that hears it (`graview.on("drop", "[data-drop]", (event) => …)`) is told once, with `event.files` as the host stored them — `{ href, name, type, bytes, width, height, poster, duration, at, field }`, never their bytes — and `event.refused`. `graview.act(name, [args, …])` runs several of one act as one change, and an act's argument takes a file only as the host handed it, in a drop or on a record the view was shown, going as the host kept it; any other `href` is refused. Where sight is not total, an act from a view's code still applies only from a press, except one that carries a drop's files and nothing the view could have read. `applyAll` takes `{ $made: n }` in an argument, the record call `n` of the same change made (`made(n)` from `@graview/core`), and judges that call once the record is there to name. A headless run (`graview view check`, `runWorkerViewHeadless`) says a `data-drop` of a kind or `data-drop-field` the manifest does not accept, and a `data-drop-connect` that is not `<relation>:<record id>` or names a record the view was not shown. A file field's editor on a host whose files take nothing (`maxBytes` 0) offers no picker and says the host takes no files, never "up to 0 bytes"; an empty video field on a record's page is drawn as its editor where the host takes `video/*`, as it already was, now held by a test. The graview-view-drawing skill teaches the drop, and every event a view hears as `{ type, target, element }` with what it carries: `value` and `checked`, `key`, `pressed`, `files` and `refused`. The guest-sandbox drop harness proves it in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox. A hosted page's first load carries 0.7 KB more (0.3 KB gzipped), the store's `{ $made: n }`; the drop itself is fetched only with a view that draws a drop target. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-193.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored formats are unchanged; an op keeps the record id a `{ $made: n }` was read as. The declaration: a worker view's manifest takes `accepts`. The wire: a worker view's runtime sends `{ type: "hears", events }` the first time its `graview.on` listens for each event, an act request's `args` may be a list of argument objects (1 to 100) for several of one act as one change, and the host sends a `dom-event` whose `event` is `drop`, carrying `files` and `refused`; a view that asks for none of it is sent nothing new. Derived tools are unchanged, and `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-193. What Graview Cloud does: mount views as it does — the page's `files` (`filesAt` under the app's base) is what a drop is stored through, so the files route's upload is the whole seam; let `propose_view` take `accepts` in a view's manifest; and teach `data-drop`, `data-drop-connect`, `accepts` and `graview.act(name, [args, …])` in its views guide.

## 0.1.38

### Patch Changes

- 28a63eb: A status board moves a card without a button under it, and the shipped lenses take search, filter and column options (Roadmap f-board-move-without-buttons, f-lens-search-filter). A Move… button under every card doubled a board's height, and the Recon app's Actions board could not be searched without a hand-drawn view that could not drag. A card is now dragged, carried by the keyboard — Space picks it up, the arrows carry it between and along the columns, Space drops it and Escape puts it back, each step said in a live region ("Public forms, picked up. Planned, 2 of 16.") — or moved from its own menu's "Move to ›", which lists every other column: the ones this seat may move it to, and the withheld ones struck through with the reason a press would meet (`columnChoices` on `@graview/core/describe`). The card stays one stop for Tab and says how it moves. `add-lens` and a declared or TypeScript `columns` lens take `options`: `search` (the fields Find looks in), `filters` (fields and relations standing as chips), `move` (the Move button back), `collapsed` and `hidden` (columns, by value), and `cap` (the newest cards a column keeps, with "Show N more"). A board of more than twenty cards shows Find without being asked; a smaller one shows it when `search` names its fields. A narrowed card moves as before. A calendar and a coverage take `search` and `filters` too. `graview check` says an option the kind cannot honor as `lens-option`, and `add-lens` refuses it. Up front a hosted page carries 0.7 KB more (722 bytes, 0.6 KB handed a compiled app), the options a declared lens is held to.
  
  Compatibility: a declaration gains the `columns` lens's `search`, `filters`, `move`, `collapsed`, `cap` and `hidden` options and the calendar's and coverage's `search` and `filters`, and `graview check` gains the warning `lens-option`; every declaration that compiled still compiles, and ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged.

## 0.1.37

### Patch Changes

- cf691f4: A record can hold a video. A photo gallery built on Graview Cloud with three iPhone clips could show only a play glyph over a still: no field took a video, the host's files took none, and a view may not draw `<video>`. A document now says `{"type": "video"}` (or `{"type": "list", "of": "video"}`), a declaration `videoField()`, and the value is the `FileValue` the host's upload answered — `{ href, name, type, bytes, width?, height?, caption? }` — with what a player wants before it loads a byte: `poster` (a picture on the host), `duration` in seconds and `captions` (a WebVTT file on the host), each a path on the host or a length above 0, or refused. A video field takes only `video/*`, a picture field no video. The editor asks for videos where the host's `kinds` take `video/*` (`accept="video/*"`, "Choose a video", "This host doesn't take videos" where they do not) and sends one as it was picked: making a phone's HEVC `.mov` an MP4 every browser plays, its poster and its length are the host's to answer. The face plays a record's video — its poster first, the browser's own controls, `preload="metadata"`, captions where the value has them, sized from the value — drawn and fetched where the host serves files (`fileSrc`), its length read from what the player loaded where the value does not say it; a card or a row shows its poster (or its first frame) with a play glyph and its length. A record's facts, an image block and a headless run say it as "a 45-second video" (`videoWords`); the act's form and its argument's shape say `video: true`. A worker view whose manifest says `pictures` places `<graview-video data-record data-field>` and the host plays that record's video in it, from the records the view was shown, never handing the view a path or drawing it a `<video>`; and a viewer's press on a `data-lightbox` element opens the host's full-screen viewer for a picture or a video. The dev server's files door takes videos and serves them a range at a time. The `graview-view-drawing`, `graview-worker-view` and `graview-node-kind` skills say so. A hosted page carries 0.6 KB more up front for a video field's schema; the player, the card and the view's player are behind the doors already there, and the guest host's first load grows by under 1 KB for the press and the door.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged; a stored file value is read as before, and a value may now also carry `poster`, `duration` and `captions`.
