The declaration takes the field type `video` and `of: "video"`; a declaration that compiled still compiles. Derived tool input schemas for a file argument list the optional `poster`, `duration` and `captions`, and a video field's form control and argument shape say `video: true`. The guest wire is unchanged in shape: a video on a record is handed to a view that asks for `pictures` as its facts (`GuestFile.duration` beside `width`, `height` and `caption`) where it was handed its name, type and size. The open kit draws one element more, `graview-video`. `DEV_FILES_KINDS` takes `video/*`.
- f860e41: A rule's today() is the app's day, not UTC's. Datetimes were said in the app's zone, and `today()` — what a rule, a computed field, a template and a label read — was still the UTC date, so an app in New York turned its day at 8 PM: at 01:30 UTC on Oct 17 a chore due Oct 16 was already overdue over dinner. With `brand.timeZone` declared, today() is the date there, and the day turns at the app's midnight: a document's rules, computed fields, guards and labels read it from the document's own brand, and a store told the zone (`new Store({ timeZone })`, which `openStore`, `openRemote`, the embed and `graview mcp` pass from the app's brand) is judged on that day, hands it to a rule written as a function in its context, as `openStore` already did, and ticks to it: `tick(now)` takes `now`'s day in that zone, and `tickEachDay` fires at that midnight, 23 hours after the last on the day clocks spring forward and 25 on the day they fall back, at the day's first moment where midnight never happens. A page reads the day of the app it draws, never the reader's zone and never the runtime's, so a server and a page agree on the day for the same app. Without a declared zone, today() is the UTC date, as before, and `openStore` still judges on the host's own date. A host that keeps a store on its own alarm (a Durable Object) passes `timeZone: brand.timeZone` to its store and sets the alarm `untilTheDayTurns(now, store.timeZone)` ahead; a tick at the turn of the day now says what the turn moved, judging what stood on the day the store was last ticked to. `isoToday(timeZone?, at?)` and `dayZoneFor` are exported, `dayOf` and `untilTheDayTurns` take a zone, and `rules-read-the-clock`'s fix says which midnight. The `graview-invariant` skill says so. A hosted page carries 0.7 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too); their budgets are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the declaration are unchanged. An app that declares `brand.timeZone` reads today() as the date in that zone, where it read the UTC date, on every surface and in `graview serve`. `StoreOptions` takes `timeZone` and `Store` has `timeZone`; `dayOf(now, timeZone?)` and `untilTheDayTurns(now, timeZone?)` take a zone; `tickEachDay` reads the store's `timeZone`. A store told its zone hands its day to its rules' context as `today` where neither the call nor its options carry one.
- 7d6f521: Datetimes are said in the app's time zone, and a template can say a time of day. An app declares its zone once, `brand.timeZone` (an IANA name, "America/New_York") on `defineApp`'s brand or a document's `brand`, and every instant kept with a zone (`2026-10-16T14:47:00Z`, `…-04:00`) is said on that clock: in lists, cards, record pages, a rule's sentence, an act's sentence and its echo to an agent, Activity's digest, the static page and the date editor, which reads and keeps an instant on the same clock. A person's zone wins over it where a host passes one — `timeZone` on `GraviewProvider`, the embed and the pages face, beside `locale` — and each reader's own clock is said when neither says, as before. A moment kept without a zone is still the wall time written. Describe templates take `{at | time}` ("10:47 AM") and `{at | datetime}` ("Oct 16, 2026, 10:47 AM EDT"), and a field block takes `as: "time"` and `as: "datetime"`; `{at | date}` and `relative` count an instant's day on the app's clock. An agent's act given `2026-10-16T10:47:00-04:00` echoed "2:47 PM", the server's UTC; a tool runtime handed the app now says it in the app's zone, and a host answering away from any page says its own words in it with `sayingIn({ appTimeZone }, run)`. A view is handed the zone rather than formatted strings: every props push carries `locale` and `timeZone`, for `Intl.DateTimeFormat(props.locale, { timeZone: props.timeZone, … })`, so no view hard-codes a zone again. `graview check` and the document's checker refuse a zone the runtime does not know, or an offset, by name (`brand-time-zone`); `set-brand` sets one (`timeZone`, null clears it), and a diff says "Times are said in America/New_York." `describeApp` says the zone. The `graview-brand`, `graview-node-kind` and `graview-worker-view` skills say so. A hosted page carries 2.0 KB more up front for it (the compiled page too), and the guest host about 0.5 KB; their budgets are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged; the declaration takes an optional `brand.timeZone`, and a declaration without it says every time as before. The check finding `brand-time-zone` is new. The guest wire's props carry `locale` and `timeZone`. `Money` (a block's and a template's `money`) takes `timeZone`; `readableFields` takes `timeZone`; `momentAsRead`, `timeAsRead` and `whenAsRead` take a zone after the locale. `sayIn` takes `readerTimeZone` and `appTimeZone` beside `reader` and `app`. A derived edit's sentence and Activity's digest say an instant's day and time on the page's zone, where they said them on the runtime's clock.
- f97a149: Every relation comes with its connect and disconnect, as every kind comes with its edit, remove and archive. After an `add-relation` — a moment taken on a day, a moment that features a traveler — nothing could link two records until somebody also wrote an act that `connects`. Now each kind's relation is given `connect-<kind>-<relation>` and `disconnect-<kind>-<relation>` (the relation in kebab-case, `connect-moment-taken-on`), on the record it leaves as `id` and the record it reaches as `to`. A cardinality-one relation is set and cleared ("Set the day", "Clear the day") and connecting another replaces the link it had; a many one is linked and unlinked ("Link a traveler", "Unlink a traveler"); each reads from the other end too ("Link a moment"), its description carries the relation's own, and a kind with two relations to the same kind tells them apart by the relation's words ("Set the person (reviewer)"). Any act the app declares that `connects` a relation replaces the derived connect, any that `severs` it the derived disconnect; an `appendOnly` relation is given no disconnect, and a relation to another app's kind is given neither, because it is a field the derived edit already sets. They are permitted as a derived edit is — to whoever may make or change the kind the relation leaves, or a grant naming them — and refuse a record the seat may not see. Because they declare what they connect and sever, a line's acts, the far record's page, the inline AI's fills, an import's links and an agent's tools offer them with nothing more; `graview describe` says, per relation, which acts make and break it. A grant or a repair in a document may name one, and it follows a renamed kind or relation. `edge-without-severer` is gone: a relation some act makes and none breaks is now given its disconnect, so the asymmetry it warned of cannot be declared.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. Derived tool names and input schemas gain `connect-<kind>-<relation>` and `disconnect-<kind>-<relation>` ({ id, to }, strict) for every relation no act connects or severs — in this repository's apps, only the desk's `uses` relation (`connect-app-uses`, `disconnect-app-uses`); the conformance kit announces `connect-vendor-fills` and `disconnect-vendor-fills` on the 0.1.10 shortlist. The check no longer gives `edge-without-severer`. A hosted page carries 2.6 KB more up front, handed a compiled app or not: the derivation the store registers and a document's grants are checked by.

## 0.1.36

### Patch Changes

- 9f3e1d1: A record's pictures and documents load where the host serves them, on every face. A record keeps its file at the path the host's upload answered (`/graview/files/…`), without the base the app is served under, and the face drew `<img src>` and every link and download at that path; Graview Cloud serves an app at `/a/<app>/` and its files only under it, so every thumbnail, gallery, picture full size, inline PDF and download asked the workspace's root and broke. `HostFiles` now says where it serves a file (`src(href)`), and every place the framework draws, links or downloads a record's file goes through it (`fileSrc(files, href)` from `@graview/core`), the href itself where the host does not say. `filesAt(url)` derives it from its own url: an href under `/graview/files/` is drawn under `url`, so a host that already hands `filesAt(base + "/graview/files")` gets its pictures under its base with nothing else changed; the dev door's `/__graview/files/…` is drawn as it is. A worker or frame view the framework mounts as a kind's view takes its `file` from the page's `files.src` unless its definition gives one. What the page says of a file (`data-graview-thumb`, `data-graview-full`, `data-graview-removed-later`) and what it asks `kept` still name it by the href the record keeps. The `graview-ship` and `graview-view-drawing` skills say so. The guest host's first load grows by under 200 bytes for it, and its two budgets are raised by that.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records, the declaration and the guest wire are unchanged. `HostFiles` takes an optional `src?(href: string): string`; a host without it draws each file at its href as before. `filesAt` answers `src`, which draws an href under `/graview/files/` under its url — a host that handed `filesAt` a url elsewhere than its files' own address now draws its files at that url. `WorkerViewDefinition.file` and `guestView`'s `file` default to the page's `files.src`.
- 8536e92: A view draws the app's own image however it writes the address, and a headless run can draw one at all. `add_image` answers `/graview/assets/<sha256>.<ext>`; a view that wrote it under the app's base (`/a/<app>/graview/assets/…`) or as the page's own address for it was refused, and a headless run — Graview Cloud's `propose_view` — refused every one, since it had no way to hear where the app's images live. Each form is now the one image, handed to the host's `asset` as its path; another origin, a path that climbs and a query are still refused. `runWorkerViewHeadless` takes `asset` and `origin`, as the live page takes `asset` and is on its origin. The sentence a refused image says offers only what this host draws: the app's own images only where it passed `asset` (and, where it did not, says so by name), `https:` images only from the origins it lists, and an address of the app's own image on another origin is told to be written as its path.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records, the declaration and the guest wire are unchanged. A headless run's input may carry `assets` and `origin` beside `imageOrigins`. `sayRefusal(refusal, host)` takes `{ origins, assets }` where it took the origins.
- 8536e92: A view whose manifest says `pictures` is handed each picture's `src` where its app is served under a base, and in a headless run. A record keeps its file at a path on the host (`/graview/files/…`), and the host fetched it at that path resolved against the page; Graview Cloud serves an app at `/a/<app>/`, so the fetch reached the workspace's root instead of the app, and a photo gallery's view never got `src`. A host now says where a record's files are with `file` on the view's definition (and on `mountWorkerView` and a frame's `mountGuestView` and `guestView`): `(href) => base + href`. A picture the host did not answer for says what it answered (`the host answered 401`, or the type it sent) and, where `file` was not given, names it, in the view's `left`, its console and the host's problems; it still never names the path. A headless run (`runWorkerViewHeadless`, what Cloud's `propose_view` runs) fetches nothing, and now hands each picture a stand-in `src`, a blank image, so the view's `<img>` is drawn and judged as it will be on the page rather than skipped. The `graview-view-drawing` skill says both.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records, the declaration and the guest wire are unchanged. `WorkerViewDefinition`, `mountWorkerView`, `mountGuestView` and `guestView` take `file?: (href: string) => string | undefined`; without it a file is fetched where it was before. A headless run hands a picture `src` (`PICTURE_STAND_IN`) where it handed none.

## 0.1.35

### Patch Changes

- ac91d40: An `add-relation` edit can name another app's kind. Since 0.1.30 a whole document could declare `"forHouse": { "to": ["@nick/house/house"], "cardinality": "one" }`, but the edit refused the same relation (`"@nick/house/house" is not a kind this app has`), so a host that changes an app by edits — graview.cloud's builder, `propose_change`, the studio — could not link one app to another. `editDocument` now takes `{"op": "add-relation", "kind": "scenario", "relation": "forHouse", "to": ["@nick/house/house"], "cardinality": "one"}` and gives a document that checks clean, judged by the rules `graview check` applies: a `to` that mixes this app's kinds with another's, reaches two apps, says `inverse` or `appendOnly` is refused at `edits.<n>` with the checker's own finding (`linked-mixed`, `linked-apps`, `linked-target`, `linked-inverse`, `linked-append-only`). It says `scenario gains a relation, "forHouse", to one house in the app @nick/house, another app, read there as whoever is looking.` `rename-relation` and `remove-relation` work on one. `planMigration` dropped a far relation's values on every migration, since it carried only declared fields; it now keeps them, moves them with a rename and clears them with the relation, said like a relation's links. The studio read a far relation as a text field; it now reads it as a relation (`toAnotherApp` on the edge), makes one with `add-edge-to-another-app` (`to: "@nick/house/house"`), says it as `add-relation`, and writes it back to a checkout as `linkedRecordField([...]).optional()`.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration is unchanged — a document that compiled compiles the same — and the edits take what they refused, with the checker's finding codes at `edits.<n>`; a stored graph migrates keeping what it dropped; ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tools are unchanged. The studio's own tools gain `add-edge-to-another-app`.

## 0.1.34

### Patch Changes

- 8f09e52: A reader who may do nothing is told how to get in, not shown every act struck through (FR-183). A signed-out visitor on a public app right-clicked an era and the menu said "Nothing you may do with an era — 7 actions withheld" over seven disabled buttons, each with the policy's sentence; the list's foot said "~~Add an era~~ — Not permitted: …"; nothing said how to get the right, and "Notify me…" was offered to `anonymous` and kept a subscription for nobody. A host now says what a signed-out reader could gain: `signIn: { label, run, roles? }`, handed only while the reader is a visitor, on `mount` from `@graview/embed` and `@graview/embed/pages`, on `GraviewProvider` (which the Shell draws under, and a provider inside another takes from the one around it) and on the pages' context. Where every act withheld from the reader is one a member could take — one some role could, or one of `roles`, the roles signing in gives, when the host names them — the context menu and the Inspector on either face, the list's foot and a record's acts say `label` as one press that calls `run` (`data-testid="sign-in"`), in place of the struck acts; the face's own rows (Open, Show in the scene) stay. With `signIn` given, "Notify me" is not offered on the menu, a record's page or after an act, and the seat's chat keeps no subscription. A member lacking one role is still told each act they may not take and who could (FR-16), and so is a visitor where one act is one no role can take. `signInInstead(withheld, signIn)` and the `SignIn` type are exported from `@graview/core`, the same judgment for a page of an app's own. A hosted page, compiled or not, carries 0.2 KB more up front for it, under a raise of 0.3 KB more up front on each of the two budgets. The embed's README and the graview-embed skill say the option. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-183.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire is unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-183. What Graview Cloud does: pass `signIn: { label: "Sign in to make changes", run }` to `mount` while nobody is signed in, and drop its interim `visitorMenuSections` row and the CSS hiding `[data-testid=withheld]`, `[data-testid=no-affordances]` and `[data-menu-section=notify]`.

## 0.1.33

### Patch Changes

- a583b18: `mount` takes the host's subscriptions, and a page that keeps them on a served store carries no rule parser (FR-182). `GraviewProvider` and the pages' context took `subscriptions`, but the embed's options had none, so on a hosted page the sheet "Notify me…" and Activity's list kept a reader's subscriptions in the page's memory. `subscriptions` (any `SubscriptionStore`) is now one of the embed's options, on `mount` from `@graview/embed` and from `@graview/embed/pages`, and on `<Embed>`, handed to the provider on both faces and to the pages' context. `remoteSubscriptions` moved out of the module that answers `/graview/subscriptions` into one of its own, so asking the route no longer brings the expression and template parsers, which the answer judges a subscription with, into a hosted page's first chunk: the compiled shell handing `mount` a `remoteSubscriptions` store was 10,360 bytes (10.1 KB) heavier up front than the shell without one, and is now 767 bytes heavier, 9.4 KB less; the page that compiles its document, which carries the parsers anyway, is 1.9 KB less. Both are still exported from `@graview/ship`, `@graview/ship/browser` and `@graview/ship/runtime` as they were. The embed's README and the graview-embed skill say the option. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-182.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The wire is unchanged: `/graview/subscriptions` answers as before, and `answerSubscriptions`, `remoteSubscriptions` and `SUBSCRIPTIONS_PATH` keep their entries; `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-182. What Graview Cloud does: pass `subscriptions: remoteSubscriptions({ url, principal })` to `mount` and drop its interim stand-in for the store.

## 0.1.32

## 0.1.31

### Patch Changes

- ff88015: A person can ask to be told when something happens in an app, and the host is told when it does (Roadmap f-notify-subscriptions). A subscription is `{ who, app, when }`, where `when` is an act running (any, one by name, and/or one on a record), a record of a kind added, a kind's records or one record changed, or a record reaching a condition written in the app's own rule language (`done == true`, `amount > 100`); it is told only of changes somebody else made unless it says `alsoMine`. `matchSubscriptions(store, ops, subscriptions)` on the new `@graview/core/subscriptions` judges a commit batch by batch as each subscriber's seat sees it — a change to a record they cannot see never matches, a field kept from them is neither said nor read by a condition, a change withheld from them is said as "Someone" — and answers at most one `SubscriptionMatch` per batch per subscription, with who made it, its sentence, the acts it ran and each record it touched with the fields it changed. `watchSubscriptions(store, { subscriptions, onSubscriptionMatch })` calls a host's hook on every commit; `subscriptionWords` says a subscription in words and `subscriptionProblem` says why one could never be told anything. The shapes and `memorySubscriptions()` are on the main entry.
  
  `@graview/ship`'s handler keeps them (`subscriptions`, a `SubscriptionStore` of the host's; in memory when unsaid), serves each seat its own at `/graview/subscriptions`, and calls `onSubscriptionMatch` once per matching batch; `remoteSubscriptions` is the page's half, `fileSubscriptions` the file `graview serve` keeps beside the store and prints each match from. A chat subscribes its person with three new tools, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe` and `list_subscriptions`, listed where the host keeps subscriptions (`ToolRuntimeOptions.subscriptions`, `createMcpHttpHandler`'s, and always under `graview mcp`, which shares the file with `graview serve`).
  
  On both faces the context menu offers "Notify me…" on a record, a kind's list and an act (a menu target now says the `act` it was opened on), a record's page offers "Notify me when this changes", and an act's form when it is done offers "Notify me when someone else does this": each opens one sheet already filled in, so from a right-click a person subscribes in two presses. What a person is told of is listed in Activity, each with one press to stop. The provider keeps them where its host says (`subscriptions`), else in the page.
  
  It adds 0.8 KB more up front to a hosted page (864 bytes, 330 gzipped), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: the wire: a new route, `/graview/subscriptions` (GET the seat's own; POST `{ add: { when, alsoMine? } }` or `{ remove: id }`), and `createStoreHandler` takes `subscriptions` and `onSubscriptionMatch`; every other route answers as before. Derived tool names and input schemas: unchanged for a runtime given no `subscriptions`; given one, `subscribe`, `unsubscribe` and `list_subscriptions` are listed beside the read tools, and an act named any of them is listed as `<name>_2`. New: `@graview/core/subscriptions`, `Subscription`, `SubscriptionWhen`, `SubscriptionMatch`, `SubscriptionStore`, `OnSubscriptionMatch`, `memorySubscriptions` and `subscriberOf` on `@graview/core`, `MenuTarget.act`, `PointerMenu.act`, `GraviewContextValue.subscriptions`, and `NotifySections`, `NotifyDoor`, `NotifyButton`, `NotifiedDoor`, `useNotifySection`, `useNotifiedCount` and `askToNotify` on `@graview/primitives`.
- 6c92669: Pages honors the app's setting for the AI it offers in place. An embed mounted with `seat: "hidden"` drew no ask field on the scene and one on every page anyway: the routed face drew its own without reading the setting. In this release the ask field itself gives way to the quick ask (see "The quick ask replaces the Ask box"), and its setting, `quickAsk`, is read by both faces alike: the embed hands it to the scene and to every page, and `handle.setQuickAsk(on)` changes it on whichever face is open, on a phone and a desk, without mounting again.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes beyond those the quick ask's changeset names.
- 294f181: The inline AI picks from what the app already knows, previews before it keeps, and leaves an entry in Activity (Roadmap f-ai-candidate-catalog, f-activity-ai-entries). A host that hands the seat a model now gets an AI section in the context menu on both faces: a few suggestions the model picked for what was right-clicked, "Fill in with AI" and "Draw a panel". The model never invents: `catalogOf(store, target, { principal })` on the new `@graview/core/catalog` derives, for a record, a selection, a field, a list or a lens, an act or a place, the candidates with an id and a line each — the acts the viewer may run there, the panels its kind can be drawn as, the places and records next to it, the values its fields may take — and `aiContextOf` the compact state a model reads beside them (where the viewer is, the target, the selection, the records one link away, what was done lately, a digest of the declaration cached per declaration), both under the viewer's sight: nothing they may not see or run is ever offered or said.
  
  `HostAi` gains two provider slots: `fast`, a typed-decision model in Jev's shape (`{ model, decide }`; `jevFast()` fills it with Jev), and `thinking`, a language model (`{ model, think({ system, prompt, state, catalog }) }`); unsaid, `decide` and `complete` serve them. Every call reports its tier, model, tokens and cost to the interaction and to `ai.meter`. `@graview/tools/inline-ai` holds the engine (`suggestHere`, `fillHere`, `panelHere`), the ghost (`ghostOf`, `acceptGhost`) and stub providers (`stubFast`, `stubThinking`) for tests and a dev app; the todo example takes them at `?ai=stub`. A proposed change waits as a ghost with Accept, Accept some and Dismiss, is kept as one batch whose ops carry lineage `interaction` and come through `ai:<interaction>`, and is taken back with one undo; a panel is drawn where the seat draws a view, and "Keep as a lens" makes it a real one.
  
  Each interaction is one entry in Activity (`AiInteraction`: who, when, the target, what was asked, offered and taken or dismissed, the batches it made with their undo, the models and their cost), and the change it made is drawn in it rather than again beside it. Activity filters All, Changes or AI and "Just mine"; a record's page lists what AI did there (`RecordInteractionsDoor`); an entry can ask again or show its panel again. A host keeps them with `ai.interactions` (`{ load, keep }`). `ai: { off: true }` hides every AI part on both faces — the menu's section, the ghost, the ask field, the entries and the filter — rather than disabling it.
  
  It adds 1.3 KB more up front to a hosted page (1,305 bytes, 460 gzipped), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: ops and primitives: an op's `lineage` may carry `interaction`, the id of the AI interaction it came from; a reader that does not know it reads the lineage as before. New: `@graview/core/catalog`, `@graview/tools/inline-ai`, `HostAi.off`, `.fast`, `.thinking`, `.interactions` and `.meter`, `GraviewContextValue.aiDesk`, `GoTo.draft`, and `GhostDoor`, `InlineAiSections`, `useInlineAiSection` and `RecordInteractionsDoor` on `@graview/primitives`.
- 9a5e067: The quick ask replaces the Ask box (Roadmap f-quick-ask-framework). No face draws a field at its foot that grows into a chat thread any more: `SeatField`, its panel and Pages' `PageAsk` are gone, and so are their settings, the Shell's `ask` and `chat`, a routed face's `context.ask`, the embed's `seat` option and `handle.setAskField`. In their place, where the host's `ai` gives a model and does not say `off`, ⌘K (Ctrl+K) opens a command bar beside what the reader is on — the selected card on the scene, the record or list a page is about — that completes the acts on it, "Show …" for a panel the catalog can draw of it, its places and its records. Words that complete nothing are asked, and the answer is a card beside the record or the list: a few sentences, the places, records and panels that act on it, and the changes it proposes as a ghost on the card, accepted all or some as one batch with its one undo. A follow-up is asked on the card and replaces its answer; it is not a thread. The context menu's "Ask about this…", Find's last row and the search page's "Ask: ‘…’" open the same card, Activity keeps each ask as an AI interaction (`ask.kind` `"ask"`, with its `answer`) and "Ask again" asks it on the card again. Where the quick ask is drawn ⌘K is its and `/` stays Find's — Pages' Find hears `/` too, and the bar says `/` over its box; where it is not, both are Find's.
  
  `quickAsk` (a boolean, on by default) on `Shell`, a routed face's context and `mount`/`<Embed>`, and `handle.setQuickAsk(on)`, say whether it is drawn; `quickAskOf(setting)` reads a setting a host kept from the Ask box — `"field"` is on, `"hidden"` is off — and the embed reads a kept string the same way. The answer is the host's: `HostAi.answer` (`{ model, answer({ said, target, targetLabel, state, catalog, follows }) → { text, pick?, usage? } }`) answers it, else the Thinking slot, else the Fast slot's pick among the candidates; `answerHere` on `@graview/tools/inline-ai` is the engine, and `stubThinking` answers it for a test. A provider drawn inside another now takes the outer one's `ai` unless given its own, so an embed's routed face draws the inline AI and the quick ask too.
  
  The conversation stays the app's (`seatTalk`, the responders, `ai.turn`): no face draws it, and `ChatPanel` is there for a product that places one in its own layout. The strip the scene kept clear at its foot for the field stays, always, as the foot's (where the zoom, the way back, a notice and a proposal stand), so the city keeps its fit; `useSeatDrawn` and the conversation's `open`, `side`, `drawn`, `setOpen` and `setSide` went with the field. The four harnesses that existed for the box (`chat`, `phone-seat`, `seat-guide`, `seat-goes-and-draws`) are retired, and `quick-ask` holds the new surface on both faces in three engines.
  
  It adds 0.1 KB more up front to a hosted page (100 bytes, 60 gzipped, about the 19 its first load grew by and a margin), and its raises are filed in `scripts/budget-raises/`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. Removed: `SeatField`, `SeatFieldProps`, `SeatStart`, `SEAT_WIDTH`, `SEAT_PHONE_BELOW`, `PageAsk`, `useSeatDrawn`, `SeatSide`, the Shell's `ask` and `chat`, `PageContext.ask`, `EmbedOptions.seat` and `EmbedHandle.setAskField`. New: `QuickAskDoor`, `openQuickAsk`, `useQuickAskDrawn`, `quickAskOf`, `QuickAskOpening` on `@graview/primitives`, `PageQuickAsk` on `@graview/pages`, `quickAsk` on `ShellProps`, `PageContext` and `EmbedOptions`, `EmbedHandle.setQuickAsk`, `HostAi.answer` with `AnswerProvider`, `AnswerRequest` and `AnswerReply`, `answerHere`, `answerOf` and `QuickAsk` on `@graview/tools/inline-ai`, `AiInteraction.answer`, and `HeldGhost.onCard`.
- a2f741b: The scene draws on the GPU by default where the browser can, and on the DOM path everywhere else, silently and per page (Roadmap f-gpu-default). `Scene`, the `Shell` and the embed default to `renderer="auto"`: where a page has WebGPU, an element-capture call and a canvas `paint` event (`gpuCapturePresent`, asked without a device), `@graview/render/gpu` is fetched and `attachGpu()` takes the canvas; a page without them — Firefox, Safari, Chromium before element capture — never fetches it and says nothing. A first-frame check falls back to the DOM path for good when no adapter is given, no paint arrives within 1.5 s, a frame is refused, the device is lost, or the middle of the first four frames' work is over three quarters of a frame. A renderer is handed `fallBack()`, `blur` and `scheme` with each picture. `?renderer=gpu|dom` on the address, or `graviewRenderer` set on the page before it loads, decides `auto` for an operator (`rendererAsked`, `chooseRenderer`); `GRAVIEW_RENDERER` does it for every page of a harness run, and the nightly runs every shard on both paths.
  
  The two paths draw one picture: the treatment of a view — its plane style, blur, and fade — is worked out once (`treatmentOf`, `recededOpacity`) and drawn by CSS on one path and the shader on the other, in the page's scheme. The shader no longer mixes toward a fixed ground color or darkens a quad's edges, and it premultiplies the capture's straight alpha once: a light card on the GPU had gray bands down its sides and a pale fringe for a shadow. The accessibility tree is the same on both paths.
  
  On Chromium 158 the browser's own hit-test follows the geometry a view is reported at, without the crash 154 had, so there the canvas says `data-graview-hit="platform"`, the views' content takes the pointer, and hover — a record's ties drawn as the pointer reaches a chip — works on the GPU as on the DOM path; earlier builds keep `PointerRouter`. Geometry is reported a budget a frame until every view is, and a view gone off the canvas is reported there rather than left, invisible, where it was last drawn. A drag or a wheel moves the canvas by a transform, as it moves the DOM path's stage: at altitude the GPU path panned at 20 to 30 frames a second on a quartered processor, re-laying the picture for each notch.
  
  Receded planes are blurred, the focused plane sharp (Roadmap f-plane-blur). On the GPU the blur costs nothing measurable; the DOM path's CSS blur is dropped for the rest of the page if its frames run over a frame and a half while the picture moves. `blur={false}` on the Scene or the Shell turns it off, and so do `prefers-reduced-motion`, `prefers-reduced-transparency` and the reader's Motion setting (`planeBlurOn`). `pnpm verify renderer` holds all of it.
  
  Nothing up front changes on a hosted page; the scene face before it draws is 7.5 KB larger (940,163 bytes), and the embed's budgets are raised in `scripts/budget-raises/`, the capture path itself (about 23 KB) a chunk of its own fetched only where a page can capture.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes; `Scene` and `Shell` take an optional `blur`, the Shell's `renderer` defaults to `auto` where it was `dom`, `attachRenderer`'s argument gains `blur`, `scheme` and `fallBack`, the frame plan's `PlannedView` takes `depth` and `onPlot`, `PlanOptions` a `scheme`, `ViewDraw` carries `blur` and `FramePlan` `offCanvas`, `PlatformCapabilities.hitTestFollowsGeometry` is now decided, and `@graview/render` gains `gpuCapturePresent`, `rendererAsked`, `chooseRenderer`, `planeBlurOn`, `framesHeld`, `FRAME_BUDGET_MS`, `treatmentOf` and `recededOpacity` (`./gpu`: `FIRST_FRAMES`, `FIRST_PAINT_WAIT_MS`, `FIRST_FRAME_BUDGET_MS`, `Compositor.setBlur`, `Compositor.setScheme`).

## 0.1.30

### Patch Changes

- 6cbecc0: A card says its kind's glance and no more, the bar names a place once, and a drawn home stays Home beside `pages.first` (the same review app). A finding card read "Critical · Security · High" — the third its confidence, though a severity can be high too — and still did after `confidence` was taken out of the kind's `glance`: the glance chose the order and the rest of the kind followed it. A glance a kind declares is now said exactly, and a choice another field shown beside it shares says its field ("Confidence: high", "Likelihood: low"), on a card, a row, a describe line and a table alike. A default card no longer draws a long or Markdown text as a chip cut after a couple of dozen characters: it says the text's start on a line of its own under the chips. With a home declared and `pages.first: "This week"`, the app opened on This week, and a press on Home from the scene drew the pages at their first entry and went to This week again, so the home the author drew was nowhere: `pages.first` now picks where a new visit lands, once, and after that `/` is the home on both faces. `graview check` says so when both are set (`pages-first-beside-home`, a note). And a kind whose plural is "root causes" beside a blocks place titled "Root causes" put two "Root causes" on the bar: the place keeps the name and stands, as `pages.first` and `pages.primary` already read the word, and the kind's list folds into More as "All root causes", still at `/root-causes`; `graview check` warns of it (`pages-place-named-as-a-list`). The graview-pages and graview-lists skills teach each. With the template fixes beside them, a hosted page carries 1.2 KB more up front (and 0.6 KB more up front handed a compiled app).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and wire messages are unchanged. New check finding codes `pages-first-beside-home` (a note) and `pages-place-named-as-a-list` (a warning). `readableFields` with `glance: true` says only a kind's declared `display.glance` when it has one, and a choice's `alone` may carry its field's label. `barPlaces` labels a kind's list "All <plural>" and folds it when a place has its name (`allLabel` from `@graview/core`). `@graview/react/provider` exports `hasLanded(views)` and `markLanded(views)`, which the pages and the embed read `pages.first` once by. A default card's prose is `p[data-graview-excerpt="<field>"]`.
- 6862f3a: A calendar of years spans its records, from the first to the last. A career calendar opened on 1970-04-10 with a horizon of thirty years drew 1970–1999, and a discography's releases of 1962–1969 and 2000–2023 were nowhere. Its years now reach back to the first record and on to the last as well as across the horizon from the day it opens on — "The career · 1962–2023" — and a planning calendar whose records stop short still looks out as far as its horizon. A horizon says its ends outright with `from` and `to` (years), which win; a horizon written as a number is its years, called "Years"; and one that is not a span of years is a warning by its path (`lens-horizon`) and offers none. The graview-lens skill says the options, and `verify-declared` holds the span in a browser.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged. A calendar lens's `horizon` takes `from` and `to` beside `years` and `title`, and a number; a document's lens `options.horizon` is held to that shape by its reader. `spanOf` takes `fit` (the records' first and last years). A new warning code, `lens-horizon`.
- 6862f3a: A coverage with nothing to cross says so instead of listing its rows. "Songs by genre" — songs against genres through `inGenre` — with no genre yet drew forty songs as a plain list with no columns, which read as the answer to a question it could not yet ask. With no columns it says "No genres yet: link songs to genres.", and with columns and not one link over its relation "No song is linked to a genre yet: link songs to genres."; it draws no row, and a glance at it says "nothing linked". `describePlace` says the same words. A coverage over a path still draws its grid of empty rows, since the records between can be ones a seat may not see, and a filter that keeps only rows with nothing against them still draws its grid. The graview-lens skill says so, and `verify-declared` holds it in a browser.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the declaration are unchanged. `coverageUnlinkedWords` is new on `@graview/core`.
- 4feaed1: A document is checked, described and taught in its own words. `graview check` refuses a rule's repair that is an act on another kind than the rule judges — a rule over tasks offering an act on modules hands that act the task, and it could only refuse — as `repair-wrong-kind`, naming both kinds and the act, with the acts on the judged kind as the fix; it is asked of every rule written in the rule language (a document's rules and `expressionRule`), whose repairs are handed the record they judged, and not of a rule that is a function, which names its repairs' arguments itself. Every finding `graview check --document` hands back is located by its JSON path — `acts.file.connects`, `rules.no-orphan-task.repairs.1`, `kinds.task.glance` — and its sentence and fix name the document's places, never `defineMutation(...)` or `defineNode(...)`. Kinds whose hues sit within 12° on the color wheel are one answer for `graview describe` and `graview check`, measured round the wheel: describe says so as before, and check now asks it out loud as the note `brand-hues-crowded`. The `AGENTS.md` that `graview create` writes, and the `graview-new-app`, `graview-node-kind`, `graview-invariant` and `graview-seed` skills, teach the document path beside the TypeScript one: `app.json` as a graview-document, its schema at `node_modules/@graview/core/dist/document.schema.json`, `graview check --document`, `graview describe --document` and `graview preview app.json --seed seed.json`.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes — `repair-wrong-kind` is a new error, so a document or an `expressionRule` that checked clean with a repair on another kind now fails `graview check` and a host's `compileDocument` refuses it (no example app or fixture relied on one); `brand-hues-crowded` is a new note, never a failure. A finding's `where` for a repair names its place in the list: `defineInvariant("x").repairs[0]` (was `.repairs`). A document finding from the checker carries a JSON path where it carried the TypeScript call, and its message and fix are reworded to match. Ops, stored formats, the wire, the document format and tool names and schemas are unchanged.
- e46f304: A figure may be said large. In a home whose headline wears a display face, a stat tile's number was drawn at a figure's modest size with no way to say it was the point of the page. `{"figure": "count(all('finding'))", "label": "findings", "emphasis": "large"}` draws it at the size of the home's own heading, in the display face (smaller on a card or a row). The check refuses any other word at its path (`view-emphasis`) and refuses `emphasis` on `{"figure": true}`, the kind's picture. `FIGURE_EMPHASES` is exported beside `FIGURE_FORMATS`.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: a figure block takes a new key, `emphasis`, whose one value is `"large"`, and a new code, `view-emphasis`, refuses another. A document that does not say it is unchanged. Ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tool schemas are unchanged.
- 6862f3a: A coverage orders its rows and its columns, and a status board its cards, as the declaration says. "Releases by era" listed its releases in the order they were written. A coverage lens takes `sort` for its rows and `columnSort` for its columns, and a status board `sort` for the cards in each column, written as a kind's list writes its order: a field (`"released"`), or `{ "by": "released", "direction": "desc" }`, with `label` the record's name; numbers as numbers, words and dates in the page's order, and a record with none last. Within a coverage's row groups the order holds before its `rowRef`. The lens, `describePlace` and the checker read one comparison (`sortForGrid`), and an order by a field the kind does not have is a warning by its path (`lens-sort`, "release has no field "year" to order by …") and draws as written. A document's reader holds the shape. The graview-lens skill says so, and `verify-declared` holds the order in a browser.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged. A document's lens `options.sort` and `options.columnSort` are held to a field or `{ by, direction: "asc" | "desc" }` by its reader, where any value was passed over before; the coverage and status board lenses take `sort` (and the coverage `columnSort`) as options. `sortForGrid`, `gridSortOf`, `gridSortProblem` and `GridSort` are new on `@graview/core`. A new warning code, `lens-sort`.
- 37f2b3b: A kind's list opens in the order and groups its declaration says (Nick, prioritizing the Graview Roadmap in a Graview app). Only a blocks lens could sort and group; a kind's own list, `/requests`, could not be told to, so done and declined requests read among the new ones. `pages.lists.<kind>` now takes, beside its `filters`, a `sort` — a field, a computed field (a rank the kind works out) or `label`, lowest first unless `"desc"`, with a `then` for ties — and a `group`: a choice or yes-or-no field, an `order` its groups come in, and the `closed` ones, drawn last and shut. A closed group is a disclosure: its heading is a button saying "done · 12 requests", expanded or not, the rows it controls drawn once it opens; it stands open when the list is narrowed by words or by the very field it groups, and `?open=done` keeps it open, so an opened group is a link too. A person still sorts or groups the list another way from its arranging line, and the address says so; a declared order keeps its tiebreak whenever the list is in it. `graview check` refuses a list sorted by what the kind does not have, grouped by a field that is not a choice or a yes or no, or ordering or closing a value that is not one of its choices, each in a sentence at its path (`pages-list-arrangement`, an error). `arrange-pages` sets them and says what it did (the list of requests opens grouped by "status", "done" and "declined" closed at the end), as `diffDocuments` does; a field renamed is followed, and a field removed, a computed field no longer worked out or a choice dropped lets go of what named it, said. `describeApp` says how each declared list opens. The graview-lists skill teaches it.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and wire messages are unchanged. The document format takes `pages.lists.<kind>.sort` (`"<key>"` or `{ "by", "direction"?: "asc" | "desc", "then"?: "<key>" | { "by", "direction"? } }`) and `pages.lists.<kind>.group` (`"<field>"` or `{ "by", "order"?: [...], "closed"?: [...] }`), optional within `graview-document@1`; a build that predates them refuses a document that says them. `arrange-pages` takes `sort: { "<kind>": … | null }` and `group: { "<kind>": … | null }`, and setting a kind's `filters` no longer drops what else its list says. New check finding code `pages-list-arrangement` (an error). `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say "The feature list opens sorted by "rank", highest first, then "name", A to Z." `PagesArrangement.lists` is `Record<string, KindList>` (`KindList`, `KindListSort`, `KindListGroup` from `@graview/core`); `Sort` may carry `then`, and `ArrangeContext` takes `read` for a computed field's value. `@graview/core/arrange` exports `listOpening`, `listSort`, `listGroup`, `orderListGroups`, `kindListFindings`, `listFieldsOf` and `withWorkedOut`. A kind's list page reads `?open=<value>,…`; a closed group's section is `[data-testid="list-group"][data-closed="shut" | "open"]` and its button `[data-testid="list-group-toggle"]`.
- 5b8ac1f: A lens can be kept off the lists (Roadmap f-hidden-lenses). A kind could be left off the home, but a lens could not be left off anything, so an app whose custom home links to lenses as targets ended with a long page list of them. A lens marked `hidden: true` is still a place at its address — a link, a view's `navigate` and `/places/<as>` reach it, and `placesOf` lists it with `hidden: true` — but the bar's places, the scene's tabs (unless it is the one shown), the page list and the home's gallery do not offer it, and it never becomes what its kind draws when an address names no picture. In a document `set-lens-hidden` sets it — `{ "op": "set-lens-hidden", "title": "Building now", "hidden": true }`, with `on` where two lenses share a title — and says so ("The lens "Building now" is kept off the bar and the page list; a link or navigate still reaches it.", and "… is listed on the bar and the page list again."), as `diffDocuments` does; a lens drawn anew in a hidden one's place (`add-lens` with `replace`) stays hidden, and a lens `pages.primary` stands on the bar is refused, in a sentence, until it is taken out of `primary`. `graview check` says a `hidden` that is not true or false (`lens-hidden-not-a-boolean`, a warning), a hidden lens with no title to be a place (`lens-hidden-untitled`, a note), and `pages.primary` naming a hidden lens (`pages-primary-hidden`, a warning). In TypeScript a lens says `hidden: true` beside its `title`, and a registration `views.register(kind, cell, View, { title, hidden: true })`. The studio reads a hidden lens as hidden and keeps one off the lists, or lists it again, with its own act, said to the document as `set-lens-hidden`. The graview-pages and graview-studio skills teach it.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored records and the wire are unchanged. The document format takes `lenses[].hidden` (a boolean, optional within `graview-document@1`); a build that predates it draws the lens listed. New edit op `set-lens-hidden` (`title`, `on?`, `hidden`); `EDIT_OPS` lists it. New check finding codes `lens-hidden-not-a-boolean` (a warning), `lens-hidden-untitled` (a note) and `pages-primary-hidden` (a warning). `DocumentDiff`'s sentences may say "The lens "…" is kept off the bar and the page list; a link or navigate still reaches it." `LensDeclaration.hidden`, `DrawnLens.hidden`, `Place.hidden`, `ViewMeta.hidden`, `ViewRegistration.hidden` and `AppPlace.hidden` (now a lens's too) are `boolean | undefined`. The studio's meta-schema gives `lens` a `hidden` field and the act `set-lens-hidden` (`id`, `hidden`).
- 6862f3a: A list shows more past a page, and groups by a linked record. A list block held at most 100 records and a `limit` past 100 was refused, so 158 songs had to be split by their names into two lists; and a list grouped only by a choice field, so songs by genre, where a genre is a record, could not be said. A list now holds up to its `limit` (1,000 at most, the most it holds unsaid), draws a page of 100 and a "Show 58 more" button that draws the next page — under their headings, where it is grouped — and a list cut past what it holds still says "See all" or "and N more". `group` takes a relation as a kind's list writes a field: `"inGenre"`, or `"out('inGenre')"` and `"in('writtenBy')"` to say which way, a heading per linked record in the order of their names, a record linked to two under both, and one linked to none last, under "No genre". A group by what is neither a choice nor a relation of the kind listed is refused by its path, as before. The graview-lists skill says so, and `verify-declared` holds both in a browser.
  
  A hosted page's first load carries 4.0 KB more up front for these and the other fixes from the Beatles' discography (a coverage that says it has nothing to cross and orders its rows and columns, a career calendar's span, a field called label kept a field, a place known by its title), and 3.3 KB more where it is handed a compiled app, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/lenses-and-views-a-hosted-page.json` and `…-a-hosted-page-handed-a-compiled-app.json`; the embed's pages face, every face, the embed with the studio handed in and two of the guest host's budgets are raised in their own files beside them.
  
  Compatibility: ops and stored records are unchanged. A list block's `limit` is a whole number from 1 to 1,000 where it was 1 to 100, and its `group` may name a relation; a document that compiled compiles. `MAX_LIST_LIMIT` is 1,000, and `LIST_PAGE` (100) is new. A resolved list (`ResolvedList`) keeps its first page in `members` and the records after it in `rest`, with `everyGroup` over them all where it is grouped; `more` counts the rest; `ListGroupOf` names a group's shape, and `groupRelationOf` reads a group by a relation.
- 412abf0: A misnamed kind or field is told both naming rules and its right name. A document's kinds, acts, rules and roles are kebab-case (`tool-note`) and its fields and relations camelCase (`supersededBy`), and the checker said one rule at a time, so the first write met the trap twice. Now a refusal of either says both, and its fix names the spelling it wants: `kinds.toolNote` is told `Call it "tool-note"`, a field `superseded-on` `Call it "supersededOn"`, and a value naming a kind (`"creates": "toolNote"`) the same. A per-place key rather than a form folded behind a button, because the author knows which places are read: a button would still put an act on every summary, and a place that is written keeps its form open as before. Linking picks a record there and never touches the other app: the record that links keeps which one under the relation's name, as `"house:h1"` (or a list), so its derived edit sets it, undoes it, logs it and keeps it from a seat a sight keeps it from like any value. `graview check` says in sentences what such a relation cannot be — `linked-mixed`, `linked-apps`, `linked-target`, `linked-inverse`, `linked-append-only`, `act-linked`, `rule-linked` — with the checker, so a page that compiles without it carries none of their words. When the far value changes, the host calls `recordLinkedChange(store, { ref, fields, app })` and the reading app's history keeps an op authored by the other app on each record that links it — "Buy it outright: the linked record changed in House — list price" — naming what changed and none of its values. `describe_app` lists each relation under "Linked from other apps" with how to link one, the derived edit's tool says its argument is a record of that app as `"<kind>:<id>"`, and a seat finds one with `find_linked`, listed only for an app with such a relation. `@graview/core/linked` holds `linkedRecordsOver`, the host over stores held in memory, read through each far store's `seenBy(viewer)`, which the tests read two compiled documents through and a host holding both apps can use as it is; `graview-linked-records` is the skill that teaches it. A hosted page's first load is 5 107 bytes larger (550 088 over the vendors document, 544 981 on main), 4 648 handed a compiled app, both under their budgets; what draws a linked record is a chunk fetched with the first one drawn, and the embed's budgets are raised by files of their own: the pages face 4 585 / 1 704 bytes minified / gzipped, every face 20 162 / 7 423, the studio handed in 13 618 / 4 936.

- 412abf0: A running preview holds its data folder, so nothing writes it behind the preview's back. `graview preview app.json --data data` keeps the graph in memory and writes it back as it changes, and `graview sync-seed --apply` against the same folder said it had landed while the open pages kept the old records (44 instead of 50) and the preview's next write could put them back: a write lost with nobody told. A dry run of `sync-seed` still reads beside a preview and says the apply would be refused. A hold whose process has died is taken over, so a crash leaves nothing to delete by hand. Refusing was chosen over watching the folder or applying through the server: it is the one answer under which no write is lost and the person is told which process to stop. Tested with real processes in a scratch folder.

What a reader scans is the top of the view, and a group is one thing on the page, so that is what the 40 counts; the ceiling of 120 in all stays because every block is worked out on every draw, and a view nobody could read should still be refused. `view-size` says which of the two was passed: "has 41 blocks at its top; a view has at most 40 there, a group or a condition counting as one", or "has 121 blocks counting the ones inside its groups and conditions; a view has at most 120 in all". `MAX_VIEW_BLOCKS_IN_ALL` is exported beside `MAX_VIEW_BLOCKS`.   Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: a view that was refused for more than 40 blocks counted inside its groups now compiles when it has at most 40 at its top and 120 in all; a view of more than 120 blocks in all, which needed more than 40 at its top before, is still refused. `view-size` keeps its code with new words. - 6862f3a: A worker view shows an `https:` image from an origin its host lists. An artwork whose picture was a Wikimedia address drew nothing in a view, because a view loads nothing the host has not made or holds; the only way round it was a `data:` image in a text field, which is cut at 20,000 characters. A host now lists the origins a view's images may come from — `images: { origins: ["https://upload.wikimedia.org"] }` on `mountWorkerView`, on a registered worker view's definition, and on `runWorkerViewHeadless` — and an image there is drawn as the view gave it, asked for with no referrer (`referrerpolicy="no-referrer"`, set on every `img` before its source), so the origin learns nothing of the page it is drawn on. An allow-list rather than an image proxy, because a proxy fetches whatever address a view hands it from the host's own server, and an address is data a view writes: it would carry what the view reads to any origin through the host, and to the host's network besides. With a list the view never chooses an origin, nothing is fetched on the host's side, and a list entry that is not an `https:` origin, an `http:` address and one with a password in it are refused. Any other address is refused as before, and now said once, in the view's console and to the host's `onProblem`: "The image "https://collector.example/c.png" was not drawn. Its origin is not one the host lists for images (https://upload.wikimedia.org). A view shows a data: image, the app's own image (/graview/assets/…), a picture on a record (pictures in its manifest), or an https: image on an origin the host lists (images.origins)." A headless run says the same. A picture the app holds belongs in an `image` field, which keeps the uploaded file (what `add_image` stores, `/graview/assets/<sha256>.<ext>`) and a view draws by that path. The graview-view-drawing skill teaches it, and the guest harness holds it in a browser.

- 5b8ac1f: A view's `navigate` goes to a kind's list or a lens narrowed, and to a section of a page (Roadmap f-navigate-filters). The filter is held to what the kind can be filtered by — the offers the list's own arranging line makes — and written in the words that line already writes, so the routed face's address is `/features?filter=stage%3Abuilding#shipping-soon` and can be shared: opened from a link it lands on the list narrowed, the section scrolled to and given the keyboard. A lens is gone to narrowed the same way (`/places/<as>?filter=`, the records it is handed narrowed and said so over it, "Only those where stage is building. - 6862f3a: A view's places are known by their slug and by their title, and named with both when one is not there. A home view linked `data-place="songs-by-writer"`, a lens the app has, and the check of it said the place did not exist and listed the app's places as "undefined, undefined, …": its host handed the places as an app lists them (`placesOf`, each a `slug`), and the check read a guest's `as`. - 6862f3a: A view's style may hold a header in place and reset a button of its own. `graview.style` refused `position: sticky` and `all: unset`, so a view could not keep its header on screen as it scrolled, and the browser's button chrome leaked into the toggles it drew. Both are kept. `sticky` sticks within its own parent's box, and the region a view is drawn in is painted contained (`contain: layout paint style`, `overflow: clip`, a stacking context of its own), so a sticky header never paints past the view or over the app; `fixed`, which escapes to the page's viewport, is still refused. `all` takes only the four keywords every property takes (`unset`, `initial`, `inherit`, `revert`), which reset and load nothing. The graview-view-drawing skill says so, and the guest harness holds both in a browser.

- cf85240: An app can name one standard emoji as its page icon and, where it has no logo, its mark. A brand's icon was an SVG or a path, so an app somebody made in a chat had a blank tab and an empty corner of the bar until somebody drew it a picture. `brand.favicon` now also takes `{ emoji: "🏡" }`, in a TypeScript `Brand` and in a document alike: exactly one emoji as the reader sees it (Intl.Segmenter), and one of the standard emoji every emoji set draws (`RGI_Emoji`), so a skin tone, a joined emoji and a flag — 🏡, 👩🏽‍🌾, 🇺🇸 — are each one, and two emoji, letters or nothing are refused in words: `"🏡🏡" cannot be the app's icon, because it is 2 emoji, and a mark is one` (`brand-emoji`, at `brand.favicon.emoji`), from `compileDocument`, `graview check` on a declared app, and `editDocument`. `set-brand` takes `favicon: { emoji }` and says "The app's page icon and mark become 🏡." (or "The app's page icon becomes 🏡." when the app has a logo), and the preview of a changed document says the same sentence. Where the brand has no logo, `AppMark` draws the emoji as text on the app bar of both faces and in an embed, and the routed face's static pages put it in the masthead; with a logo, the logo is the mark and the emoji is only the icon. `faviconHref` makes the icon an SVG with the emoji as text, so a self-hosted app's tab shows it in the reader's own emoji font; a host that wants it alike on every system renders it from a bundled emoji set into icon PNGs and serves those instead, as Graview Cloud does. The every-face and studio-handed-in budgets are raised by 2_385 / 795 and 2_409 / 920 bytes (scripts/budget-raises/an-emoji-as-the-apps-mark-every-face.json, scripts/budget-raises/an-emoji-as-the-apps-mark-studio.json); a hosted page's first load stays within its budget.

What Graview Cloud does: render `brand.favicon.emoji` from its bundled emoji set into the icon PNGs it serves, and serve those rather than the framework's text SVG.
- c3564eb: The app's own actor suggests what it is not sure of, is held to what people said, and its run of changes is one row of Activity. A background actor that links and prunes records on its own ("Graview, tidying") could apply a change with its lineage (FR-172) and nothing else: what it was unsure of had nowhere to wait, a person could not say "leave this one alone", an app could not say "never auto-link Vendors", and a pass of thirty links buried a person's own changes in Activity. An author may now say `automated: true` (a `system` or `agent` author: the app's own actor; `isAutomated`).
  
  A suggestion that waits is `suggest(store, calls, { author, lineage, via?, intent? })`, on `@graview/core/tidying`: the calls are judged as `applyAll` would judge them for that author, on a rehearsal, and none of it is applied — the log keeps one op that `suggests` them, with no primitives and its lineage, so it persists, travels and is withheld from a seat exactly as any op is. The same suggestion asked again answers the one that waits, and one that would change nothing is not suggested. `suggestions(store, principal?)` lists what waits, under the seat's sight (or the seat whose view `store` is); `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options)` applies the calls as the person, carrying the suggestion's lineage with `lineage.suggestion` naming it; `declineSuggestion(store, id, options)` is an undo of the suggestion, which keeps its lineage as `undoneLineage`. `declined(store, calls)` answers the op that declined these calls, or any one of them — a declined suggestion, or an actor's confident change a person took back — and the store refuses the app's own actor the same change or suggestion again, in a sentence ("Graview, tidying may not make this again: a person declined it on 2026-10-14."). The log is where suggestions live, rather than a seam a host fills, because accepting and declining are then a `call` and an `undo` like any other, and a host persists nothing new. Both faces show what waits in a quiet Suggested tray beside Activity on the bar — what it would do, who suggested it, its basis and how sure, Accept and Decline — fetched, with what reads which suggestions wait, only once the log holds one, so a hosted page carries up front only what holds the actor to what people said (what the 0.1.30 wave adds up front together is said in `.changeset/the-0-1-30-wave-leaves-the-past-and-the-suggestions-off-the-first-load.md`).
  
  Leave this one alone: every kind is given `leave-alone-<kind>` ("Leave this one alone") and `allow-tidying-<kind>` ("Allow tidying again"), ordinary undoable acts that set and clear `$leftAlone` on the record as archive sets `$archived`, offered on the record's page and the scene's record card beside archive, and said at the head of the record's page and in its card. `isLeftAlone(store, id)` reads it. The store refuses the app's own actor any change that touches a record left alone — a patch, a removal, a link at either end — and a suggestion about one, naming the record. A declaration says what is never tidied: `tidying: "never" | "no-links"` on `defineNode` (a document's kind) and `tidying: "never"` on a relation; the actor is refused a change to a kind never tidied, a link to or from a kind never linked, and a link of a relation never tidied, each in a sentence, and may still suggest them for a person to decide. The document edit `set-tidying` (`{ kind, relation?, tidying: "never" | "no-links" | "allowed" }`) says it in a sentence, `diffDocuments` says the same sentence, and `graview check` refuses a grant naming a leave-alone act of a kind never tidied (`grant-act`) and warns of a relation that says what its kind already says (`tidying-redundant`).
  
  A digest in Activity: consecutive batches by one automated author fold into one row, "Graview, tidying Tidied 6 things today", which opens to each change with its own lineage and its own undo; a person's change and a chat seat's never fold, and a suggestion and its declining are the tray's, not rows. The agent's tools gain `list_suggestions` (the actor's basis handed over as somebody else's words), `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion`. `@graview/core/tidying` makes, settles and reads it all for a host and a face: `suggest`, `suggestions`, `acceptSuggestion`, `declineSuggestion`, `declined`, `suggestionsIn`, `declinesIn`, `declinedIn`, `suggestionKey`, `tidyingOf`, `tidyingRefusal`, `foldDigests`, `digestWords`, `isSuggestionBatch` and `LEFT_ALONE`. The embed's budgets for the pages face alone, every face and the studio handed in are raised by what they grew (`scripts/budget-raises/the-apps-own-actor-*.json`). The graview-lists skill teaches it.
  
  Compatibility: ops gain two optional fields, `suggests` (on an op with no primitives, which folds to nothing) and, on a lineage, `suggestion`; authors gain the optional `automated`; a record may carry `$leftAlone: true`, written by an ordinary `patch-node` — additive within op format 1, and a log folds to the same graph on an earlier version. The wire is unchanged: accepting is a `call` whose lineage names the suggestion, declining an `undo`. The document format gains the optional `kinds.<kind>.tidying` and `kinds.<kind>.edges.<relation>.tidying`, the edit `set-tidying`, and the check finding `tidying-redundant` (a warning); `grant-act` also names a leave-alone act of a kind never tidied. Derived tools: every kind gains `leave-alone-<kind>` and `allow-tidying-<kind>` (`{ id }`) unless it is never tidied, and the agent's surface gains `list_suggestions`, `accept_suggestion` and `decline_suggestion` (`{ suggestion }`). New APIs: `isLeftAlone`, `isAutomated` and the type `Suggestion` on `@graview/core`, and `@graview/core/tidying` with `suggest(store, calls, options: SuggestOptions): Operation | undefined`, `suggestions(store, principal?): readonly Suggestion[]`, `acceptSuggestion(store, id, options?): ApplyResult`, `declineSuggestion(store, id, options?): ApplyResult`, `declined(store, calls): Operation | undefined` and `SuggestOptions`. Stored formats are unchanged.
- 412abf0: The document's JSON Schema types its lenses and its views' blocks. `lenses` items were any object and a view's blocks any value, so an editor reading `document.schema.json` offered nothing for a lens's name, its bindings, `options.blocks` or a block's keys, and a misspelling surfaced only at `graview check`. Each lens is now held to a name `SHIPPED_LENSES` lists (`timeline`, `calendar`, `coverage`, `board`, `plan`, `reach`, `blocks`, `columns`), the options that lens takes, its bindings as roles bound to fields where it binds fields, and the keys a lens declaration has; every block — in a kind's card, row and page, the home view and a blocks lens's `options.blocks` — is held to the closed set `BLOCK_KEYS` names, each block with what it may say besides and the values each takes (tones, formats, a list's sort and grouping, a group's direction). Both are generated where the build writes the schema, from the tables the checker judges by, and a block or a block key added there without a shape stops the build. Every example document and fixture in the tree validates.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration and check finding codes: the compiler reads documents as before. `document.schema.json` is stricter than it was on lenses and blocks, so an editor now underlines a lens Graview does not ship, an option or key a lens does not take, and a block or block key the checker refuses, where the compiler warns (`lens-not-shipped`, `lens-option-unknown`) or refuses (`view-block`, `unknown-key`). Ops, stored formats, the wire and derived tool schemas are unchanged.
- 8ca608c: The GPU capture path keeps a page of live views under the capture cliff. Chrome Canary 158 respelled HTML-in-Canvas and every capture threw; the canvas now says `content="drawable"` as well as `layoutsubtree`, each view carries `drawable`, and the capture call's destination shape is found on the first call. Capture costs about 0.22 ms a view on that build, so retaking every live view every frame held 42, 21, 10 and 6 fps at 64, 128, 256 and 512 views (median of three runs). A live view is now retaken only when the `paint` event's `changedElements` says it changed, a view off the canvas is neither taken nor drawn, a frame spends at most `CAPTURE_BUDGET` (32) retakes, and a view that keeps changing faster than that is handed to the DOM path and drawn at the same transform under the canvas. The discography holds 417 fps with 512 live views moving every frame, and 125 with the processor at a quarter speed, and `verify-scale` holds it at 256 and 512. `attachGpu()` from `@graview/render/gpu` is the capture path as a Scene's `attachRenderer`; the discography opts in with `?gpu=1`. The uniforms of every view go up in one write, and geometry is reported only for what moved, once a crowd stops.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. New on `@graview/render`: `CAPTURE_BUDGET`, `PlanOptions.changed` and `.captureBudget`, `PlannedView.dom`, `FramePlan.deferred` and `.culled`, and a `viewId` on each of `FramePlan.placements`; `planFrame` no longer draws a view wholly off the canvas. New on `@graview/render/gpu`: `attachGpu`, `AttachGpuOptions`, `GpuScene`, `CompositedFrame`, `markDrawable`, `changedElementsOf`, `Compositor.paint`, and `CompositorOptions.captureBudget` and `.transparent`; `Compositor.render` takes the changed elements and returns a `CompositedFrame`. `SceneProps.attachRenderer`'s argument gains `drawOnDom`.
- eb3bdb7: A right-click on the pages opens the scene's context menu. It was the scene's alone, so on a list, a record's page or a picture's page a right-click met the browser's menu and the acts were a scroll away. It is the same menu now — the same pane, the same acts `@graview/tools` derives, narrowed by the seat, with what is withheld said the way the scene says it — opened on what the gesture landed on: a list's row, a record's page, a card or a link to a record, a lens's mark, all about that record, with "Open", "Open in a new tab" and "Show in the scene" beside its acts; a value on a record's page, with "Change “Due”" first, which opens that value's own edit in place; a picture's ground, about its place, headed with its title and offering what its kind offers ("Add a task"); and the page's own ground, with the list's kind's acts and Find. Shift+F10 and the context-menu key open it on what has the keyboard, and a finger resting on the page opens it on a phone (on a list that chooses, resting on a row still chooses it). The keyboard goes into the menu and back where it was opened; Escape closes it.
  
  The browser keeps its own menu in a text field or editable text, over text somebody selected, and whenever Shift is held with the right-click — the way to the browser's "Open link in new tab", which the menu also offers itself. A design's own value takes part by carrying `data-graview-field` beside `data-graview-editable`, and its own row by being a link to the record or carrying `data-graview-record`.
  
  A host adds a section of its own to the menu on either face: `menuSections` on `GraviewProvider`, on `embed()` and on the pages' context, each a function told what the menu was opened on (`MenuTarget`: `face`, `on`, `about`, `field`, `place`, `href`, `element`) that answers a `MenuSection` — rows (`items`: `{ id, label, run, hint? }`) or what it draws itself (`render(close)`), before the acts (`at: "start"`) or after them — or nothing. A face adds its own with `MenuSections` from `@graview/react/drawing`. `graview-desk` teaches the seam, and `graview-lists` how a design's own rows and values take part. The menu and its pane stay off a page's first load: they are fetched when the page is first idle, or at the first right-click, and a hosted page's first load carries nothing more for them. The embed's pages face alone carries 3.7 KB more in all (the menu's pane, now fetched there too), every face 0.5 KB and the embed with the studio handed in 5.0 KB, raised in `scripts/budget-raises/the-pages-open-the-scenes-context-menu-*.json`.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes. New on the provider, `embed()` and `PageContext`: `menuSections` (the context menu's section seam). New on `PointerMenu`: `about`, `face`, `field`, `place`, `href`, `title` and `returnTo`, all optional. `Inspector` takes `openActs`. The `acts` popover is drawn on the pages too.

## 0.1.29

### Patch Changes

- 55ec52b: A date can repeat: a weekly practice, a monthly bill or a yearly renewal is one record, not one per occurrence made by hand (the Roadmap's f-recurring-dates). A document says `"repeats": true` on a `date` or `datetime` field; a declaration puts `startsAtRepeat: repeatField()` beside `startsAt`, and `toDocument` writes it as `repeats`. A record keeps its first occurrence in the date and, beside it under the date's name and `Repeat`, how it repeats: `{ "every": "week", "on": ["tue"], "until": "2026-12-15" }` — `every` day, week, month or year, an `interval`, `on` weekdays for a week or days of the month for a month (`-1` the last), `until`, `count` and `except` days — or `null` once it no longer does. The store refuses anything else (`validRepeat`). Occurrences are worked out and never stored (`occurrences`, `nextOccurrence`, `dateOn`): a rule, a template, `today()`'s comparisons, a list's sort, filter and date groups, and a kind's stop read the next occurrence on the day they are judged on, so the tick that turns the day (FR-176) moves it with nothing written. A day a month lacks falls on the month's last (Jan 31 monthly is Feb 28, Feb 29 yearly is Feb 28 off a leap year), a moment's time of day is kept as written so six in the evening stays six across a clock change, and `count` counts skipped days. The calendar draws every occurrence in the days it shows, marked ↻ and "· repeats" and not dragged, and counts a series once, at its next, where it is a glance; a kind's stop opens on a calendar by a series' next occurrence and counts it in "in the next 7 days"; a record's facts, a row and a card say "Every Tuesday, next Oct 27" in the app's locale (`repeatAsRead`, `repeatingDateAsRead` on `@graview/core/words`). Three derived acts on a kind with a date that repeats, permitted as its edit is, each one undo with a sentence: `skip-<kind>` ("Skip this one", `{ id, on }`) adds the day to `except`; `detach-<kind>` ("Change just this one") makes that occurrence a record of its own, with the series' fields, the links it makes and what was changed, and an exception on the series; `split-<kind>` ("Change from here") ends the series the day before and starts a new one that day, `continues` naming the old. A record's page has a "Repeat…" control beside the date: a picker (does not repeat, daily, weekly, monthly, yearly; every how many; which weekdays; the month's day or its last; until) that says the rule back in words as it is chosen, every control labeled, kept by the edit act; a form asks for a repeat with the same picker (`FormField` gains `control: "repeat"`). `editDocument` gains `set-repeats`, the studio offers "Say whether it repeats" and writes `repeatField()` back, a migration moves a repeat with its renamed date, `graview types` writes `<date>Repeat?: Repeat | null`, and an act's tool schema describes it with `"x-graview-repeat": true`. Time zones beyond the one a moment says, and the rest of RFC 5545 (by-week-number, by-set-position, hourly), are left out. A hosted page's first load is 5 797 bytes larger minified, 2 381 gzipped, than the batch it joined (549 531 / 192 258 against 543 734 / 189 877, under its budgets of 550 912 / 192 512), and 5 425 / 2 274 handed a compiled app (507 320 / 177 411 against 509 440 / 177 664): the occurrence math, the repeat's check and the three acts, which a rule and the store need as the page loads; the words, the picker and the calendar's drawing are fetched with what draws them. The embed's every face, the studio handed in and the pages face alone are raised by files of their own. A document's `datetime` field now reads back as a date and time (`fieldSpecOf`), so a kind's stop finds it for its calendar and its dates, and the stop says its date by the field's label ("in the next 7 days by starts"). verify-repeats holds it at desk width in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The declaration and check finding codes: the document format gains `repeats` on a date or datetime field, within `graview-document@1` and `graview-compiled@1`, the edit `set-repeats` and the finding `repeat-clash`; a build that predates them refuses the key as `unknown-key`. Derived tool names and input schemas: a kind with a date that repeats gains `skip-<kind>`, `detach-<kind>` and `split-<kind>`, and its `edit-<kind>` takes `<date>Repeat`; a kind with none is unchanged. `ReadableField` gains `repeats`, `PlacedEntry` gains `repeats`, `DerivedAct` gains `skip`, `detach` and `split`. Ops and primitives, the stored formats and the wire are unchanged. What a host does: serve 0.1.29 or later before writing `repeats`; a record's repeat travels as any other field value.
- 3f607a8: A door for everybody is not a key to every record (the ninth walk, W-201). A kind's derived edit, remove, archive, bring-back and merge ride the declared acts that make or write it — and a beekeeping co-op's "Anybody may join" (`roles: "*"` on the act that makes a member) let a novice remove her mentor from the co-op, and handed somebody browsing the tools to change, archive or merge any member they could see. Riding only a grant to every role, a derived act now reaches the records that seat made, as the store's log says who made each; a role's own grant on the acts it rides, or a grant that names the act or says `*`, reaches the rest as before. Refused, it says so: "Not permitted: “Remove the beekeeper” on a beekeeper — only on what you made; a coordinator can." The graview-permissions skill says it.
  
  Compatibility: derived tools are unchanged. Narrowed: a seat whose only way to a kind's derived `edit-`, `remove-`, `archive-`, `unarchive-` or `merge-<kind>` is a grant with `roles: "*"` naming the declared acts that make (or write) the kind is now refused it, as `forbidden`, on a record it did not make (`store.permits`, `apply`, what a face offers and an agent's call), where it reached every record it could see. Unchanged: a grant of everything to everybody (`{ roles: "*", mutations: "*" }`, which `add-sight` writes on a document with no policy) and any grant naming the derived act or saying `*` reach every record; a role's own grant on the acts it rides reaches every record of the kind. A store with no policy permits everything, as before. `permits` from `@graview/core` takes an optional ninth argument, who made the record. docs/stability.md says it under derived tools.
- 42dbfb7: A guest view is handed the pictures on the records it reads, as addresses the host made from the bytes (FR-175). A record keeps a file as a path on the host (FR-162's `FileValue`), which the host serves to the app's members with their credentials; a worker view and a frame run at an opaque origin, so a view of a job's photographs was handed paths it could not load, and learned the host's paths besides.
