A worker view whose manifest says `pictures: true`, or `pictures: { width: 400 }` (the widest it draws a picture, in CSS pixels; 1 200 by default), and a frame whose host passes `pictures` to `mountGuestView` or `guestView`, is now handed each file on a record as a `GuestFile` in the record's own field, never the path: `{ name, type, bytes, width?, height?, caption?, src?, left? }`. A picture (`image/*`, not SVG) carries `src`, which the host made from the bytes it fetched on its own origin as the reader (`credentials: "same-origin"`): a `blob:` URL of the page for a worker view (a `data:` image where the page's policy refuses `blob:` images), a `data:` image for a frame, as the brand's logo is (FR-127). It comes without `src` on the first push and the view is pushed again once the host has made it. A document, and an SVG, is its name, type and size alone, and is never fetched. Only files on records the guest is handed are fetched, so a file on a record the seat may not see is never asked for. A picture wider than the view draws one (`width` × the screen's pixel ratio, at most 2) is made smaller on a canvas, kept in its own type where the canvas writes it; one picture is at most `limits.pictureBytes` as handed (1 500 000 by default) and a view's pictures together at most `limits.picturesBytes` (12 000 000); a picture left out says why in `left`, naming the view — "The picture "porch.png" on "Porch repair" is left out: the view "Job photos" is handed at most 12,000,000 bytes of pictures, and the pictures before it took them." — and a worker view's console and `onProblem` hear it once. Pictures past the view's budget are not fetched. A frame that asks is answered once the pictures' host has arrived, and a worker view starts once it has; the pictures' host is a chunk of its own, fetched only for a guest that asks. `checkManifest` says what is wrong with a manifest's `pictures`. Headless (`graview view check`, `runWorkerViewHeadless`), a view that asks is handed each file without its path and without `src`. The graview-worker-view skill says in one line how a view shows a record's picture. `guest-sandbox --transport=pictures` draws a worker view of a crew's jobs as a member on one of them, in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox: the pictures are `blob:` URLs of the page made from bytes fetched once each with the reader's session, the step's byte for byte and the porch's, 1 600 pixels across, made 400; the PDF and the SVG are said by name, type and size; nothing the view drew names a path on the host; and the roof's photograph, on a job the member is not on, is never requested. A hosted page's first load is 391 bytes larger (587 597 bytes over the vendors document, 587 206 on main; 546 594 handed a compiled app, 546 203 on main), the frame's wait for the pictures' host; the guest host before a worker is drawn is 400 bytes more, registering a worker view 547 more, and a worker view on the open kit 5 353 more, the pictures' host itself, each raised by a file of its own in `scripts/budget-raises/`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-175.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names are unchanged. The guest protocol is additive and `GUEST_PROTOCOL` stays 1: no message is new, and a guest that does not ask for pictures is handed each file as the record keeps it, as before; one that asks is handed a `GuestFile` in that field instead. New: `GuestFile` from `@graview/guest` and `@graview/guest/host`; `WorkerViewManifest.pictures`, `MountGuestViewOptions.pictures`, `GuestViewOptions.pictures`; `GuestLimits.pictureBytes` and `GuestLimits.picturesBytes` (`GuestPictureLimits`), and `GuestPictures`, from `@graview/guest/host` and `/host/views`; `GuestHostOptions.hand`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-175. What Graview Cloud does: nothing to serve, since its files route already serves each file to the reader with their credentials on the page's own origin; a view it stores may say `pictures` in its manifest once `capabilities().shipped` includes FR-175, and a view's author draws `<img src="${photo.src}">` once `src` comes.
- 3accacb: A kind opens to something useful. Gone into, a district the app drew no picture of — "open", a double press, `#focus=aggregate:<kind>` — drew six chips and "+30 more" in a box floating in an empty field, under a "⚠ 36" that was the number of rooms in the warning tone: nothing said what the records were, what was wrong with them or what to do. It opens now to the kind's stop, in the box a lens fills, derived from the declaration and the records. Its head says the plural, how many and what they are, and offers the kind's creating act first ("Add a room"), asked in place, with the rest behind "Acts…". Under it, only where there is something to say: how many are overdue or due in the next seven days on its due date, each a press that narrows the list; its problems counted by the rule that found them ("1 problem in rooms: A workshop fits its room"), each opening the bar's own rows and their repairs, and "Show only the rooms with a problem"; and what was added or changed since this reader last looked. Then the picture its data chooses: a board by its main choice (the lifecycle's field, or one called status, stage, state or phase) up to 300 records; a calendar on its main date where today's month holds records a fortnight long or less; otherwise a table of the kind's glance and its relations, read as the far records' names, an empty value said as a quiet dash ("none" to a screen reader), 50 rows at a time with "Show 50 more". Each row is its record to the scene, as a record is anywhere: pressed, it is chosen; right-clicked, long-pressed or given the menu key, its acts open; its name goes to it and its "⋯", drawn with an edge at rest, opens the same acts. Opened from altitude — a double press on its district, or Enter on it chosen — a kind with no picture opens to its stop in the panel beside the map a lens opens in, taking the place of a lens or another kind's stop already open there (one panel at a time; with a lens open it used to open behind it), its district shut on the map; the address says so, the keyboard lands on its heading, and Back returns to what was open. The other shape is one press away, the stop says the picture is derived from the declaration, and "Keep as a lens" keeps it as the seat keeps a view it drew (`add-lens`). The stop is a region named by its heading, a real table on a desk and rows of words in a narrow box, with 44-pixel targets under a finger and nothing scrolling sideways; it is fetched when a district is first gone into, so a page that never opens one carries a door of about a hundred bytes. `@graview/tools/draft` gains `recipeDraft(recipe, options)`, a draft from a recipe with no ask, titled and judged as an asked-for one ("Every room" for a plain list). The graview-lists skill says what a kind opens to and what to declare first, and `pnpm verify kind-stop` walks every example's kinds with no picture at a desk and a phone, light and dark.
  
  Compatibility: from altitude, opening a district with no picture now focuses it (`#overview=1&focus=aggregate:<kind>`) and draws its stop in `[data-testid="lens-panel"]`, where it used to choose it (`sel=kind:<kind>`, the district panel), which a single press still does; `LayoutOptions.besideTheMap` (set by the scene where a panel is drawn) keeps a focused group's district shut and stamps nothing in the city's middle. A view an app registers for a kind at many × full replaces the stop as before; the framework's default for that cell is now the stop, fetched lazily, drawing the old list until it arrives. New test ids are `kind-stop`, `kind-stop-primary`, `kind-stop-acts`, `kind-stop-problems`, `kind-stop-as-<shape>`, `kind-stop-keep`, `kind-stop-table`, `kind-stop-row-go`, `kind-stop-row-acts`, `kind-stop-more` and the arranging line `kind-arrange-*`, and the table's rows carry `data-graview-pick` as a record's mark does; `@graview/tools/draft` adds `recipeDraft`. The embed's every-face budget is raised by the stop's chunk (`scripts/budget-raises/a-kind-opens-to-something-useful-every-face.json`); the hosted page's up-front budget is unchanged.
- 897c7bb: A list reads well by default. On the Program's Sessions grouped by day, the title stood at forty pixels, every row's name was set bold and wrapped to a third and fourth line on a phone so every row shouted and nothing was secondary, each row said "Sep 25, 2017" under a heading that said "Sep 25, 2017", its date stood at whatever x its length left it, and the related links were underlined heavily in the line's own ink. The routed face now sets its words on one scale, a minor third from the reader's own rem, written by the theme as four custom properties: `--graview-text-small` (0.833rem: a row's date, a count, a relation, a group's heading), `--graview-text-body` (1rem: reading text and a row's name), `--graview-text-heading` (1.2rem: a section) and `--graview-text-title` (1.728rem: a page's title, where it was 30 to 40 pixels by the window's width); the sizes the pages had picked between them, from 0.75rem to 1.4375rem and a title by the window's width, are gone, and the static export reads the same four. A row of a kind's list, and a hit on the search page, is one anatomy: its name at the reading size and a medium weight, two lines at most with the rest on its page (and in its title when it is long), the whole row its link with a hover, a press and a keyboard ring of its own; its glance small and muted in a column of its own on a desk, one column for the whole list so the dates down it start at one x, and under the name where the list is narrower than 36rem. A list's rows are one line or two, so it keeps two heights. A row leaves off its glance the field its group is by (a date grouped by day, unless the row's value has a time the heading does not say) and a choice the list is narrowed to one value of. A group's heading is small and semibold in the body face, its count beside it, and over the framework's own rows stays at the top of the scroll on a desk. A link on a page is in the accent `graview check` holds to 4.5:1 on the ground, the same visited or not, with a hairline underline at half strength. A page's end clears the ask field by its measured height and a phone's home bar. A declared row's title on a list, and the kind stop's heading, header row, small text and names (at a medium weight), read the same scale. The graview-brand skill says how an app moves a step.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the declaration and derived tools are unchanged. The look moves (docs/stability.md, "Not a surface: the look"): four new custom properties, `--graview-text-small`, `-body`, `-heading` and `-title`, which an app's own stylesheet may set to move a step everywhere it is used. A brand that names its own faces and weights keeps them: headings on the routed face no longer set their own family and weight but take the theme's heading rule (`--graview-font-display`, `--graview-weight-display`), so a brand's display weight now wins where 600 was written over it; a group's heading is set in `--graview-font-body` at `--graview-weight-label`. A row of the derived list is `li.graview-row` holding `a.graview-row-title` and `span.graview-row-glance` (a search hit is the same, still `[data-testid="search-hit"]`); a group's or a section's heading is `h2.graview-group-head`; `record-row`, `records`, `list-group`, `list-section` and `list-index` are as they were. Links on the routed face are drawn in `--graview-accent`, where they were the text's own ink.
- A phone, a place and a length of time are field types of their own. Templates held all three as strings: a phone that was not a link to call, an address that was not a link to a map, and lengths of time nobody could add up. A document says `{ "type": "phone" }`, `{ "type": "place" }` and `{ "type": "duration" }`; a declaration `phoneField()`, `placeField()` and `durationField()`. A phone is kept as it is entered (a form trims it) and refused only when it cannot be one — fewer than three digits, or letters other than an extension's (`PHONE`: digits, spaces, `+`, `-`, `.`, `/`, parentheses, `ext. 12`, `x12`), at most 40 characters; it is said grouped as "(555) 123-4567" for a reader in the US or Canada where it is a North American number and as entered everywhere else, and drawn as a `tel:` link. A place is a line of words, at most 500 characters, drawn as a link that opens the reader's maps app in a tab of its own (`rel="noopener noreferrer"`): maps.apple.com on Apple's platforms, which opens Maps, and Google's universal Maps address elsewhere, which opens the Google Maps app on Android and the map in the browser otherwise — no one address opens every reader's own app (`geo:` does nothing in a desktop browser), and neither needs a key or fetches anything until it is followed. A duration is a whole number of minutes, none less than none, bounded by the field's `min`, `max` and `step` like a number; it is typed as "1h 30m", "90", "1:30" or "1.5 h" (`parseDuration`), said "1 h 30 min" with the locale's figures (`sayDuration`), and sorted and summed as the number it is: a computed field that sums, adds or subtracts durations — `sum(out('rehearsals'), length)`, `planned - spent` — is said as a duration too, as is one a declaration says `as: "duration"`. Each is taken by `editDocument`'s `add-field` and `retype-field` (a duration keeps its range), kept by a migration where the old value reads as one and cleared where it does not, read from a spreadsheet's cell or refused in words, written by `graview types` (a phone and a place as `string`, a duration as `number`), asked for by a form (a `tel` box; a duration in words, sent as minutes), the workbench and a value changed in place, and described to an agent with `"x-graview-type"` (and `"x-graview-unit": "minutes"` for a duration, which a seat sends as 90, never "1h 30m"). A record's page, the scene's record panel and a document card's field block draw the links, each a target of at least 24 pixels; where the value is changed in place, "Call" and "Map" stand beside it. The studio offers, declares and writes back all three. `@graview/core/words` gains `parseDuration`, `sayDuration`, `sayPhone`, `telHref`, `mapsHref`, `fieldSays`, `typedWords`, `typedHref` and `typedFieldOf`. A hosted page's first load is 392 / 168 bytes larger minified / gzipped (360 / 179 handed a compiled app), under its budgets; the embed's every face carries 7 885 / 3 336 more and with the studio handed in 7 242 / 3 150, each raised by a file of its own. verify-field-types holds it at desk width on both faces in three engines.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field types `phone`, `place` and `duration`, and `graview-compiled@1` carries them; a host's build that predates them refuses a document using them by path, and a page that predates them refuses such a compiled app as `compiled-shape` and compiles the document, which refuses the type by path. These carry no FR id, so a host validates a document that uses them against the `document.schema.json` of the oldest build it serves. `ReadableField` gains `href`, `ScalarField` gains `holds`, `ArgShape` gains `holds` (words) and `duration` (a number), and a computed field may say `as: "duration"`. A derived tool's input schema for a declaration with none of these fields is unchanged.
- 3b06199: A record can link another app, and the host says what that app is called (FR-171). A relation's `to` names kinds inside one document, so a scenario in one app could not point at a house in another. The first step is a field: `{ "type": "app-link" }` in a document, `appLinkField()` in a declaration, holding another app's address — `@owner/app`, an id its host knows it by, or an absolute http(s) address, at most 500 characters (`isAppAddress`). The store refuses anything else as `invalid`, `editDocument`'s `add-field` and `retype-field` take the type, a migration keeps a value that is an address and clears one that is not, a spreadsheet's cell is read as one or refused in words (FR-177), `graview types` writes it as a string said to be another app's address, a form asks for it with "@owner/app or https://…" as its placeholder, and an act's tool schema gives it the address's `pattern` and `"x-graview-app-link": true` so an agent sets an address rather than a name. A host hands `resolveApp(address, viewer)` to `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` or the routed face's context, answering `{ name, icon?, headline?, href }`, `{ denied: true }` or `null`: the field draws as that app's icon, name and home headline, linked to `href`, on a record's page and in the scene's record panel, and as its icon and name on a list's row and a document card's field block. A viewer the host refuses sees "linked app (no access)" and nothing of the app. Without a resolver, or where the host knows no such app, the address is drawn as it is kept, a link only when it is an absolute http(s) address. The host is asked once per address and viewer, however many records link it, and a throw is taken as `null`. Where an act may write the field, a "Change" control beside the link edits the address. What draws it is a chunk fetched with the first app-link drawn: a hosted page's first load is 509 bytes larger (557 327 bytes over the vendors document, 556 818 on main), 501 handed a compiled app, and the scene before it draws 1 647, all under their budgets; the embed's pages face carries 1 884 / 1 607 bytes more minified / gzipped, every face 7 001 / 3 726, each raised by a file of its own. A relation whose `to` is another app's kind, an expression that walks it and the history of the far value are left for 0.2. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-171.
  
  Compatibility: additive. The document format gains the field type `app-link`, and `graview-compiled@1` carries it; a build that predates it refuses the type by path, and a page that predates it refuses such a compiled app as `compiled-shape` and compiles the document. `mount`, `<Embed>`, `GraviewProvider` and the routed face's context gain an optional `resolveApp`; `ReadableField` gains `app`, `ScalarField` gains `app`, and a document card's resolved field block gains `app`. A derived tool's input schema for a declaration with no app-link field is unchanged. What a host does: read `capabilities().shipped` for FR-171 before writing an `app-link` field, and pass `resolveApp` to have one drawn as the app rather than as its address.
- df64e2b: A sight hides a field, not only a kind (Roadmap f-field-sight). A role saw a whole record or none of it, so a roster where parents see every player's name and not their phone number could not be said. A sight may now say `hide: ["phone", "medical"]`, or `fields: ["name"]` as an allow list, and sights add up: a field is shown when any sight that shows the seat the record shows it, so `{ roles: ["parent"], kinds: ["player"], own: true }` beside the hiding one gives each parent their own child whole. A field kept from a seat is judged in the seat view, so it never leaves the store for that seat: not on the record it is served (a required one withholds the record, as a hidden required reference does), not in the log — an op that adds or removes such a record, writes such a field or whose sentence read one is withheld, whatever the field held, its primitives served without it — nor in an act's answer, a conflict, a search hit, a name a call resolves, a form, an edit offered in place, a static page or a tool's answer. A computed field and a rule are worked out over what the seat is served, so a phone it may not see reads as none. The seat may not write such a field: an act that would is refused as one given an argument it does not take (`invalid`), the same whatever it held and whatever was written, and an import refuses a column that goes to one as it refuses a column that goes to no field. `graview check` names a field a sight misspells (`sight-names-an-unknown-field`), one a record cannot do without (`sight-hides-a-required-field`) and a label or description that reads a kept field (`sight-hides-a-labeled-field`, a warning). `add-sight` and `remove-sight` take the keys, a field the sight's kinds lack is refused (`sight-field`), and the studio carries, adds, changes and writes them back. Judging a field is `@graview/core/field-sight`, which `@graview/ship`'s server entry loads; a page that holds its whole store (`openStore` from `@graview/ship/browser`, or a `Store` of its own) imports it once, as does a host that serves seats without `@graview/ship`, and a page `openRemote` opened needs none, its host having judged what it sent. Without it, a sight with `hide` or `fields` shows nothing: the store keeps the record rather than serve a field it cannot judge. The new graview-sight skill teaches it; graview-permissions points there.
  
  Compatibility: the declaration gains two optional keys on a sight, `policy.sees[].hide` and `policy.sees[].fields` (field names), and the document schema with them; a declaration without them compiles and serves as before. Four finding codes are new (`sight-names-an-unknown-field`, `sight-hides-a-required-field`, `sight-hides-a-labeled-field`, a document edit's `sight-field`). Ops and the wire are unchanged in shape: a seat with a kept field is served fewer fields and more withheld ops, and an op's `described` may name `<id>#<field>` — a field its sentence read — beside the record ids it named; a build that predates it reads those as ids that name no record. `WIRE_PROTOCOL` does not move. The risk is an older build: a host or a page on a build before this one ignores `hide` and `fields` and SHOWS the field to the role it was hidden from — so a host serves a declaration that says either only on a build that has `@graview/core/field-sight` (`FIELD_SIGHT` on `@graview/core`), and refuses such a declaration otherwise; `capabilities().shipped` names no FR id for it. Stored data is unchanged. docs/stability.md says it under the seat's sight.
- 7bd102c: A skill for lists that stay usable as an app grows, `graview-lists`: what a kind's list offers to filter by (`pages.lists`, the chips and the Filter headings), cards read at a glance with the `excerpt` formatter, records set aside with archive, merge and "Bring it back" instead of deleted, and a place printed by `renderPlace` — each verified with `graview check` (`pages-filter-unknown`, `pages-kind-unknown`, `view-whole-text`) and `graview describe`. The pages skill was at its length, so the lists are a job of their own. `graview-agent-seat` says how a host passes what an agent's guess was based on (`lineage`), and that a link made less than sure stays provisional until a person keeps it. The skills test reads a view's finding codes where the view's validator says them.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged.
- 1076b0c: A spreadsheet comes in as one undoable batch (FR-177). `importRows(store, kind, rows, mapping, { by })` on the new entry `@graview/core/import` takes rows as arrays under a header row or as records keyed by header, and a mapping from columns to the kind's fields and relations — a relation the kind declares, or one declared toward it (`{ relation, direction: "in" }`). Each cell is read by its field's type: a date written as ISO, as `M/D/YYYY` (`dayFirst` for `D/M/YYYY`), as "Sep 10, 2023" or as a sheet's serial day; a number with its currency, unit, thousands separators, a parenthesized loss or a percent; a choice by its option or its words, case aside; a yes or a no as yes/no, true/false, x or ✓. A cell that names another kind's record links it by its label as acts take names — an exact name, or the one label it starts — under the importer's sight. Every row that fits lands as one batch, each record its own op ("Add a vendor: Bloom & Co") under the batch intent "Import 52 vendors", attributed to `by` and recorded `via: "import"`, and `store.undo(result.batch)` takes it all back. A row that does not fit is left out with every reason in words — "“soon” under Booked on is not a date", "“Smith” under Event could be Smith reunion or Smith wedding", "it has no Name, which every vendor needs" — and the rest still land. The importer is held to the acts that create the kind: a policy or an agent's `may` that refuses them refuses every row in its own words, and a host's `admit` is asked with the import as planned before anything is kept. `previewImport` (or `preview: true`) says per row what would be made, linked and refused, and the totals, and keeps nothing. `proposeMapping(app, kind, header)` proposes a mapping from the header row — fields by name, label or name spoken, relations by name or by the noun of the kind at the far end — with the columns nothing answers to and the required fields no column fills. `parseCsv(text)` reads a CSV or a paste: RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, the byte-order mark, and a tab or semicolon delimiter read off the first line. The import is a library function; no agent tool is derived for it. The graview-seed skill says when to reach for it.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `@graview/core/import` is a new entry, nothing on a hosted page imports it, and `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-177.
- 0b722e4: Days, times and numbers are said in the app's locale, en-US first (FR-178). The eighth walk read "10 Sep 2023" in an app written for the United States: every day was said day first, in an order fixed in code, and no face knew a locale. Now every day, time and figure a person reads is said through `Intl` — a record's facts and its glance, a template's `{due}`, `{due | date}` and a moment's `{at}`, a block's field and figure, a rule's line and a rule's message, a creating act's line, an arrangement's headings ("Week of Aug 24, 2026"), the calendar's titles and labels, a declared timeline's hours, and the seat's lines ("On Thursday, Sep 17: …") — in en-US unless the app says otherwise: "Sep 10, 2023", "3:30 PM", "1,250". A moment (`2023-09-10T15:30`) is said with its time where it was said as it is kept. The app says its locale where it says its currency, `brand.locale` ("en-GB" keeps the day first, "de-DE" writes "1.250"), and a host that knows its reader passes theirs as `locale` on the embed (both faces), `GraviewProvider` and a pages `PageContext`, which wins over the app's. `describeApp` says what the locale makes of a day, a time and a figure; `editDocument`'s `set-brand` and `diffDocuments` say "Days, times, numbers and money are written for de-DE." The framework's own words stay English. `@graview/core` exports `dayAsRead`, `momentAsRead`, `timeAsRead`, `numberAsRead`, `weekdayAsRead`, `dayOfMonthAsRead`, `monthAsRead`, `weekdayName`, `minutesAsRead`, `whenAsRead`, `localeFor`, `sayIn` and `DEFAULT_LOCALE`; each takes an optional locale. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-178.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and derived tool schemas are unchanged; `brand.locale` is the key it was, and now says days, times and numbers as well as money. The words a person reads change: a day is said "Aug 28, 2026" where it was said "28 Aug 2026", a moment with its time where it was said as stored, a number in a block or a rule's line with the locale's separators, a week's heading with its day in words, and the seat's day "Thursday, Sep 17" where it was "Thursday 17 Sep". `editDocument`'s sentence for `set-brand`'s `locale` and `diffDocuments`' for a changed locale say "Days, times, numbers and money are written for …"; a changed currency is its own sentence. What a host does: pass the reader's locale as `locale` on `mount`, and match words a test reads in en-US.
- 05bac56: Drawing in a worker view is a skill of its own, `graview-view-drawing`, and it says the images a view may show as they are. `graview-worker-view` had reached the 11,000 characters a skill is held under, and the last facts put in it were paid for by shaving prose that carried meaning; it still said an `img` draws only a `data:` image, when a view also draws the app's logo (a `blob:` URL of the page the host made, FR-127), the images the app holds by their content address (`/graview/assets/<sha256>.<ext>`, where the host passes `asset`), and the pictures on records once its manifest says `pictures` (FR-175). The new skill holds what the host keeps of a drawing, the chrome a view never speaks as, the app's tokens and `theme`, and those four kinds of image, with how a view draws a record's picture while its address is on its way and when it was left out; it ends, as every skill does, in `graview check`, a headless run and reading `refused`. `graview-worker-view` keeps the manifest, the global, links, writes, limits, registering and the headless check, points at the new skill for drawing, and has back the sentences it lost to the limit. `graview skills install` installs sixteen skills.
  
  Compatibility: unchanged on every surface: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document and compiled formats, check finding codes, and tool names and input schemas. Only the skills an assistant installs change: `graview-view-drawing` is new, and what `graview-worker-view` said of drawing moved into it.
- 93a7636: A text field may be written in Markdown, and every list says a text field by its start (FR-180). Graview Cloud's Roadmap keeps its briefs as Markdown in a text field, and the Briefs list drew each body whole with its syntax showing, and the record page too. A document says `{"type": "text", "format": "markdown"}` and a declaration `markdownField()`; the value is kept as written. The record's page — the routed face, the scene's record, the scaffolded page and a static page from `renderPlace` — draws it as prose: headings demoted under the page's own, paragraphs with their line breaks kept, lists (nested, and tasks as ☐ and ☑), emphasis, code and code blocks, quotations, rules and tables, and links only to `http:`, `https:` and `mailto:`, each `rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow"` in a new tab. No HTML passes through: a tag, a handler or a `javascript:` or `data:` link is its words, an entity its character, and a picture `![…](…)` is its words — a record's pictures are its image fields' (FR-162). The reader is a small one of the framework's own, `@graview/core/markdown` (`markdownBlocks`, `markdownHtml`, `safeHref`), linear in what it reads, fetched with the first Markdown a page draws. Editing it opens the text area, with a "Preview" that shows it as the record will; the form says "Markdown" beside the field.
  
  A row, a card, a describe line, a kind stop's table cell, a search hit and a seat's line about a record say a text field by its start (`excerptOf`, on `@graview/core/words`): its first paragraph, heading or list item, or about 160 characters cut at a word, with an ellipsis where words were left out and Markdown's syntax taken out — "## Heading **bold**" is "Heading bold". A template's `{body|excerpt}` takes out Markdown's commonest marks too, and a derived edit's history line says a long string by its start rather than whole. How many lines each draws is still the surface's clamp. Plain text fields keep their paragraphs and line breaks on the record's page, as before.
  
  The checker refuses `markdown` on any type but `text`, and a number's format on a `text`, as `field-format`. `retype-field` takes `"format": "markdown"` and drops it when left out, saying "brief's body is now written in Markdown" or "is drawn as written, not as Markdown"; `diffDocuments` says the change too. A Markdown field's tool argument carries `"contentMediaType": "text/markdown"` and "Written in Markdown." `graview types` notes it. The studio reads it as a text field that says Markdown (`markdown` on its field node, set by `add-field` and `retype-field`), writes it back as `markdownField()` and hands a document back with `"format": "markdown"` kept. `pnpm verify long-text` now also asks it of the Roadmap's real briefs, on a desk and a phone in both schemes: a Markdown body renders as prose with no syntax showing, a list row says a text field as one excerpt line with no Markdown syntax, and hostile Markdown runs nothing. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-180.
  
  A hosted page's first load is 171 bytes (0.2 KB) heavier, 114 gzipped: the mark on a compiled text field, the format's name, the template excerpt's marks and the history line's start. Nothing else is up front; the reader and the renderer are fetched with the record that draws one. Fetched all together, every face is 16_333 / 6_776 bytes more and the embed with the studio handed in 5_888 / 2_421, each raised by a file of its own (scripts/budget-raises/markdown-in-a-text-field-every-face.json, -studio.json).
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats and the wire are unchanged. The document format gains `"markdown"` among a field's `format` values within `graview-document@1`, refused by a build that predates it, and the checker a finding code, `field-format`. A derived tool's input schema for a Markdown field gains `contentMediaType` and the words "Written in Markdown."; for a declaration without one it is unchanged. A derived edit's intent, which the log keeps, says a string of more than 60 characters by its start. `excerptOf` and `isMarkdownField` are on `@graview/core/words`, `markdownField` on `@graview/core`, and the new entry `@graview/core/markdown`. What Graview Cloud does: offer `"format": "markdown"` on a text field when `capabilities().shipped` lists FR-180, and send `retype-field` with `{"type": "text", "format": "markdown"}` to turn it on.
- 0d585ba: Rules that read the clock are judged again when the day turns (FR-176). A rule was judged on every change and once on load, so "overdue" stayed unsaid in a room that stayed open past midnight. `store.tick(now)` judges the store's rules on `now`'s day (UTC, as `today()` reads the clock, or a day given as `YYYY-MM-DD`) and answers `{ day, was?, appeared, cleared }`: the problems the day turning made and mended. Every subscriber hears it with an empty diff and no ops, so the bar, the problems page and the counts read again; nothing is written or logged, and a store pinned to a day (`invariantOptions.today`) stays on it. The day a store judges on is handed to every rule as its `today` — a rule in the rule language reads it before its own clock, a rule that is a function reads it from its arguments and says `readsTheClock: true` — and a broken rule's line is said on it too. `@graview/ship`'s `openStore` (and so `serve`) and `openRemote` tick their store just after each midnight until `close()`; `openStore({ tick: false })` leaves it to a host that sleeps through timers, which calls `store.tick(now)` from its own alarm. `tickEachDay(store)`, `untilTheDayTurns()` and `dayOf(now)` are on `@graview/core`.
  
  `clockReaders(schema, rules)` on `@graview/core/check` says which rules and computed fields read the clock — `today()`, `now()`, a computed field or fact that does, a kind whose records retire on a date — and `graview check` notes them (`rules-read-the-clock`), saying what a host owes them. The `graview-invariant` skill says how a rule that is a function reads the day and how a host ticks.
  
  Compatibility: additive. `Store.tick`, the `today` an invariant is handed, `InvariantDefinition.readsTheClock` and `openStore`'s `tick` option are new; a new check finding code, `rules-read-the-clock`, is a note and never fails a check. A store whose `invariantOptions.today` is pinned now has its document rules judged on that day too, where they read the clock. `openStore` and `openRemote` hold an unref'd timer until `close()`. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-176.
- 8da8189: A hosted page's first load is 31 KB lighter, and a change that spends more of it has to say so (FR-179). Graview Cloud's shell measured 562 KB of its 570, the framework's hosted page 573.8 KB of 579.7, and every release had spent a little of it. A bundler places a whole file in a page's first chunk when the first chunk can reach it and any fetched chunk uses it, so a name a barrel the page imports re-exports rode up front as soon as a face, a form or the seat used it. Those names left the barrels for entries of their own: the matcher (`search`, `actsOn`, `touchWeights`, `parseQuery`, `matchNode`, `fold`, `squeeze`, `strengthOf`, `fragmentOf`, `searchableFields`, `describeSearched`) is `@graview/core/search`, handed to the provider by the scene and the Find box that search (`matchWith`, from `@graview/react/provider`); a record read in words and an argument's shape (`readableFields`, `pageSections`, `isLongText`, `describeNode`, `tellApart`, `argShape`, `describeArg`, `argumentWords`, `failureWords`, `viaSaid`) are `@graview/core/words`; an act's answer to its seat, every place an app has and the order things are made in (`answerSeenBy`, `placesOf`, `placesFrom`, `beginning`) are `@graview/core/seat`; judging the arrangement (`arrangementFindings`) is `@graview/core/check`'s; a card's sizes and a line's estimated width (`rosterRows`, `MARQUEE_WIDTH`, `estimateWidth` and the rest) are `@graview/layout/sizes`; and an address's arrangement is read apart from what a kind can be arranged by. A frame guest's host — `mountGuestView`, `createGuestHost`, `createGuestLimiter`, `readAcross`, `readTheme` and the protocol's constants — is `@graview/guest/host/frame`, and `guestView` fetches it as the view is first drawn, the frame's height held meanwhile, asked for again when it did not arrive. The studio's door, the seat's panel and the lenses were already fetched with the face that draws them; a test now holds each of them, the frame guest's host and the matcher off the first load.
  
  `pnpm verify hosted` measures a hosted page at 556 818 bytes up front (543.8 KB, 187.4 KB gzipped), where main measured 589 206 (575.4 KB, 199.9 KB gzipped): 31.6 KB less, 12.5 KB gzipped. Handed a compiled app (FR-123) it is 515 126 bytes (503.1 KB, 173.1 KB gzipped), where main measured 547 516 (534.7 KB, 185.6 KB gzipped): 31.6 KB less. Before a face draws, with what it fetches as it is drawn, the scene is 876.3 KB and the pages 806.2 KB, each smaller than before. The budgets fall to 554.0 KB (191.5 KB gzipped) and 513.5 KB handed a compiled app (177.5 KB gzipped), which keeps 10 KB of room for the five features landing beside this one (FR-171, FR-172, FR-173, FR-176, FR-178, about 8 KB together), and a base is never edited upward again: a change that spends more files a raise in `scripts/budget-raises/` naming `"a hosted page"` or `"a hosted page handed a compiled app"`, the bytes it adds and the changeset that ships it, and that changeset says the kilobytes it adds up front ("1.2 KB"), or the test and the pull request's changeset check fail. `pnpm verify hosted` says the gzipped weight beside the minified, and docs/hosted-page.md carries both. Fetched all together, every face's chunks are 4 063 / 3 665 bytes more for the chunks the moved files split into, the studio handed in 1 816 / 2 926, the pages face alone 411 more gzipped (2 637 fewer minified), the guest host before a worker 282 / 288 and registering a worker view 223 / 639, each raised by a file of its own. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-179.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the wire, the document and compiled formats, check codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Breaking for an import: the names above are no longer exported from `@graview/core`, `@graview/layout/view` or `@graview/guest/host`, and are imported from the entries named (their types — `ReadableField`, `FieldSection`, `ArgShape`, `AppPlace`, `Beginning`, `Hit`, `SearchResult` and the rest — stay where they were). What Graview Cloud does: import `placesOf` from `@graview/core/seat`, and `mountGuestView`, `createGuestHost` or `readTheme` from `@graview/guest/host/frame`, if it uses them; its shell's `guestView` import is unchanged and now carries the frame's host only when a guest view is drawn.
- 2ca4722: From altitude the map draws only the lines that explain what is chosen, and a picture pressed on a district opens beside the map at a size a person reads.
  
  At rest every relation between two districts is one ribbon (`Ribbons`), run along the gutters between the blocks so it crosses no third district, as wide as the links along it, and said in words when pointed at: "146 features → 14 areas, the part it lives in", from the declaring kind's own description. No record has a line of its own. A chosen district keeps only its own relations' ribbons. A chosen record keeps none and draws its own links instead (`RecordLinks`): solid to a record the map draws on its own (its building, or its chip), ending in a dot, and dashed into a district for the records in it the map does not draw one by one, or for a relation that reaches more than six of them; each is labeled at its far end in its kind's words ("the part it lives in: tools", "what this part does: 46 features"), passes under the districts' names, and everything the record does not reach recedes. A link made less than sure (FR-172) stays so in the line the chosen record draws: dotted at the strength the scene draws a provisional link in, its label ending "· unsure" with what it was based on on hover. The connectors and the ties no longer draw at altitude, except for a change that just happened. The Key says the three manners first, and the unsure one at every height, and each relation's swatch is its ribbon at its own width. The roads are gone; `roadBetween` with them, and `streetPoints` moved to the ribbons.
  
  What is chosen from altitude stands in a panel beside the map (`MapPanel`, from `@graview/primitives` and `@graview/primitives/scene`, drawn by the Shell and an embed's scene face): docked at the side on a desk — the city is laid out in the room left, and Up, the Key and the ask field keep clear of it — and a sheet from the foot on a phone. A showing pressed on a district's marquee opens there at full size, in the mode a lens draws a page in, with its name, its count, the kind's description and Open to its own place; the map recedes but for its district, and no line is drawn over it. The billboard that drew the lens at the city's scale on its plot is gone, and the camera no longer flies closer to it. A chosen record lists its links there, a heading per relation in its kind's words, each record a press that chooses it; a chosen district lists its relations with how many each joins, and every record in it; a right-click on a record there, or on one in the lens, opens its acts as it does on the map — the card of chips that floated over the districts ("1 of 14 tied", "close", "+10 more — double-click to go in") is no longer drawn where the panel is. A district's "open", and a double-click on a district with no picture of its own, choose the district where a face says the panel is drawn (`BesideTheMap`, from `@graview/react`); a scene drawn without it, such as the studio's, still opens a district in place. The panel, the ribbons and a record's links are each fetched when first drawn, so a hosted page's first load and its scene before it draws hold to their budgets unraised; the embed's budgets are raised for them (scripts/budget-raises/the-map-draws-only-what-explains-the-choice-*.json): every face by 22_727 / 10_165 bytes, the pages face by 372 / 166 for the district's card asking whether the panel lists it, and the embed with the studio by 609 / 947.
  
  Compatibility: the scene changes what it draws, not any surface a host holds it to. `@graview/react` gains `Ribbons`' and `RecordLinks`' helpers (`mapChoice`, `districtOf`, `ribbonsOf`, `ribbonSentence`, `ribbonWidth`, `RIBBON_WIDTH`, `pickedFrom`) and `BesideTheMap`; `@graview/primitives` gains `MapPanel` and `mapPanelShows`. A selector on `.graview-road`, `[data-graview-screen]` or `.graview-screen-grip` finds nothing now: a relation from altitude is `[data-graview-ribbon]`, a record's link `[data-graview-link]`, and the panel `[data-graview-map-panel]`. `whereIs("screen:<kind>")` answers the district's box, where those looking at the picture now gather.
- 2450f9e: The skills say what the review before 0.1.29 changed: graview-lists that a list's boxes are one Tab stop with the arrows between them and Space to choose, and graview-sight that only the store `openRemote` opened is trusted to show what its host judged — a whole store on the same page still imports `@graview/core/field-sight` — and that an agent's tool for an act lists no argument writing a field the seat never sees.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.
- ac824fa: The skills say what the 0.1.29 wave's follow-ups added: graview-lists that a document says a kind is never archived with `"archives": false` (the edit `set-archives`), graview-seed that a sheet imported again passes `match` so a row changes the record it finds through its edit act instead of making a second, and graview-agent-seat that `keep-link` is permitted per relation.
  
  Compatibility: no surface changes.

## 0.1.28

### Patch Changes

- 085670c: Named edits for the people kind, grants and sights (FR-170). `add-kind` and `add-act` passed `people: true` and `$me` through, but nothing set `people` on a kind already there and no edit touched the policy, so an own-end grant or a sight via a relation could only be made by a raw JSON patch, which a builder's form or a chat's `propose_change` cannot offer as a named change previewed in words. `editDocument` takes five more ops. `set-people` (`{ "kind": "participant", "people": true }`) says which kind is the people who sign in, and refuses a second one with a sentence as `people-ambiguous`, so a move is `false` on the one, then `true` on the other, in one change. `add-grant` and `add-sight` take a grant or a sight as the policy holds one (`roles`, `mutations` and `kinds`, `self`, `ownEnd`, `describe`; `roles`, `kinds` and `own`, `via`): a role the document's `roles` does not list joins it, a first grant says that from now on only what a grant gives is allowed, and a first sight on an app with no policy comes with a grant to everyone of every act, since a sight alone would take every act away. `remove-grant` and `remove-sight` name the one to remove by what it says, never by its place in the list: a grant by its roles and acts, a sight by its roles and kinds, each in any order, any other key given matching too; two that match are refused, asking for the key that tells them apart, and one that matches nothing is refused with the ones there are, in words. Each says what it did in the declaration's words — "A participant may "Change the participant" on participants, only on their own record", "A participant sees matches, only those "givenBy" joins to their own participant" — and `diffDocuments` previews the change in the same sentences, where a policy changed was "Who may do what changes.". What the change leaves the people and the policy saying together is judged once it is whole, by the checker's own codes at its last such edit: `people-unsaid`, `sight-via-unknown-edge`, `sight-via-misses-people` and `grant-own-end-without-link`. The Secret Santa's and the idea board's people, grants and sights are each made from nothing by these edits alone, equal to their fixtures. The studio says a grant, a sight and the people kind it changed as these ops, where a grant or a sight was a `studio-unsaid` finding and a kind made the people was not said at all. And a creating act's line leaves out a sentence held in a string, as it leaves out a long text: "Add a feature: Named edits for the people kind, grants and sights (framework, building, 0.1.28)" where the feature's `layerWhy` stood in the parentheses; a value said in over 40 characters, or words ending in a stop, is not said again. A hosted page's first load is 103 bytes smaller (587 155 bytes over the vendors document, 587 258 on main), and 104 smaller handed a compiled app: the new line is paid for by saying a list in words in one place (`listed`). The studio and every face carry about 9 kB more minified, the edits and their sentences. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-170.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-170. The edit vocabulary (`EDIT_OPS`) gains `set-people`, `add-grant`, `remove-grant`, `add-sight` and `remove-sight`, and an edit's refusal may now carry `people-ambiguous`, `people-unsaid`, `sight-via-unknown-edge`, `sight-via-misses-people`, `grant-own-end-without-link`, `grant-act` or `sight-kind` as its code where every refusal was `edit`. `DocumentDiff`'s sentences say a grant, a sight, a role and the people kind in words where a change to the policy was "Who may do what changes." (still said of one they do not cover). `documentEdits` in the studio returns these ops where it returned a `studio-unsaid` finding for a grant or a sight. The line a creating document act is logged with leaves out a value said in over 40 characters or as words ending in a stop. What a host does: a builder's form or a chat offers the five ops by name, and reads `capabilities().shipped` for FR-170 before it sends them to a build that may predate them.

## 0.1.27

### Patch Changes

- af180f4: A seat's read is bounded, and a read it already made is not sent again (FR-167). One turn in an app of 30 components, 28 skills and 84 strengths ran seven steps whose input grew from 5.6k to 91k tokens: `get_graph` answered the whole graph, about 28,000 tokens, the model asked for it three more times, and every step resent all of it, $0.60 for one question. Now no read answers more than `READ_CHARS` (8,000) characters of JSON, for a seat's turn and an MCP client alike, since both call the same runtime. `get_graph` answers a page of at most `READ_PAGE` (25) records with the edges out of them, `total`, and `more`: "and 117 more nodes: call get_graph with offset 25 for the next page." `get_node` answers a record's fields and its links as `{ kind, id, label }`, `READ_LINKS` (20) each way a page, never the linked records' own fields, with "and 24 more links in: call get_node with id "component-1" and offset 20 for the next page." `search_graph` answers at most `READ_HITS` (20) hits whatever `limit` asked, without every match's id, and "and 65 more records match: narrow the words, or add kind:<kind>." `get_violations` answers `READ_PROBLEMS` (20) a page. A text longer than `READ_TEXT` (600) characters is cut and says "… (cut at 600 of 5000 characters)", and any other read too long to fit, a place described in full, answers `{ cut, partial }`, the cut saying how long it was and to ask for less. Within one turn of a host's model, a call with the same name and input as an earlier one, in any order of its keys, is answered `{ already: "Already read: see the answer to call 1 of this turn, which is above." }` and counts toward the turn's twelve calls, so a model asking the same thing over and over is stopped as before. The bounds are exported from `@graview/tools` as named constants. The reads are a chunk of their own, fetched with the first read: a page whose seat is never asked carries none of them, the scene face before it draws is 742 bytes smaller and a hosted page's first load is unchanged. `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-167.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and check finding codes are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-167. Tool names are unchanged; the read tools' answers, titles, descriptions and three input schemas moved, so a surface's hash moves with them. `get_graph` takes `offset`, is titled "Read the graph", and answers `{ nodes, edges, total, more? }` a page at a time where it answered every node and edge. `get_node` takes `offset` and answers `out` and `in` as `{ kind, id, label }` links where it answered whole edges, with `more` when there are more links or problems. `get_violations` takes `offset` and answers `{ violations, total, more? }` where it answered an array. `search_graph` answers at most 20 hits, with `more`, and no longer `matched`. What a host or an agent does: read `get_violations`' `violations`, follow `more` with `offset`, and read a link's `id` where it read an edge's `to` or `from`.
- 32d0c0d: A worker view's delegated event is taught by the element it matched, and a DOM method the worker lacks says so. `graview.on(type, selector, listener)` always handed `event.element`, the element the selector matched at or above what was pressed, but nothing said it, and `event.target.closest(…)` failed with "closest is not a function" because Remote DOM's polyfill has none. Now `closest`, `matches` and `dataset` on a view's element throw "A view's element has no closest: graview.on hands event.element, the element its selector matched; read event.element.getAttribute("data-…")." The worker-view skill and the guest README show a delegated handler reading `event.element`'s `data-*`.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. What a view does: read `event.element.getAttribute("data-…")` rather than walking up from `event.target`.
- 32d0c0d: A worker view's link or `graview.navigate` that goes nowhere says why. A bare string was taken as a record's id and nothing else, so `graview.navigate("projections")` went nowhere without a word, and Graview Cloud's pricing app lost its bottom links that way. Now a bare string is a record the viewer may see first, then a place by that slug, so `graview.navigate("the-packages")` goes to the place. One that is neither, from code, `<a data-record>` or `<a data-place>`, says so in the view's console and to the host's new `onProblem`, on `mountWorkerView` and on a registered view's definition: '"projections" is no record this viewer may see and is no place of this app: it goes nowhere. Its places: "the-packages".' A record the viewer may not see is said the same way as one that is not there. A headless run's problems name the places too, and take a bare place slug as the host does. The worker-view skill says what a destination may be.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. The guest protocol gains one message from host to view, `{ type: "notice", text }`, which a runtime from before it ignores; `GUEST_PROTOCOL` is unchanged. What a host does: pass `onProblem` to hear a worker view's links that go nowhere.

## 0.1.26

## 0.1.25

## 0.1.24

### Patch Changes

- 9fe145e: A host's turn knows the app the seat is mounted with, so `describe_place` describes a lens place. Inside `HostAi.turn` the seat built its read tools from the store alone, and the chat's own runtime did the same, so a model asking `describe_place` for a lens or the declared home was told "is not a place this app has": the app never reached the seat. `GraviewProvider` now takes `app` and holds it on its context (`useGraview().app`), inheriting the app of a provider it is drawn inside; `mount` and `<Embed>` pass theirs on both faces, and `PagesApp`'s `context.app` reaches the provider under the pages. The chat hands it to its own tool runtime, to the workbench's agent seat and, as `ChatContext.app`, to a host's turn, whose `describe_place` now answers exactly as the runtime `graview mcp` serves does, for the seat that asked. With `app` given, a provider's reader `settings` default to `app.settings`. `graview create` writes `app` on the provider and on the pages' context. The hosted page carries 132 bytes fewer up front (589 625; 589 757 before), and as many fewer handed a compiled app (547 324; 547 456): the embed now hands the provider each option as given, which the provider's props accept as `undefined`, rather than spreading each one in only when it is set.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `app` on `GraviewProviderProps`, `GraviewContextValue`, `PageContext` and `ChatContext`. Changed: `GraviewProviderProps`' `principal`, `seats`, `people`, `memory`, `presenceTtlMs`, `presence`, `brand`, `onKeepLens` and `ai` also accept `undefined`, which means the same as leaving them out. What a host does: one that mounts its own `GraviewProvider` passes `app={yourApp}` (and `app` in `PagesApp`'s context) so its seat and its turn can describe the app's lenses and home; `mount` and `<Embed>` need nothing.
- 108721d: A rule that reads a fact is judged, and worded, over what the seat sees. A document's fact (`fact('proposed')`) counts the whole graph, and a rule that read one was judged over the whole graph and its sentence said there, so a guest who sees one proposal was told "3 proposed, and at most two may be". A seat's problems are now judged over its sighted view, its lines and its facts with it, wherever it reads them: `seenBy(principal).violations()`, and so a seat's tools (`get_violations`, `get_node`), the store's reading for a served seat and a page's seat, which now agree with a page served only what its seat sees. A record the seat may not see never makes, mends or words one of its problems. An act's answer to a seat (`answerSeenBy`) gives what the act broke, and what stands after it, as the seat judges them once it is made; what it mended, and what a preview would do, were judged over the whole graph, and a rule there that reads beyond its subject (a fact, a sweep, a relation) is said by its title. `store.violations(context, over)` judges over another reading of the store's graph. `graview check` warns of a rule whose message says a fact over a kind some role told of the rule may not see in full (`rule-says-hidden-fact`), and the `graview-permissions` skill says what it means. The hosted page loads 30 bytes less up front (589,727), the compiled entry 36 less (547,420); the every-face budget is raised for chunks fetched later only.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Check finding codes: additive, `rule-says-hidden-fact` is a new warning, so a declaration that passed still passes. What a seat is told changes: where a policy keeps records from a seat, its problems are those its own view breaks, so a rule broken only by records it may not see is no longer reported to it, and one its view breaks that the records it may not see keep is; a preview's problems from a rule that reads beyond its subject are said by the rule's title. A store with no sights, and the system's seat, read exactly what they read before.
- 791fb0c: Each blocks lens's blocks are claimed under its own address. Every lens drawn from blocks answered to one shared view name, `place`, for `BlockSlots` claims, so two lenses whose blocks said one key could not be claimed differently: a face's claim on one was a claim on both. A blocks lens's view is now its address, as `describe_place` names it — the lens titled "The offers" is claimed as `{ "the-offers": { top: Banner } }` — and two lenses that say one key each draw their own. `SpecPlace` takes the `view` its blocks are claimed under; unsaid, it is the slot, so the home is still `home`.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the declaration, the compiled format and tool names and schemas are unchanged. A claim keyed `place` no longer reaches any lens; key it by the lens's address. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing.

## 0.1.23

### Patch Changes

- 8f7e998: A host wires its turn from the loop alone: `@graview/tools/turn` (FR-161). `turnThroughMessages` was exported only from the root of `@graview/tools`, and a host that imported it there, as the notes for FR-160 said to, carried the root's agent, providers and the rest in its first paint: Graview Cloud's shell went from 562 KB to 646 KB for forty bytes of wiring. The new entry `@graview/tools/turn` carries `turnThroughMessages`, `TURN_STEPS` and the `MessagesBlock`, `MessagesStepRequest` and `MessagesStepReply` types and nothing else; bundled on its own it reads one module and weighs under 2 KB. The root still exports them. The scaffolder's link-mode aliases and the example apps' name the new entry, and the seat skill says where to import it from.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged; `capabilities().shipped` gains FR-161. New: the `@graview/tools/turn` entry. What a host does: import `turnThroughMessages` from `@graview/tools/turn`; a host that imported it from `@graview/tools` on the first ask, to keep it out of the first paint, can import it up front instead.

## 0.1.22

### Patch Changes

- f25b52c: A document and a snapshot can be looked at with no product. `graview serve` was the store and drew nothing, so a stranger holding a JSON document and a seed had to build a product to see them — n-dx built one only to look at its projection, and proved every change with Playwright screenshots it scripted by hand. `graview preview <document.json> --seed <snapshot.json>` serves the same store and, beside it, Graview's own faces over it: the scene and Pages, with the bar and the address bar, so a place can be linked and reloaded. Nothing is built or installed at run time: the page is bundled once at the framework's build, as Graview Cloud's shell is, into the `graview` package's `dist/preview/` (71 files, 1.2 MB unpacked, about 430 KB of the tarball), so it rides with the tool a person runs and never with a product's dependencies. The store is in memory unless `--data` or `--sqlite` say where to keep it, and the page sits as a person holding every role the document declares unless `--as` or `--roles` say who. `graview shots <document.json> [--seed] [--places home,overview,people] [--out shots] [--scheme light|dark|both] [--width 1280,390] [--faces pages,scene]` photographs places headless to `<place>-<scheme>-<width>.png`: each place by its address word or its title, or a path such as `/people/p1`, every place when none is named; a place the app has not got is refused with the ones it has. Playwright is a peer the person installs, found beside the project first; without it the command says how to install it and the browser. `servePreview` and `previewPrincipal` are exported from `@graview/ship`, and `serveStore` takes an optional `answer(request)` that is asked before the store for every request but the live wire. `pnpm verify preview` runs the built command on Graview Cloud's org document and asks a browser for both faces, a change on the served store reaching the open page, and the shots. The ship and new-app skills say when to use them.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages and routes, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool schemas are unchanged. Added: `serveStore({ answer })`, optional; a host that passes none serves exactly as before. The hosted page loads nothing more: no module it imports changed.
- 9154faf: A document can declare facts of the whole graph. `computed` was a kind's only, so n-dx decided "the whole product map is proposed" in its own face with `every(n => n.proposed)`, and the scene, the pages and an agent could not say the same thing. A document's top-level `computed` is a name and a rule-language expression with no record as its subject — `"mapProposed": { "expr": "every(all('area') | all('capability'), proposed == true)", "label": "The map is proposed" }` — read anywhere the language is as `fact('mapProposed')`: a block on the home or a lens, a template, a rule over the graph, a kind's computed field, another fact. It is worked out over the reader's own graph, so what a seat may not see adds nothing to it, from the budget of the expression that reads it, and a fact that cannot be worked out is said where it is read, never thrown. `factValues(schema, graph)` and `factNames(schema)` on `@graview/core/blocks` work them out for a face of a product's own; `describePlace` (and so `describe_place` on the home, over MCP) lists each with its value for the seat; `graview describe` lists each with its expression; a compiled app carries them, `resolveFor` and `blockContextFor` read them as every face does, and the document's JSON Schema and `defineDocument` take them; a TypeScript app says them as `createSchema(kinds, computed)`. `graview check` judges a fact as a computed field — a bare name (no record is there to read it from), an expression that does not parse, a kind or relation nobody declares, work that grows with the square of the graph (a warning) or its cube (refused) — and refuses `fact('<name>')` naming no fact wherever a document reads it (`fact-unknown`). A diff says a fact added, changed or taken away; renaming a kind, a field or a relation renames it in a fact, and removing one removes the facts that read it.
  
  To pay for it on a page, a page that compiles a document its host has already judged (`compileDocumentWithoutCheck`) no longer judges its kinds' computed fields: `compileDocument` and `graview check` do, as they judge an act's ranges and a brand. The hosted page loads 589 821 bytes up front (593 247 before, budget 593 613), 547 520 handed a compiled app (547 742 before, budget 548 045), and the scene 901 241 before it draws (904 615 before), with no hosted-page budget raised. The every-face and studio-handed embed budgets are raised by the checker's growth, and the pages face alone by 145 bytes gzipped for `fact()` and the roles it draws with, as `scripts/lib/bundle-budget.mjs` notes.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: the document's top-level `computed`, the `fact()` function of the rule language, `Schema.computed` and `createSchema`'s second argument, `factValues` and `factNames` on `@graview/core/blocks`, `GRAPH_FACTS` on `@graview/core/document`, `PlaceDescription.facts`, the finding code `fact-unknown`. Changed: `graview check` now refuses facts that read each other in a circle (`computed-cycle` at `computed.<name>`); `computedValues` takes `null` for the whole graph; `compileDocumentWithoutCheck` builds an app whose kind's computed field `compileDocument` would refuse (a page refuses that value where it reads it), so a host that compiles strangers' documents in the page without the checker judges them with `compileDocument` first, as it already must for everything else the checker says.
- 9154faf: A kind can say which field is its natural time and which its author. `fieldRoles` was not a document key at 0.1.20, so n-dx could not declare a commit's `committedAt` its time nor its `author` who made it: commits had no declared order, and the Runs timeline bound its date in the lens. A document's kind says `fieldRoles` as `defineNode` does — `"fieldRoles": { "when": "committedAt", "who": "author" }`, and any role a lens binds by name — and the framework reads two of them. `when`: a list of the kind opens newest first by it at any length, indexed by year when it is large (it was a large list only, by a `start` role, which a kind that wants it now says as `when`); a timeline or calendar that binds no `start` for the kind draws each record there (`withNaturalTime`, exported for a lens of an app's own). `who` and `when`: an unchosen glance leads with them, before the fields as declared. `graview check` refuses a role naming no field (`field-role-missing-field`, now at `kinds.<kind>.fieldRoles.<role>` for a document) and a `when` that is no date or a `who` that is no name (`field-role-ill-typed`); a lens that binds no start passes where the kind says `when`. `graview describe` lists each kind's roles; a diff says a role that moves; renaming a field moves its role, and removing one lets it go; `toDocument` carries a kind's roles; the document's JSON Schema takes them, and `defineDocument` holds each to a field the kind has.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the compiled format, tool names and schemas are unchanged. New: `kinds.<kind>.fieldRoles` in the document, the `when` and `who` roles, the finding code `field-role-ill-typed`, `withNaturalTime` on `@graview/primitives`. Changed: a large list no longer opens newest first by a kind's `start` role — say `when` (Discography's albums do); a list of a kind with `when` opens newest first at any length; an unchosen glance leads with `who` and `when`.
- 0e1bafe: A snapshot says when it was made and what it holds, and can be read a kind at a time. A snapshot file was one JSON object, `{ nodes, edges }`: to learn how old a projection was, or whether it held any tasks, a reader parsed every record, and n-dx's face loaded a 3.3 MB snapshot whole into a memory adapter on every page load. `@graview/core/snapshot` writes and reads one format for every snapshot file: `writeSnapshot(graph, { generatedAt })` puts a header line first — `"format": 2`, `generatedAt`, records per kind, links per kind and where each kind's lines are, by line and by byte — then one record per line, each kind's together, then the links. `readSnapshotHeader(text)` reads the first line alone; `readSnapshot(text, { kinds })` parses only those kinds' lines and the links among them; `fetchSnapshot(url, { kinds })` fetches the header and then only those kinds' bytes by range, reading the whole answer from a server that serves no ranges. An old `{ nodes, edges }` file reads everywhere as format 1, and a newer format is refused by its number. `graview serve --seed`, `graview sync-seed --seed` and `graview describe --place --seed` read either.
  
  Compatibility: ops, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged, and so is what an adapter stores. New: the snapshot file's line format (`SNAPSHOT_FORMAT`, 2), on the stored-formats surface, and `@graview/core/snapshot` (`writeSnapshot`, `readSnapshot`, `readSnapshotHeader`, `fetchSnapshot` and their types). A seed is read as either format. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing.
- 9154faf: Each kind words its own end of a relation several kinds declare. An edge's name was one relation to every surface, so a record listed everything that reached it by `by` under the first declaring kind's words, and `graview check` refused two kinds declaring one name in two sets of words (`edge-name-shared`). n-dx's `under` sits on six kinds: a change wants "the tasks inside it", a zone "the zones inside it", and they could not both be said. Now a record's page, a record's connections in the scene, and the cards around a focus list what reaches a record by each reading: kinds that word a relation alike together, kinds that word it their own way apart, each under its own words (an artist's page says "Their songs" and "Their releases"), and a band of a kind is captioned in that kind's own words. So the refusal is gone: a kind may word its own end of a shared relation.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, tool names and schemas are unchanged. Changed: the finding code `edge-name-shared` is no longer reported; a document or app it refused now compiles. A record's relation groups (`recordFacts(...).links`, the scene's connections) are one per relation, direction and reading, where they were one per relation and direction.
- f25b52c: `graview doctor` says whether a product is on one Graview release. One release is named five times in a product — the `@graview/*` pins, `graview` beside them, the lockfile, what is installed, and each document's `formatVersion` — and React's pair makes a sixth: n-dx's face met `react` 19.2.8 beside `react-dom` 19.3.0 as a blank page, since React 19.3 refuses a mismatched pair at startup. `graview doctor [<dir>] [--document <file>] [--json]` reads the product's package.json, the nearest lockfile (pnpm, npm, yarn or bun), its node_modules and every `.json` document in it that says it is a Graview declaration, and says each problem as a sentence with its fix: two releases among the pins ("pin them all to 0.1.20"), a pin the install or the lockfile does not follow, a lockfile holding a second copy, a document of a format the installed core does not read, and the React pair to pin. A pin that is a range, a package named by a path (a checkout, a workspace or a tarball), a document of an older format and a `graview` run at another release than the one pinned are said as notes. It exits 1 on any problem. `diagnose` is exported from `@graview/core/cli` for a host that wants the findings as data. The getting-started page and the `graview` README say the npx trap: inside a pnpm workspace that already holds a release in its hidden hoist, `npx -y graview@<version>` skips the install and finds no bin (`sh: graview: command not found`) — run the project's own `node_modules/.bin/graview`.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool schemas are unchanged. Added: a `doctor` command and `diagnose`, `doctor` and `DOCTOR_USAGE` on `@graview/core/cli`.
- 0e1bafe: The sync engine decides a conflict as it is told, refuses what the remote owns, and is its own entry. A product that projects another system into Graview and syncs back (n-dx's face, over rex) found the engine pushed our value for a field in the same pass it reported that field as a conflict, so the remote was overwritten before anyone could choose; it pre-empted every run by hand. `SyncEngine` now takes `conflicts: "report" | "theirs" | "ours"`. `report`, the default, holds a field both sides changed and sends neither value until `resolve` says which stands, and a later pass that hears nothing new still reports it; `theirs` lands the remote's value through `applyInbound`, authored by the system like any inbound change; `ours` sends ours in the same pass. The report's new `resolved` says each conflict the policy settled and which side it took, and `conflicts` is every one still held.
  
  A mapping's resource may be `readonly`, with a `because` sentence: inbound changes land, nothing is sent, and once `sync.guard()` holds the store, an act that would write a record of it is refused with that sentence as a `PermissionDeniedError` (`sync.admit` is the same judgment as an `ApplyOptions.admit`; `sync.refuses(kind)` says it before an act is offered). A resource's `version` names our field that holds the remote's version, and every record that carries one is linked at it when the engine is made, so a projected graph's first run sends nothing. A local delete the remote refuses, or of a record the remote owns, is refused once with its reason and the link let go, where it was refused again on every pass. A field the mapping only sends (`ours`) is agreed after a push, where it was sent again every pass.
  
  A remote that speaks MCP is `mcpRemote({ name, call, pull, push })`, one write at a time, each refused alone. `call` is `mcpClient({ url, allow, headers })`, which opens a streamable-HTTP session, reads a JSON or event-stream answer, opens another session once when the server forgot it, and refuses a tool not in `allow` before sending it; or, in a page, `doorClient()`, posting to `mcpDoor({ call, allow })` on the server, which holds the token and passes only a POST of `application/json` from its own origin (`sameOrigin`) naming an allowed tool (`allowTools`), saying which it refused. None imports a `node:` builtin.
  
  A store attributes what its reader does: `new Store({ principal })`, or `openStore({ storeOptions: { principal } })`, is the author of every apply and undo that names none, where it was always a person with no name. `rememberedPrincipal()` on `@graview/ship` and `@graview/ship/browser` reads the name a page keeps under `graview:principal` (`PRINCIPAL_KEY`), "reader" when it keeps none or storage is locked away. The hosted page measures 593_548 bytes up front, 59 fewer than before: the store's constructor builds its graph from one `validate` it already held.
  
  Compatibility: ops, stored formats, wire messages, the document format, the compiled format, check finding codes and tool names and schemas are unchanged. Changed: the sync engine, its mappings, its conflict invariant and the Google Calendar transport left `@graview/core` for `@graview/core/sync` (`SyncEngine`, `googleCalendar`, `googleCalendarMapping`, `syncConflictInvariant`, `SYNC_CONFLICTS`, `EMPTY_SYNC_STATE`, `systemAuthor` and their types); a conflict is held and not sent unless `conflicts` says otherwise; `SyncReport.conflicts` is every conflict held, and `SyncState` may carry `conflicts`; `resolve(conflict, "ours")` re-bases on their value. New: `conflicts`, `resolved`, `guard`, `admit` and `refuses` on the engine; `readonly`, `because`, `version` and `remoteId` on a `ResourceMapping`; `mcpClient`, `mcpDoor`, `doorClient`, `mcpRemote`, `allowTools` and `sameOrigin` on `@graview/core/sync`; `principal` on `StoreOptions`; `rememberedPrincipal` and `PRINCIPAL_KEY` on `@graview/ship`. What Graview Cloud changes: nothing.
- 6384676: What the review after 0.1.21 found, each fixed with a test that failed first.
  
  A seat is read only what it sees, whatever it is handed. `resolveFor(store, principal, node)` resolved the record it was given as it was given, so a node read from the whole graph put its fields on a page for a seat that may not see it; it now reads the record through `store.seenBy(principal)`, and one the seat may not see resolves to nothing. `blockContextFor` with a principal and a bare `{ graph, schema }` says that a seat is read from a store, where it threw a `TypeError` about `seenBy`.
  
  The doors answer only this machine's own pages. `sameOrigin` (`@graview/core/sync`) is now the one rule every door keeps — `mcpDoor` and the dev server's model, decision and studio doors alike (`@graview/ship/dev` re-exports it) — and it also refuses a Host that is not this machine (`ownHost`: `localhost`, a `*.localhost` name or an IP address, or one of the `hosts` a door is told), so a page that rebinds its own name to this machine can no longer spend the dev server's model key or call a sync's tools. `mcpDoor` takes only the media type `application/json` (not a type that merely mentions it, which a page on another site may send unasked), refuses a body past `limit` (1 MB) with a 413, and decodes it as UTF-8 across chunks.
  
  `graview preview` keeps to its own page. It believes the seat its page names, so `serveStore`'s new `ownOrigin` refuses every request and live socket not from the preview's own origin on this machine; `servePreview` on a host other machines reach refuses unless `trustSeatHeaders` says the network is trusted (the CLI's `--trust-seat-headers`); `graview preview` takes a free port unless `--port` says one, where it took `graview serve`'s 5196; and a seed that does not read is said as `graview preview:` or `graview shots:` and a sentence, not a stack. `compileDocumentFiles({ seed })` and `graview view check --seed` read a seed in the snapshot's line format as well as an old `{ nodes, edges }` file.
  
  The sync engine keeps to its policy at the edges.
